- [sceOpusCeltDecCreateEx, sceOpusCeltDecDecode, sceOpusCeltDecDestroy, sceOpusCeltDecGetSize, sceOpusCeltDecInitialize, sceOpusCeltDecTerminate](../../core/libs/prx/libSceOpusCeltDec/Export.cpp) (libSceOpusCeltDec) - imported by PPSA99001, which fails to load without the module; added without an implementation. Their signatures match libSceOpusDec's in [ProsperoRadio](https://github.com/blackbearreloaded/ProsperoRadio/blob/2c4029c3a8f43e9e1dfef98374d94d99b14d745d/src/opus_decoder.cpp). PPSA99001 opens this decoder only as a fallback when libSceOpusDec reports a retryable error for a CELT packet, which [libSceOpusDec](../../core/libs/prx/libSceOpusDec/Export.cpp) here never returns, so the fallback is not reached
- [sceContentDeleteById](../../core/libs/prx/libSceContentDelete/Export.cpp) (libSceContentDelete) and [sceContentSearchGetContentLastUpdateId, sceContentSearchOpenMetadataByContentId, sceContentSearchGetTotalContentSize, sceContentSearchGetNumOfContent](../../core/libs/prx/libSceContentSearch/Export.cpp) (libSceContentSearch) - imported by PPSA06465, which fails to load without them; added without an implementation because their signatures are unknown
- [sce::Json::Parser::parse with a read callback](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJson2/Export.cpp) (libSceJson2) - the callback's contract is taken from PPSA04540's: it returns 0 with the next character, and at the end of its data writes `'\0'` and returns -1. Characters are read until the callback returns non-zero or gives `'\0'`, and the text read is then parsed as by the `(text, size)` overload, so trailing characters after the value are rejected as there. Whether the console stops reading at the end of the top-level value, and what it returns when the callback fails before the end, are unknown
- [sce::Json::Parser::parse](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJson2/Export.cpp) (libSceJson2) - a number that overflows a double (e.g. `1e309`) throws: whether the console rejects the document or stores an infinite real is unknown
- [sceUserServiceGetNpAccountId](../../core/libs/prx/libSceUserService/Export.cpp) (libSceUserService) - the account id for a user with no linked PSN account is assumed to be 0 with success; libSceNpManager's `sceNpGetAccountIdA` answers the same question with `SCE_NP_ERROR_SIGNED_OUT`
- [sceUserServiceGetUserColor](../../core/libs/prx/libSceUserService/Export.cpp) (libSceUserService) - the default profile colour is assumed to be blue (0)
- [libSceVdecsw](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVdecsw/Export.cpp) (libSceVdecsw) - structure layouts follow shadPS4's `vdecsw.h` and were checked against PPSA05684's movie decoder (`movie-decoder-vdecsw.cpp`), which asserts `pictureCount == 1` at offset 0xb of `OutputInfo` and reads `InputResult` offset 0x10 as the number of frames an access unit produced. The compute, decoder memory and decode input structures and the first 0x28 bytes of the 0x50-byte decoder config are those of libSceVideodec2, and decoding goes through it. The 0x78-byte AVC picture info is filled with Videodec2's leading fields and the cropping fields only. OUTPUT_PENDING (0x81510115), INPUT_QUEUE_EMPTY (0x81510116) and DECODE_PENDING (0x81510117) follow from the codes each call's assert accepts. At most four decoded pictures are queued and the last one is held until `sceVdecswFinalizeDecodeSequence`; the console's queue depth is unknown
- [sceGameLiveStreamingGetCurrentStatus2](../../core/libs/prx/libSceGameLiveStreaming/Export.cpp) (libSceGameLiveStreaming) - the status layout (user id, on-air flag, spectator, text and command message counts, broadcast resolution, 48 reserved bytes) is taken from shadPS4's `OrbisGameLiveStreamingStatus2`; the call reports that no stream is on air, with an invalid user id
- [Depth clear draws](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (AGC driver) - a draw with `DB_RENDER_CONTROL.DEPTH_CLEAR_ENABLE` is taken to write `DB_DEPTH_CLEAR` to every pixel it covers whatever the depth test, depth bounds and depth bias say: it is drawn with an always-passing depth write and the viewport depth range pinned to the clear value. The draw is rasterized as submitted, so its coverage is the draw's own. Whether the DB still applies the depth test or HiZ to such a draw is not measured
- [Alpha-to-coverage](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (AGC driver) - only `DB_ALPHA_TO_MASK = 0xaa01` is accepted: the enable bit with the four quad offsets at 2 and no `OFFSET_ROUND`, the undithered setting [radeonsi](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/bffd91947efc1932da99aca69fc540d6d4201b0c/src/gallium/drivers/radeonsi/si_state.c#L371-L381) programs. Draws are single-sampled, so the pixel shader [writes](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) `SampleMask` 0 when its MRT0 alpha is below one half or NaN and all ones otherwise, and runs with late fragment tests. That rule was measured on a Radeon 660M (gfx1035, RADV with `RADV_DEBUG=noatocdithering`, which programs the same register) for 0, -0, 0.25, the two floats below one half, one half, the two above, 0.75, 1, 2, -1, both infinities and both NaNs, and is pinned in [AlphaToCoverage.cpp](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/tests/execution/AlphaToCoverage.cpp); it is not measured on gfx1013. Vulkan's `alphaToCoverageEnable` is not used: the NVIDIA driver 615.78.08 dithers single-sample coverage (an alpha of 0.25 writes half the pixels of an RTX 3050 Laptop target). Other offsets, `OFFSET_ROUND`, a sample-mask export, and an MRT0 that is missing, swizzled, packed or exported without a floating-point alpha throw
- [MSAA_ENABLE on a single-sample target](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (AGC driver) - `PA_SC_MODE_CNTL_0.MSAA_ENABLE` with `PA_SC_AA_CONFIG.MSAA_NUM_SAMPLES = 0` is drawn like the same state without the bit when sample 0 of the four `PA_SC_AA_SAMPLE_LOCS_PIXEL_*_0` registers is at the pixel center and the draw rasterizes filled triangles with alpha-to-coverage and conservative rasterization off. [RADV](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/745709a000482a4a24c91faef5c5a40bd653a863/src/amd/vulkan/radv_cmd_buffer.c#L13606-13609) programs this state for one-sample custom sample locations on GFX10 and later, where the bit makes the rasterizer use the programmed location of sample 0 ([061bc615](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/commit/061bc6151a11597f720535f00e79a5752e24aff6)); that the bit changes nothing else for such a draw is inferred from that and is not measured on hardware. Other locations of sample 0, absent location registers, points, lines, rectangles, alpha-to-coverage and conservative rasterization throw
- [GPU flips](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVideoOut/src/VideoOutDriver.cpp) (libSceVideoOut) - a command-buffer flip of a buffer index with no registered buffer when the GPU reaches it presents black and completes like any flip (flip event, count, `flipPendingNum`). The console's behaviour is unknown; PPSA05684 requires that it completes: its renderer submits a flip of buffer 0 while another thread waits for `sceVideoOutIsFlipPending` to reach 0 before registering the buffers, and `flipPendingNum` counts that flip from its submission as in shadPS4
- [sceAgcDriverGetEqContextId](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Eq/src/Query.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - returns `ident`: an end-of-pipe interrupt is delivered only to the registration whose id equals the raising queue, so `ident` is that context's id (titles register 0 or 1 for graphics and `0x20 + 8 * pipe + queue` for compute). The console's value is not known; KytyPS5 returns the event's `data`, which it fills with the interrupt's context id, whereas `data` here is the EV_CLEAR trigger count. Other event filters throw
- [sceAgcSetSubmitMode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Misc/src/Suspend.cpp) (libSceAgc) - mode values unknown; only 0 is accepted
- [User data registers](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Diagnostics.cpp) (AGC driver) - a `SPI_SHADER_USER_DATA_*` or `COMPUTE_USER_DATA_*` register the title never wrote reads as 0 when a stage's RSRC2 count covers it, as Kyty (zeroed `UserSgprInfo`) and SharpEmu (missing registers read 0) do, and as AMD PAL and Mesa initialize them; the console's value is not confirmed
- [SET_PREDICATION](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Pm4.cpp) (AGC driver) - boolean predication follows the AMD PM4 encoding [Mesa](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/main/src/amd/common/sid.h) uses: operation 3 reads 64 bits and 4 reads 32 bits, and bit 8 runs predicated packets when the value is non-zero (clear: when it is zero); the bit 12 hint is ignored. The address must have its low 4 bits clear, as `sceAgcDcbSetPredication` writes it. Query predication (Z-pass, primitive count), a predicated COND_EXEC, predicated command buffer chains and flips in predicated command buffers throw
- [sceAgcGetGsOversubscription](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Shader/src/GsOversubscription.cpp) (libSceAgc) - signature and occupancy formula (base and expanded vertex/export capacities, budget shift, `GE_PC_ALLOC` / `SPI_SHADER_PGM_RSRC4_GS` encoding) follow KytyPS5 only, not confirmed on hardware or a title; a negative or NaN factor that gives a negative target, a missing GS context register or a zero `GE_MAX_OUTPUT_PER_SUBGROUP` throws
- [Vertex entry merged wave info](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/InstructionTranslator.cpp) (recompiler) - `s3` of every vertex-path shader is set to `waveSize | waveSize << 8` (vertex and primitive count of a full wave, wave index 0), or to `32 | 32 << 8` for a wave64 program on a 32-lane host subgroup, which runs it at one lane per invocation and holds no lanes 32-63. Layout taken from the prologue Mesa's ACO emits for NGG vertex shaders (`s_bfe_u64 exec` from `s3`), not measured on hardware. The driver gives every graphics program user data from `s8`, so the condition does not tell NGG from legacy vertex shaders: they just never read `s3`. A draw whose last wave is partial still reports a full wave, and so does a host wave with idle invocations (an RTX 5070 ran every vertex warp of a non-indexed triangle list with 30 of its 32 invocations).
- [NGG vertex programs on the mesh path](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (AGC driver) - an unregistered vertex program with `PRIMGEN_EN` that uses LDS or `s_barrier` runs on the geometry (mesh) path. Each input primitive is assumed to yield at most one output primitive, the subgroup is bounded by `GE_MAX_OUTPUT_PER_SUBGROUP` as a vertex budget, and the GS/ES VGPR count checks are skipped, because the merged inputs keep their positions. Taken from the code Mesa's ACO emits for NGG culling and from PPSA59322 and PPSA99010 draws, not measured on hardware. Registered programs keep the vertex path
- [s_barrier in vertex-path programs](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvFlowEmitter.cpp) (recompiler) - emits no instruction when the program has no LDS or GDS access and no memory write or atomic, so no wave of the subgroup can observe another; the `GS_ALLOC_REQ` such a barrier orders on NGG passthrough programs is already dropped on the vertex path. The Vertex execution model has no workgroup barrier. Programs with LDS, GDS or memory writes throw. Not measured on hardware
- [Strided buffer overhang](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderResources.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a strided buffer descriptor that ends less than one stride past the end of the readable registered allocation holding its base is bound only up to that allocation's end, even when another readable allocation starts right there. This assumes the bytes past the end are the rounded-up `num_records` of the last record (RADV rounds it up), which no fetch inside the buffer reads; a guest that really keeps a vertex split across two adjacent allocations would read zeros for the cut tail. Taken from the RetroArch Dolphin core case in #2912, not measured on hardware.
- [Zero-stride vertex fetch range](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/include/VertexInput.hpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - for a stride-0 V# with OOB_SELECT 2 the whole fetch reads memory unless NUM_RECORDS is 0; with OOB_SELECT 3 it reads zero when NUM_RECORDS is 0 and throws when the fetch only partly fits. Rules taken from the GFX10 OOB_SELECT table in Mesa [ac_descriptors.c](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/main/src/amd/common/ac_descriptors.c), not measured on hardware. Whether OOB_SELECT 3 checks each dword of a multi-dword fetch separately is unknown. OOB_SELECT 0 and 1 keep the structured record checks.
- [sceAgcCbCondWrite](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Cb/src/Branch.cpp) (libSceAgc) - argument order and the 9-dword COND_WRITE layout follow the clean-room [OpenAGC](https://github.com/OpenAGC/OpenAGC/blob/main/src/game_compat.c) and [sharpemu](https://github.com/sharpemu/sharpemu/blob/main/src/SharpEmu.Libs/Agc/AgcExports.CommandPackets.cs), which agree on them; SharpProspero lists another order. The write space is accepted only as 1 (memory), the one value both encode the same way, and other values throw.
- [sceAgcAcbAtomicGds_0900](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Acb/src/Control.cpp) (libSceAgc) - signature, control/mode bit mapping and the 11-dword ATOMIC_GDS (0x1d) layout follow the clean-room [OpenAGC](https://github.com/OpenAGC/OpenAGC/blob/3c7e8072f0d7684e930454794fd5c02056946d73/src/game_compat.c) only, which derives them from the library's disassembly; KytyPS5 and shadPS4 have no body for it. Not confirmed on hardware or a title (PPSA07286 imports it, nothing was run), and the driver has no ATOMIC_GDS handler (opcode 0x1d is not executed as an atomic GDS operation)
- [sceAgcGetIsTrinityMode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Misc/src/Platform.cpp) (libSceAgc) - signature from the only PPSA26344 call (a pointer to a one-byte flag, result ignored); a null pointer throws because its error code is unknown
- [sceAgcDcbAtomicGds_0900](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/DcbFlow/src/Control.cpp) (libSceAgc) - name from its NID (`zARR5aCmkoY`), the DCB counterpart of `sceAgcAcbAtomicGds_0900`, signature unknown
- [sceAgcDcbContextStateOpGetSize](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/DcbState/src/ContextState.cpp) (libSceAgc) - sizes from KytyPS5 only (5, 27, 27 and 32 dwords), equal to what `sceAgcDcbContextStateOp_0100` writes; KytyPS5 returns 0 for an operation above 3, here it throws
- [sceAgcBranchPatchSetThenTarget_0300, sceAgcBranchPatchSetElseTarget_0300](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Patch/src/Branch.cpp) (libSceAgc) - names from their NIDs (`7Wa3aeJgeVU`, `rP5xLdOf26k`); the `(packet, cachePolicy, target, sizeInDwords)` signature is inferred from PPSA28416's calls (a branch packet, 0, an aligned target, 4), with the policy stored where `sceAgcCbBranch` puts it
- [sceAgcDcbSet{Cx,Sh,Uc}RegistersIndirectGetSize](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/DcbState/src/Registers.cpp) (libSceAgc) - the count argument is ignored: PPSA28416 calls them with leftover register contents (0xf5f5039c), so the real functions likely take none; the size is 20 bytes either way
- [sceAgcUpdateInterpolantMapping_0100](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Misc/src/ShaderFusion.cpp) (libSceAgc) - name from its NID (`k0E7vkgqAuE`); the `(regs, vs, ps)` arguments are the declared ones, behaviour unknown
- [sceFontCharacterGetSyllableStringState](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/Text.cpp) (libSceFont) - the signature `(textCharacter, int* syllableStringState)` follows [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Font/SceFont.Engine.cs). Measured on PS4 firmware 11.00 `libSceFont.sprx` (not on a PS5): the state is 0 for Latin, CJK, Hangul, Arabic, Hebrew, Devanagari and the other scripts tried, and the library sets 1, 3 or 4 (never 2 on real text) for Thai while it creates the string; a null character or a null result returns `SCE_FONT_ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER`, and a null character writes 0 to a non-null result. This returns 0 for every character outside the Thai block (U+0E00 to U+0E7F), as [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/d2413fc2ebd91d1b7234197c2dd5bd48e1cb6a51/src/libs/libFont.cpp#L1805-L1815) does, and throws for Thai characters, whose cluster states are not modelled. SharpProspero's `SceFontSyllableString` names 0 NonDetection, 1 Starting, 2 Following, 3 Depending and 4 Connecting. PPSA06699 (Hot Shots Golf 2) calls it.
- [sceAgcWriteDataPatchSetDst, sceAgcWriteDataPatchSetCachePolicy and their Async forms](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Patch/src/WaitRegMem.cpp) (libSceAgc) - the signatures `(packet, uint8 value)` are inferred from the other patch functions, as no public declaration was found; the value is taken in the encoding of the `dst` and `cachePolicy` arguments of `sceAgcDcbWriteData` (graphics) and `sceAgcAcbWriteData` (Async, compute), and the patched packet is the one those calls would write. `sceAgcAsyncWriteDataPatchSetAddressOrOffset` is assumed to patch the address like `sceAgcWriteDataPatchSetAddressOrOffset`, as the compute WRITE_DATA packet keeps it in the same dwords
- [dbOlWdppb4o](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Shader/src/InterpolantMapping.cpp) (libSceAgc) - unknown name; same arguments as `sceAgcCreateInterpolantMapping_0100`; the `SPI_PS_INPUT_CNTL` bits for `is_f16` 2 inputs follow KytyPS5, not confirmed on hardware
- [sceAgcGetDataPacketPayloadRange](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Misc/src/PacketInfo.cpp) (libSceAgc) - signature, the `{base, size in bytes}` output and the type 0 range (header + 1, one dword longer than the type 1 payload) from KytyPS5 only, matching `sceAgcGetDataPacketPayloadAddress_0090`; prosper returns header + 2 for both types from that address function
- [sceAgcAsyncCondExecPatchSetCommandAddress, sceAgcAsyncCondExecPatchSetEnd](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Patch/src/CondExec.cpp) (libSceAgc) - no reference has a body for them; they are assumed to patch the COND_EXEC packet like `sceAgcCondExecPatchSetCommandAddress` and `sceAgcCondExecPatchSetEnd`, since `sceAgcAcbCondExec` writes the same packet as `sceAgcDcbCondExec`, as `sceAgcAsyncRewindPatchSetRewindState` does for REWIND
- [sceAgcAcbAtomicGds_0900](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Acb/src/Control.cpp) (libSceAgc) - name from its NID (`gQkqkLttcpw`), signature unknown
- [sceAgcDcbPrimeUtcl2](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/DcbState/src/Display.cpp) (libSceAgc) - assumed to write the packet `sceAgcAcbPrimeUtcl2` writes: PRIME_UTCL2 has the same 5 dwords on the graphics and compute rings. Not confirmed on a title. Both write a NOP of that size, as there is no TLB to prime
- [sceAgcDcbQueueEndOfShaderActionGetSize](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/DcbState/src/ContextState.cpp) (libSceAgc) - the builder is inline in the SDK and not exported; the size is assumed to be that of the RELEASE_MEM packet `sceAgcCbReleaseMem` writes (32 bytes), the same as `sceAgcAcbQueueEndOfShaderActionGetSize`, as in [OpenAGC](https://github.com/OpenAGC/OpenAGC/blob/main/src/game_compat.c) and [REmu](https://github.com/xnetcat/REmu/blob/main/crates/remu-runtime/src/agc_dcb.rs)
- [sceKernelInternalMemoryGetModuleSegmentInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Module/src/Module.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown signature
- [sceKernelGetModuleInfoForUnwind](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Module/src/Module.cpp) (libkernel) - on Linux the tables come from `sceKernelGetModuleInfoFromAddr`, whose `eh_frame_size` includes the zero terminator, as measured on firmware 13.60 (reported in [#2419](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/issues/2419), not reproduced here); on Windows it is assumed to stop before the terminator. LLVM libunwind's `CFI_Parser::findFDE` stops at the terminator or the length, whichever comes first, so it finds the same FDEs either way. Only the main executable and `.guest.prx` images report tables; other host images report none, as on Windows
- [sceKernelGetModuleInfoFromAddr](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Module/src/ModuleInfo.cpp) (libkernel) - the 0x1A8-byte info layout follows the [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4) reimplementation; a title's `libc.prx` reads only `id` (PPSA14632). The meaning of `flags` is unknown; only 2 is accepted. The input `st_size` is ignored, because PPSA21703's `libc.prx` passes an uninitialized 0x1A8-byte buffer; the full size is written back. The id is the `sceKernelLoadStartModule` handle of an image opened through the loader and otherwise a stable id from the same range. The name is the host file name without `.guest.prx`. An image with more than 4 load segments (the converted executable has 7) reports its first 4. `tls_offset` is 0 and `ref_count` is 1, as the host loader exposes neither. On Windows the module is the PE image holding the address: a relinked image reports its `.elf` sections as segments and the eh_frame_hdr recorded in `.ehmeta`, any other image its first 4 sections with memory access and the `.ehfram` section without an eh_frame_hdr; the TLS fields and `init_proc_addr`/`fini_proc_addr` are 0
- [sceKernelGetModuleList](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Module/src/ModuleInfo.cpp) (libkernel) - lists the main program and every loaded `.prx` image in load order; host runtime libraries are left out. The handles are the ids `sceKernelGetModuleInfoFromAddr` reports, not the console's. The `(int32_t* handles, size_t count, size_t* actual)` signature and returning `ENOMEM` without writing `actual` after filling the first `count` handles when more images are loaded follow [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/0fe263a4760dfbfa973366890061749b4af0de97/src/core/libraries/kernel/process.cpp#L244-L264); whether the console writes any handles in that case is unknown
- [sceKernelGetModuleInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Module/src/ModuleInfo.cpp) (libkernel) - the 0x160-byte layout (`st_size`, `name[256]`, 4 segments, `segment_count`, `fingerprint[20]`) follows [fpPS4](https://github.com/red-prig/fpPS4/blob/04cefd43e6fddd1ab033e7980cd356d14c964905/sys/sys_types.pas#L319-L337) and [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/0fe263a4760dfbfa973366890061749b4af0de97/src/core/module.h#L15-L31). Only that `st_size` is accepted, as in shadPS4; other sizes return `EINVAL`, a null pointer `EFAULT` and a handle `sceKernelGetModuleList` does not list `ESRCH`. Name and segments are the ones `sceKernelGetModuleInfoFromAddr` reports, on Windows from the same PE sections, so `prot` uses the read/write/execute bits 1/2/4, while fpPS4 and shadPS4 store the ELF `p_flags`. The fingerprint is left zero, as the host image does not carry it
- [sceKernelReleaseFlexibleMemory](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/Export.cpp) (libkernel) - unmaps the range like sceKernelMunmap, as fpPS4 and prosper do; whether the console keeps the virtual range reserved after releasing its pages is unverified
- [sceKernelAvailableFlexibleMemorySize](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/Export.cpp) (libkernel) - the segments of the title's executable and of its `.guest.prx` modules count against the 448 MiB flexible budget while they are loaded, for this size and for the flexible maps, as [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/3a9f57265d28975658f1ca941daa5c879c7a74bd/src/core/module.cpp#L165) maps every segment of a module outside its system module directory as flexible memory on the PS4. [Counted](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Module/src/ModuleInfo.cpp) are the segments the title's ELF gives permissions, each rounded to 16 KiB pages; not counted are segments without permissions, the relinker's own blocks (on Linux both are the read/write/execute `PT_LOAD`s, plus the header block at file offset 0; on Windows everything outside the `.elf` sections) and the host system libraries. The Linux image carries no marker for the relinker's blocks, so they are told apart by those flags and that offset only: a title segment that is writable and executable, or starts at file offset 0, is not counted. A module whose segments exceed what is left still loads, and the available size then reads 0; shadPS4 fails the segment map instead ([memory.cpp](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/3a9f57265d28975658f1ca941daa5c879c7a74bd/src/core/memory.cpp#L519-L524) returns `EINVAL`, [module.cpp](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/3a9f57265d28975658f1ca941daa5c879c7a74bd/src/core/module.cpp#L168-L169) asserts). Which segments the PS5 charges, its rounding, whether system modules use the title's budget and when an unload releases the charge are not measured. PPSA15508 subtracts the reported size and a 10 MiB module allowance from its own 448 MiB budget at start-up and stops when the result exceeds its 146 MiB code and data limit ([#2883](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/issues/2883))
- [Memory protection bits 0x100 and 0x200](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/DirectMemory.cpp) (libkernel) - taken as audio coprocessor (ACP) read and write, as in the [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5) and [etaHEN](https://github.com/etaHEN/etaHEN) headers; the coprocessor work runs on the host CPU, so they grant host read and read/write access like the GPU bits
- [_umtx_op](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/SyncOnAddress/src/SyncOnAddress.cpp) (libkernel) - `UMTX_OP_WAIT`, `UMTX_OP_WAKE`, `UMTX_OP_WAIT_UINT`, `UMTX_OP_WAIT_UINT_PRIVATE`, `UMTX_OP_WAKE_PRIVATE` and `UMTX_OP_NWAKE_PRIVATE` follow FreeBSD 11's [kern_umtx.c](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/stable/11/sys/kern/kern_umtx.c): a timeout whose size argument is at most 16 is a relative `timespec`, a larger one an `_umtx_time`, and a wake of 0 or fewer wakes one waiter. `UMTX_OP_NWAKE_PRIVATE` reads the value as an `int` count of pointers at the object and wakes every waiter on each one; a count of 0 or fewer reads nothing, and a null list fails with `EFAULT`, where FreeBSD's `copyin` would also fail for any other unreadable list. Waiters share the queue of `sceKernelSyncOnAddressWait`. Operation 0 fails with `EOPNOTSUPP` and operations above 26 with `EINVAL`, as in FreeBSD 11; `UMTX_OP_WAIT_UINT` with a value that does not fit in 32 bits returns 0 at once, as FreeBSD's zero-extended compare does. Operation 1 throws, since PPSA12544's call_once uses it in a way FreeBSD rejects; the other operations throw, and so does a misaligned object. PPSA12544's `libc.prx` passes `UMTX_OP_WAIT` and `UMTX_OP_WAIT_UINT` a pointer to a 4-byte microsecond timeout with size argument 0, which FreeBSD reads as a `timespec`, and waits for a running call_once with operation 1; its `PS5Util.prx` passes a `timespec`. Whether the console reads a microsecond timeout there, and what operation 1 does on it, is unverified. A libc call cannot be told apart from a `timespec` call by the size argument alone, so a timeout with size argument 0 is read as a `timespec` and only throws when it is not a valid one (negative seconds, nanoseconds outside [0, 1e9)), which is where FreeBSD would return `EINVAL`; a microsecond value that happens to read as a valid `timespec` is waited on as one
- [sceKernelSyncOnAddressWait](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/SyncOnAddress/src/SyncOnAddress.cpp) (libkernel) - the only known caller passes a null timeout and a name string as the fourth argument; the timeout is assumed to point to microseconds like the other kernel waits, and the name is ignored
- [sceKernelSyncOnAddressWait8 / sceKernelSyncOnAddressWait16 / sceKernelSyncOnAddressWait32 / sceKernelSyncOnAddressWait64](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/SyncOnAddress/src/SyncOnAddress.cpp) (libkernel) - libc's `__std_atomic_wait_direct_8/16/32/64` pass a microsecond timeout or null and ignore the result; the by-value comparand, return values and the 2- and 8-byte alignment of Wait16 and Wait64 are assumed
- [scePthreadRwlockDestroy, pthread_rwlock_destroy](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/src/Rwlock.cpp) (libkernel) - measured on a PS5 (firmware 13.60): destroying a rwlock returns 0 whether it is free or held for reading or writing, and leaves 1 in the handle; every call on that handle then returns `EINVAL` until it is initialized again, and a zero handle is initialized on first use by the `scePthreadRwlock` calls as by the POSIX ones. A rwlock destroyed while held is not freed, so its memory leaks. Destroying a rwlock while another thread is acquiring it is not guarded
- [scePthreadCondWait / scePthreadCondTimedwait](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/src/Cond.cpp) (libkernel) - before waiting, FreeBSD libthr's [`cond_wait_common`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/main/lib/libthr/thread/thr_cond.c#L1992-L2029) checks ownership through [`_mutex_owned`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/main/lib/libthr/thread/thr_mutex.c#L1166-L1178): an unowned mutex returns `EPERM`, while its destroyed marker returns `EINVAL`. An untouched adaptive static initializer (slot value 1) therefore returns `EPERM`; the test covers this, an uninitialized slot, a mutex owned by another thread, and the destroyed marker for both timed and untimed sce and POSIX waits. A recursive mutex locked several times is released completely for the wait and relocked to the same depth afterwards, as FreeBSD libthr does (`_mutex_cv_unlock`, `_mutex_cv_lock`). These FreeBSD-derived behaviors remain unverified on console hardware
- [sceKernelAioSubmitReadCommandsMultiple / sceKernelAioSubmitWriteCommandsMultiple / sceKernelAioWaitRequests / sceKernelAioPollRequests / sceKernelAioCancelRequest / sceKernelAioCancelRequests / sceKernelAioDeleteRequests](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Aio/src/Aio.cpp) (libkernel) - behaviour from shadPS4 (one id per request for the Multiple submits, cancel turns any request aborted and id 0 reports processing, wait mode 2 returns once one request completed, poll copies every state without waiting); the 128 requests or ids per batch limit is from SharpProspero, with an unknown error, so larger batches throw; wait modes other than 1 (and) and 2 (or) throw; null pointers, negative counts and invalid ids return the codes the single-request siblings use, checked before any state is written
- [scePthreadAttr setters and getters](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/src/Attr.cpp) (libkernel) - measured on a PS5 (firmware 13.60): a null or uninitialized attribute returns `EINVAL`, as does a null output of the stack size, detach state and scheduling parameter getters; a policy other than 1 and 3, an inheritance other than 0 and 4, or a priority outside 256 to 767 (or a null parameter) returns `ENOTSUP` (`0x8002002D`); a detach state other than 0 and 1 and a stack size below 16384 return `EINVAL`; any affinity mask is accepted. A null attribute pointer to `scePthreadAttrInit` and a null output of `scePthreadAttrGetaffinity` crashed the payload, so they still throw. A new attribute holds a 64 KiB stack size on the console and 1 MiB here, which the host code that runs on guest threads needs
- [sceKernelGetAvailableCpumask](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/src/Thread.cpp) (libkernel) - returns the default thread affinity, 0x1FFF (CPUs 0 to 12), as KytyPS5 does. Titles pin threads within it (PPSA02664 passes 0x1FFB, Hades II PPSA36082 0x3 and 0x3F); whether CPU 13 is available to a title is not confirmed
- [sceKernelGetCurrentCpu](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) - guest affinities are not applied to host threads, so the host CPU index is mapped into the calling thread's affinity clipped to `sceKernelGetAvailableCpumask` (the available mask when they do not overlap): host CPU n reports the (n modulo the CPU count)-th CPU of that mask, so a thread pinned to one CPU reports that CPU, as a FreeBSD `cpuset` guarantees on the console. Which CPU the console reports for a thread allowed on several is not known; shadPS4 returns 0
- [pthread_attr_setsolosched_np](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/Posix/Attr.cpp) (libkernel) takes `(attr, int)` and stores the value through `scePthreadAttrSetsolosched`, returning `EINVAL` (22) for a null or uninitialized attribute like the other POSIX attribute setters. Only the name is public (aerolib `2+pVfgiEd7A`); the signature and behaviour follow the SCE twin, which [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/947627d10d4ef4b68ba3c0189aab6c368e70a8d2/src/kernel/pthread.cpp#L2188-L2199) and [sharpemu](https://github.com/sharpemu/sharpemu/blob/a2983692930564815a77f81e74c151268f5b935f/src/SharpEmu.Libs/Kernel/KernelPthreadExtendedCompatExports.cs#L891-L913) store as an `int` without a range check, and [PS5PCEM](https://github.com/iStark/PS5PCEM/blob/71de7681f06c5db59a1120a3bb554c86780c3f5f/src/hle/libs/kernel_threading.zig#L1396-L1398) implements this alias over it. On a PS5 (firmware 13.60) only 0 and 16 are accepted; every other value returns `ENOTSUP` (45, `0x8002002D` from the SCE call) and keeps the stored one, and the SCE calls return `EINVAL` for a null or uninitialized attribute. PPSA15878 passes 16. Threads created from the attribute are not scheduled differently; what solo scheduling does on the console is unknown. Returnal (PPSA01285 v03.000.005) stopped on it with `what(): pthread_attr_setsolosched_np_nid_postfix not implemented` (#2872, #1155); LEGO Horizon Adventures (PPSA14632, #734) and Palworld (PPSA23126, #2598) import it
- [sceKernelMapNamedFlexibleMemoryInternal](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/Export.cpp) (libkernel) - flag 0x8000 is accepted and ignored; its meaning is not established. KytyPS5 (`GUEST_MAP_UNKNOWN_8000`) and sharpemu accept it without reading it. Every known caller is the SDK 9 `libc.prx` heap (Astro Bot PPSA21564, PPSA22520), which passes it with read/write protection and uses the mapping as an older `libc.prx` uses `sceKernelMapNamedFlexibleMemory` with flags 0
- [sceKernelMtypeprotect](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/DirectMemory.cpp) (libkernel) - sets the memory type of the direct mappings in the page-rounded range and of the direct memory behind them, as shadPS4 and prosper do (KytyPS5 ignores the type); the type is not validated, flexible pages in the range only get the protection, and a later map of the retyped direct memory reports the type it was allocated with. `sceKernelBatchMap2` operation 4 takes the same path with the entry type
- [Map hints inside a reservation](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/DirectMemory.cpp) (libkernel) - `sceKernelMapDirectMemory` and the flexible and anonymous maps place a mapping without `MAP_FIXED` at its hint address when the whole range lies in a range still reserved by `sceKernelReserveVirtualRange`, as if `MAP_FIXED` were set. Taken from PPSA01474, which reserves 64 GiB and then maps its heaps inside it with hints and no `MAP_FIXED`, and uses the hint addresses afterwards; not measured on the console. Hints outside a reservation, or over a range that is partly mapped, stay hints
- [Guest arena](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/GuestArena.cpp) (libc) - the application map area is assumed to end at 0xFC_0000_0000, as `SCE_KERNEL_APP_MAP_AREA_END_ADDR` does on the PS4. On Windows, host allocations made in the arena before libc loads stay, and a fixed guest mapping over one throws
- [sceLibcInternalBacktraceForGame](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/HeapDiagnostics.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal, implemented in libc) - unknown signature
- [sceLibcInternalHeapErrorReportForGame](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/HeapDiagnostics.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal, implemented in libc) - unknown signature
- [malloc_usable_size](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/ApplicationHeap.cpp) (libc) - with the default allocator it returns the size the block was requested with (the range registered for it), not the rounded-up chunk size Sony's allocator reports; both satisfy "at least the requested size", and bytes past the request would not be write-watched. With an application allocator it calls entry 9 of the replacement table (`malloc_usable_size` in KytyPS5's `MallocReplace` order) and throws when the table leaves it null, like `malloc_stats_fast`
- [reallocf](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/FileAndHeap.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal, implemented in libc) - follows FreeBSD 11 `lib/libc/stdlib/reallocf.c` on top of this libc's `realloc`, not measured on the console: size 0 frees the block and returns null as `realloc` does here, so the block is not freed twice; when `realloc` fails because the application allocator returns null or the default heap rejects the size, the block is freed through the same allocator, `errno` is `ENOMEM` and null is returned. Other failures (an exhausted guest arena, an unknown pointer) throw, as they do for `realloc`
- [_sceLibcInternalThreadAtexit, _sceLibcInternalThreadDtors](../../core/libs/prx/libSceLibcInternal/Export.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal) - FreeBSD `__cxa_thread_atexit` semantics assumed; a null or non-image destructor and an allocation failure throw
- [_sceKernelRtldThreadAtexitIncrement, _sceKernelRtldThreadAtexitDecrement](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Rtld.cpp) (libkernel) - the argument is the `dso` address a title's `libc.prx` passes to `__cxa_thread_atexit` (PPSA14632), which fails when Increment returns nonzero. As in the [fpPS4](https://github.com/red-prig/fpPS4) reimplementation, Increment takes a reference on the loaded image containing the address and Decrement releases one; the variants without the leading underscore are assumed to match
- [sceImeGetPanelPositionAndForm](../../core/libs/prx/libSceIme/Export.cpp) (libSceIme) - the output is taken to be the `PositionAndForm` that `sceImeDialogGetPanelPositionAndForm` fills; it is never written, since no panel can be open. That a closed panel is reported before a null output is assumed by analogy with `sceImeSetCaret`, not confirmed on a PS5 title
- [_sceLibcInternalForceTlsDestructor](../../core/libs/prx/libSceLibcInternal/Export.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal) - behaviour follows `sceLibcForceTlsDestructor(int)` in a title's `libc.prx` (disassembly): the calling thread's destructors whose dso belongs to the module run newest first, only if the dso's module is loaded and the dso points to itself, in up to 4 passes. The return type is unknown (that function returns nothing), 0 is returned. The module of each dso is looked up when the destructors are forced, not when they are registered as `libc.prx` does
- [acosl](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Math.cpp) (libc) - computed by the host's math library, like the other libc math functions here, not by the console's (FreeBSD msun ld80 code in Sony's `libc.prx`): results can differ from the console's in the last bit, and on Linux glibc sets `errno` to `EDOM` for `acosl` outside [-1, 1] where msun only raises the floating-point exception. `long double` is the x87 80-bit format, as Sony's `frexpl` uses it (`fldt 0x8(%rsp)`, result in `st(0)`)
- [atanl, sinl, cosl, tanl, sinhl, coshl, tanhl, expl, exp2l, logl, log10l and powl](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Math.cpp) (libc) - computed by the host's math library, as `acosl` is, not by the console's FreeBSD msun ld80 code: results can differ from the console's in the last bit (glibc's `log10l(1000)` is not exactly 3), and on Linux glibc sets `errno` to `EDOM` or `ERANGE` where msun only raises the floating-point exception. `ldexpl` and `truncl` are exact operations and give the console's result; `logb` and `remainder` give the console's value too, but on Linux glibc sets `errno` (`ERANGE` for `logb(0)`, `EDOM` for `remainder` by zero or of an infinity) where msun only raises the floating-point exception
- [std_execute_once](../../core/libs/prx/libc/Export.cpp) (libc) - assumed to be the plain-named twin of `std::_Execute_once` (`_ZSt13_Execute_onceRSt9once_flagPFiPvS1_PS1_ES1_`, the callee of Dinkumware `call_once`): it delegates there and gets the [thread.once.callonce] semantics already verified for that symbol (one invocation per flag, concurrent callers block until it completes, and a failing or throwing invocation leaves the flag unready so the next caller runs it). The name equivalence is not confirmed on a title, and the callback convention (called with (null, arg, null), non-zero return meaning success) is the twin's, unverified for this symbol
- [Dinkumware mutexes and conditions](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Threads.cpp) (libc) use pointer-to-handle arguments, matching the console aerolib signatures reported in [#3079](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/pull/3079). The title's `_Mtx_lock` maps `0` to `_Thrd_success` (0), `0x8002000b` to `_Thrd_busy` (3), and other failures to `_Thrd_error` (4); its internal mutex init recognizes recursive flag `0x100`, while every other type uses default attributes. The observed relock failure for a plain mutex suggests those defaults are error-checking, but that has not been confirmed on hardware. The title's `_Mtx_unlock` and `_Cnd_wait` return success regardless of their internal result. The host implementation treats invalid ownership preconditions as no-ops, and throws for invalid handles, host wait errors and destroying a locked mutex as debug guards; those cases are not confirmed against the console.
- [random, srandom, initstate, setstate](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Random.cpp) (libc) follow FreeBSD 11 [random.c](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/4ab2e064d7950be84256d671a7ae93f87cc6aa36/lib/libc/stdlib/random.c): the additive feedback generator on a default 128-byte `TYPE_3` table, `good_rand` seeding, state sizes rounded down to 8, 32, 64, 128 or 256 bytes, and `setstate` returning the previous state or null for a rear position past the degree. `initstate` below 8 bytes returns null without setting errno, as that file and its manual do (glibc sets `EINVAL`). FreeBSD does not lock the generator; here every call is serialised by one lock, so concurrent callers draw the serial sequence between them. A null state throws, where FreeBSD faults. That the console's `libSceLibcInternal` uses this FreeBSD code and not another generator is unverified
- [tcgetattr, tcsetattr](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Terminal.cpp) (libkernel) - every file and pipe descriptor is taken as not a terminal (`ENOTTY`), and a socket gets `EADDRNOTAVAIL` for `AF_INET` and `EOPNOTSUPP` otherwise, as FreeBSD's `in_control` and `pru_control_notsupp` answer a termios ioctl; what descriptors 0 to 2 of a title are on the console, and whether any of them is a terminal there, is unknown
- [select](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Socket.cpp) (libkernel) - descriptor 0 is never reported readable and descriptors 1 and 2 are always writable, as for a console title with no input; reads from descriptor 0 still go to the host stdin. `/dev/random` and `/dev/urandom` are always readable and writable, as FreeBSD reports a seeded random device. Other descriptors that are not libSceNet sockets throw. Dungeons (PPSA16064) selects on descriptor 0
- [select](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Socket.cpp) (libkernel) - a descriptor the socket poller reports as unknown that is below `GuestSockets::FirstDescriptor` is polled on the host instead of throwing, except on Windows where it still throws; an invalid host descriptor fails with `EBADF`, and mixed host and socket sets are polled in short alternating slices so neither side waits out the full slice alone. Wobbly Life (PPSA29413) selects on file descriptors while streaming its bundles
- [__progname](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown data export
- [environ](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) - data export pointing to a fixed empty environment so titles that import it load; it is not the environment libc's `getenv` reads, so it lists none of those variables and `setenv`/`putenv` changes do not appear in it
- [seteuid, setegid, setuid, setreuid, setgroups, getgroups](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) - the guest process is assumed to run as root, with real, effective and saved user and group ids 0. As in FreeBSD's `sys_seteuid`, `sys_setegid`, `sys_setuid` and `sys_setreuid`, id 0 (or -1 for an unchanged `setreuid` id) is then accepted without a privilege check and changes no credential, so 0 is returned; any other id would change an id, which is not modelled, and throws. `setgroups` keeps the supplementary group list as `kern_setgroups` does (`NGROUPS_MAX` 1023, an empty list leaves the effective group as the only entry, a first entry other than 0 would change the effective group and throws) and `getgroups` reports it. The ids and privileges a title has on a console are unverified
- [pthread_barrierattr_setpshared](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/Posix/Barrier.cpp) (libkernel) - PTHREAD_PROCESS_SHARED is accepted on the assumption that the console follows FreeBSD 11.0 libthr, which accepts it (9.0 rejects it with EINVAL); unverified on hardware. Guest code runs in one process, so a shared barrier behaves as a private one
- [pthread_getname_np](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/Posix/Thread.cpp) (libkernel) - a thread that has already been joined returns `ESRCH` (3) on a PS5 (firmware 13.60), but its object is freed after the join here, so the pointer cannot be told apart from a live thread and its name is read as for a live one
- [sceSslClose](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSsl/Export.cpp) (libSceSsl) - unknown signature
- [sceSslGetSerialNumber](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSsl/Export.cpp) (libSceSsl) - unknown signature
- [sceAudioInIsPadEmbeddedMic](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioIn/Export.cpp) (libSceAudioIn) - name from its NID (`X+4jdIS75P0`); signature `int32_t(int32_t handle)` from the Ghost of Yotei (PPSA26344) call site in #2355; the result's meaning is unconfirmed, so it still throws rather than invent one
- [sceAudioOut2Set3DLatency](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2System.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) - assumed to take `(user_id, latency)`: KytyPS5 reads `(user_id, output, latency_us)`, but the known calls set just edi and esi; only `(0xFF, 2)` (PPSA26344) and `(0xFF, 1)` (PPSA14632) are accepted
- [sceAudioOut2MasteringInit](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2Mastering.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) - flag values unknown; only 0 is accepted. Whether the console reports an error for a repeated `sceAudioOut2MasteringInit` or for `sceAudioOut2MasteringTerm` without a matching initialization is unknown; both return 0. PPSA01289 creates the audio device of its next world before it destroys the previous one, so it calls Init twice before Term twice, and only logs a non-zero result
- [sceAudioOutSetMixLevelPadSpk](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) - effect of a negative mix level unknown; only 0 to 32768 (0 dB) is accepted
- [sceAudioOutSetMixLevelPadSpk](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) - no source applies the level (shadPS4 only stores it); it is assumed to be a linear gain on the port's own output, and the -9 dB default (11626) is applied to every pad speaker port, including those of titles that never call the function
- [sceAudioOutGetLastOutputTime](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) - signature and behaviour taken from the [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4) and [fpPS4](https://github.com/red-prig/fpPS4) reimplementations of the PS4 library, not confirmed on a PS5 title: the time is the process time at which `sceAudioOutOutput` or `sceAudioOutOutputs` accepted the port's last block, not the time the block reached the device
- [sceAudioOutSysClose, sceAudioOutSysConfigureOutput, sceAudioOutSysOpen](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) - imported by PPSA99051 and PPSA99997, which fail to load without them; added without an implementation and declared without parameters. [EVO-PLAYER-PS5](https://github.com/sainsaji/EVO-PLAYER-PS5/blob/da0c0451333bab82e79fffd2de4718e917f9dcb0/projects/evoplayer/core/src/audio/evo_pt.cpp#L166-L240), whose audio code both titles share, calls `sceAudioOutSysOpen`, `sceAudioOutSysConfigureOutput` and `sceAudioOutSysClose` only to open a port for HDMI bitstream passthrough. Its declared arguments are not confirmed on a console. Audio reaches the host device through SDL as PCM, so there is no HDMI output to switch
- [sceAudioOut2PortCreate](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2Port.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) - MAIN (type 0) format validation follows PS5 firmware 13.60 measurements: bits 8-11 accept 1, 2, 8 or 12, bits 0-6 accept 0 or 1, bit 7 requires 8 channels, and bits 12-31 do not affect validation. The sample types are still read as float and int16; bit 7's layout, the PCM effect of bits 12-31 and the port `flags` are unverified. Other port types retain unverified format validation, including 6 channels and rejection of bits 12-31
- [sceAudioOut2PortCreate](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2Port.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) - 12 channels are assumed to be L R C LFE Ls Rs Lb Rb Ltf Rtf Ltb Rtb as in KytyPS5's 12-to-8 fold, and 6 and 8 channels to be its first 6 and 8; no title or SDK header confirms the order
- [sceAudioOut2PortCreate](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2Port.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) - the stereo fold is assumed, not the console's downmix: C at -3 dB into both sides, Ls/Rs, Lb/Rb and Ltf/Rtf at -3 dB and Ltb/Rtb at -6 dB into their own side, LFE dropped
- [sceAudioOut2PortSetAttributes](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2Port.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) - each PCM buffer set (attribute 0) is taken as one grain: buffers set between two pushes are queued and played one per grain, at most 16 queued. A grain with no new buffer repeats the last one only when the port has always been given that same buffer (rewritten in place); a port whose buffers move on plays silence instead. Inferred from PPSA12544, whose object port (type 6) is given one grain of a 64-grain ring per set, often two or three between pushes and then none for a few; the console behaviour for several sets per push and for a push without a new buffer is not documented
- [scePsmlMfsrGetContextBufferRequirement1100, scePsmlMfsrCreateContext1100, scePsmlMfsrGetDispatchMfsrPacket1100](../../core/libs/prx/libScePsml_debug/Export.cpp) (libScePsml_debug) - argument layouts unknown; only 0x8A810001 (not initialized) is returned
- [scePsmlMfsrGetDispatchMfsrPacket1000](../../core/libs/prx/libScePsml_debug/Export.cpp) (libScePsml) - unknown signature, imported by PPSA25344; declared without parameters. libScePsml and libScePsml_debug are built from the same source
- [scePsmlMfsrCreateContext1300, scePsmlMfsrGetDispatchMfsrPacket1300](../../core/libs/prx/libScePsml_debug/Export.cpp) (libScePsml) - unknown signatures, imported by PPSA28416 next to `scePsmlMfsrGetContextBufferRequirement1100`; declared without parameters. The names are not in the aerolib NID list; they hash to the imported NIDs `kLF7WtQ1-Dg` and `DDVUyPyYHTA` (`tools/nid_names.py`)
- [scePngEncEncode](../../core/libs/prx/libScePngEnc/Export.cpp) (libScePngEnc) - whether rows may stay unfiltered when `filter_type` names a subset of the filters is unknown; as in shadPS4, they may only for 0 and for the all-filters mask
- [sceJpegEncEncode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJpegEnc/Export.cpp) (libSceJpegEnc) - `restart_interval` follows SharpProspero's field doc (0 none, -1 every row of blocks, positive a block count); a positive value is taken as the JPEG DRI interval in MCUs and -1 as one restart per MCU row, which no reference confirms. Other negative values throw
- [sceVoiceQoSInit](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVoiceQoS/Export.cpp) (libSceVoiceQoS) - `0x804E0905` for a second initialization comes from [prosper](https://github.com/mattias800/prosper)'s reading of the module and was not measured: on a PS5 a valid call blocks inside a homebrew payload, so it never returns there. The app type and the block are not used, as no endpoint is implemented
- AudioIn and NpSessionSignaling exports added without an implementation have assumed signatures
- [sceNpSessionSignalingGetConnectionFromPeerAddress](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNpSessionSignaling/Export.cpp) (libSceNpSessionSignaling) - imported by PPSA01289 and added without an implementation; unknown signature
- [vieBRwlh1Lw](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Shader/src/InterpolantMapping.cpp) (libSceAgc) - unknown name; the `(regs, gs, ps)` arguments of `sceAgcCreateInterpolantMapping_0100` come from OpenAGC only
- [scePadIsRemoteController](../../core/libs/prx/libScePad/Export.cpp) (libScePad) - the name hashes to NID `fCWdlnmB1Ks`; the signature `(int handle, bool* remote)` comes from PPSA16064, which zeroes a byte, passes its address after `scePadGetControllerInformation` and ignores the result. The error codes follow the other pad calls. Controllers are local devices, so the answer is always false
- [scePadSetAngularVelocityBiasCorrectionState](../../core/libs/prx/libScePad/Export.cpp) (libScePad) - inferred signature `(int handle, bool enabled)` follows `scePadSetTiltCorrectionState` and `scePadSetAngularVelocityDeadbandState`; an invalid handle returns `PAD_ERROR_INVALID_HANDLE`, disabling is a no-op, and enabling throws because bias correction is not modelled
- [sceKernelGetOperationMode](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) - the signature `(int* mode, int* submode)` comes from PPSA12544, which logs both values after the call and ignores the result. A homebrew payload on a PS5 (firmware 13.60) reads mode 2 and submode 0, nothing else is written, and the result register holds no status, so the function is taken to return nothing. Whether a title process reads the same values is unverified. Null outputs throw
- Font, Http2, Net, Ssl, SystemService and libkernel exports imported by PPSA12544 and added without an implementation have unknown signatures
- [sceHttp2GetMemoryPoolStats, sceSslGetMemoryPoolStats, sceNetGetMemoryPoolStats](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNet/Export.cpp) (libSceHttp2, libSceSsl, libSceNet) - the signatures `(int id, stats*)`, the layouts (pool size, peak and current use, and for Http2 and Ssl a 32-bit reserved field) and the error codes were measured on a PS5 (firmware 13.60). The pool size is the one given at init or pool creation; nothing is allocated from these pools, so the peak and current use are 0, where the console already reports use right after initialization (0x15C0 bytes for Ssl, 0x1640 for Http2 and 4 for a Net pool)
- [sceNpWebApi2GetMemoryPoolStats](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNpWebApi2/Export.cpp) (libSceNpWebApi2) - imported by PPSA21703's Unity PSN plugin. The signature `(int libCtxId, stats*)` and the layout of the Http2 statistics above (measured on a PS5 for Http2, Ssl and Net) are assumed for this library, not measured. The pool size is the one given to `sceNpWebApi2Initialize`, and the peak and current use are 0. The error codes are unknown, so a null output or an unknown context throws
- [sceSslGetCaList, sceSslFreeCaList, sceSslUnloadCert](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSsl/Export.cpp) (libSceSsl) - signatures and the 16-byte `SceSslCaList` layout (an array of certificate handles and a 32-bit count) follow [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/5dfdb4b0b166e83dc9ae275001afb695f12c7174/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Net/Http.cs) and shadPS4's `OrbisSslCaList`, not confirmed for the PS5. On the console the list holds the certificate authorities the SSL service has; [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/pull/4104) returns a placeholder certificate from `sceSslGetCaCerts` because Rise of the Tomb Raider fails its initialization checks without one. No root certificate bundle is shipped and no loaded certificate is kept, so here a context holds none: `sceSslGetCaList` clears the list and returns `NOT_FOUND`, as `sceSslGetCaCerts` does, `sceSslFreeCaList` accepts only that cleared list, and `sceSslUnloadCert` (imported by PPSA15508) has nothing to drop and is assumed to return 0. A null list returns `0x8095177A`, which `sceSslGetCaCerts` and [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/59183dbfa81ed7efdff6069177fed50de0552725/src/libs/network.cpp) return for a null argument (shadPS4, PS4, returns `0x8095F007`). An unknown context throws (KytyPS5 returns `SSL_ERROR_INVALID_ID`, `0x8095F006`, from `sceSslGetCaCerts`), and so does a list `sceSslGetCaList` did not return
- [sceHttp2SetResolveRetry](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp2/Export.cpp) (libSceHttp2) - signature `(int id, int32_t retry)` from libSceHttp's `sceHttpSetResolveRetry`; the value is accepted and ignored like the other Http2 settings, since no request reaches the network
- [sceHttp2WebSocketCreateRequest, sceHttp2WebSocketCloseAsync, sceHttp2WebSocketSendTextMessageAsync, sceHttp2WebSocketSendDataMessageAsync](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp2/Export.cpp) (libSceHttp2) - imported by PPSA16064 and not called during its start-up; unknown signatures
- The Share, NpTrophy2, NpEntitlementAccess and WebBrowserDialog exports imported by PPSA12544's Unity plugins and added without an implementation have unknown signatures
- [sceNetShowNetstat](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNet/Export.cpp) (libSceNet) - imported by PPSA11386 and not called during its start-up; unknown signature, declared without parameters
- [sceAjmDecWVorbisCreateHeaderPacket, sceAjmDecWVorbisCreateSetupPacket](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/Export.cpp) (libSceAjmWVorbis, implemented in libSceAjm.native) - unknown signatures
- Acm, GameLiveStreaming, Net, NpEntitlementAccess, NpManager, NpWebApi2, VoiceQoS, WebBrowserDialog and libkernel exports imported by PPSA14632 and added without an implementation are declared without parameters
- [libSceAudioPropagation](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioPropagation/Export.cpp) (libSceAudioPropagation) - signatures and struct descriptors recovered from PPSA21567's calls (#481), not from documentation. The system options layout is unknown (only checked for non-null); the fourth argument of `sceAudioPropagationSourceCalculateAudioPaths` is unknown; `sceAudioPropagationSourceGetAudioPath` is assumed to output an 8-byte path handle. The meaning of the `RenderInfo` word at +0x28 is unknown (PPSA21567 and PPSA21564 pass 2); only 2 is accepted. Both titles pass one `RenderInfo` per call: a count of 0 throws, and larger counts are handled element by element without proof. No error code is known, so invalid input throws instead of returning one
- [NGS2 mastering voice gain](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Voice.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native) - the param id 0x30000004 is taken from PPSA22520 and the layout from [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4)'s `OrbisNgs2MasteringVoiceGainParam`; no reference gives the units or range. The levels are assumed to be linear factors applied when the mastering voice is mixed into the render buffer, so the voice's own samples stay unscaled; the LFE level applies to channel 3 of 6- and 8-channel voices, assuming the L R C LFE order of the AudioOut2 entries above, and the full-band level to the rest. Negative levels are accepted, and a new setup resets both levels to 1
- [NGS2 stereo mastering voice into a surround buffer](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Render.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native) - PPSA21402 renders a 2-channel mastering voice into a 6- or 8-channel render buffer, which the mixing stage rejected. The console's conversion is unknown: the left and right channels are assumed to go to the first two buffer channels (front left and right) and the remaining channels stay silent, with no centre, LFE or surround content. Any other channel count mismatch still throws
- [NGS2 silent waveform blocks](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Voice.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native): flag `0x10` (named `SILENCE` here; no public reference names it) lets a waveform block declare samples without data. PPSA04867 adds one block with flags `0x11`, `data_size` 0 and `num_samples` 16385, then another with flags `0x11` and a fully specified 0x800-byte, 1024-sample mono block, which takes the ordinary path. A dataless block under the flag outputs `num_samples` frames of silence, reports them through `decoded_data_size`, and finishes like a complete block; a dataless block without the flag still throws as not fitting its data. The flag's real name, its interaction with ATRAC9 and repeats, and the console's `waveform_data` for silent blocks are unverified on hardware
- [NGS2 streaming PCM waveforms](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Voice.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native) - PPSA21402 streams its music by adding one block whose `num_samples` is the whole track and whose `data_size` is a 16 KiB first piece, with `CONTINUE`, and then adds each following piece with flag `0x2` (called `APPEND` here) and the same `num_samples`. No reference describes flag `0x2`. A 16-bit PCM block without skipped samples or repeats whose `num_samples` exceed its data is assumed to start such a stream; flag `0x2` is assumed to append the block's data to the last streaming block, ignoring its `num_samples`; the voice is assumed to output silence without advancing while it has played all appended data and `CONTINUE` is set; and the waveform is assumed to end at `num_samples` or, once `CONTINUE` is cleared, at the end of the appended data. `sceNgs2VoiceGetState` reports the bytes played as `decoded_data_size`, which the title uses to release its queued data, and the read position as `waveform_data`. Appending to a block that is not a stream, or other formats, skipped samples, repeats and partial frames, throw
- [NGS2 matrix levels](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Voice.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native) - levels are clamped to -4..4 and NaN is kept, as the PS4 firmware 11.00 `libSceNgs2.sprx` stores them; not yet confirmed on the PS5 library. That library rejects a level count of 0 and keeps the matrix; the PS5 error code is unknown, so a count of 0 throws. `sceNgs2VoiceRunCommands` is not in that library, so its matrix levels command is assumed to clamp the same way
- [NGS2 sampler voice state flag EMPTY](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Render.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native) - a playing sampler voice that still accepts blocks (`CONTINUE`) and has played all queued blocks reports `INUSE|PLAYING|EMPTY` (0x20) and keeps playing, so blocks added later play on. PPSA18391 (engine: Clickteam Fusion) reads `state_flags & 0x22 != 2` from `sceNgs2VoiceGetState` as the end of a sound and only then reuses the voice. When the console sets and clears EMPTY, and whether it also reports it for other voice types, is unverified on hardware.
- [sceAvPlayerStartEx](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAvPlayer/Export.cpp) (libSceAvPlayer) - start info layout unknown; it is ignored and playback starts as with `sceAvPlayerStart`
- [sceAvPlayerInit / sceAvPlayerInitEx](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAvPlayer/src/Player.cpp) (libSceAvPlayer) - behaviour without a memory replacement unknown; frame and sample buffers then come from the guest heap
- [sceAvPlayerInitEx](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAvPlayer/Export.cpp) (libSceAvPlayer) - the PS5 init data layout (`this_size` 0x230: auto start at 0x74, nine 0x30-byte thread parameter blocks from 0x78, output video frame buffer count at 0x228) follows [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/main/src/libs/avPlayer.cpp), [prosper](https://github.com/mattias800/prosper/blob/main/prosper/src/hle/video/avplayer.cpp), [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Media/AvPlayer.cs) and [sharpemu](https://github.com/sharpemu/sharpemu/blob/main/src/SharpEmu.Libs/AvPlayer/AvPlayerExports.cs), which agree on it. PPSA25063 fills the same fields, writes auto start 0 and starts playback with `sceAvPlayerStart`; a title that sets auto start has not been seen. A smaller `this_size` is read with the PS4 offsets. The thread parameters are ignored
- [AvPlayer auto start](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAvPlayer/src/Player.cpp) (libSceAvPlayer) - the auto start that `sceAvPlayerAddSource` queues for a player with auto start or without an event callback starts the player only while it is still ready: a title that starts the player itself before the auto start runs keeps its playback and the frames it holds. Not confirmed on a title.
- [SceAvPlayerVideoEx](../../core/libs/SceTypes.hpp) (libSceAvPlayer) - frame rate field and encoding unknown; it is left zero in frame and stream info
- [sceRazorCpuFlushOccurred](../../core/libs/prx/libSceRazorCpu/Export.cpp) (libSceRazorCpu) - signature and return values (1 = flush since the last call, 0 = none) from [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero); without a capture nothing is flushed, so it returns 0 and writes 0 cycles to the optional out pointer (whether the real library writes it when nothing was flushed is unknown)
- [sceRazorCpuPushMarkerStatic, sceRazorCpuPopMarker](../../core/libs/prx/libSceRazorCpu/Export.cpp) (libSceRazorCpu) - signatures from PPSA26344's calls; (name, color, flags) assumed for the push; both do nothing without a capture
- [sceRazorCpuPlotValue, sceRazorCpuWriteBookmark](../../core/libs/prx/libSceRazorCpu/Export.cpp) (libSceRazorCpu) - signatures (series, float value) and (label, optional description) from [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero), matching PPSA06320's calls; both do nothing without a capture and return 0
- [sceAjmBatchJobGetInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp) (libSceAjm.native) - writes the result and format sideband of a run job without buffers with the `SIDEBAND_FORMAT` flag (0x400000000000), as [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5) does, not confirmed on a PS5 title; the format reports bitrate 0 for ATRAC9 as in KytyPS5, while [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4) derives it from the superframe size; before the instance is initialized it returns the not-initialized result (1) and no format, like the other run jobs here, while KytyPS5 returns result 0 with the format of the uninitialized decoder
- [sceAjmDecMp3ParseFrame](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp) (libSceAjm.native) - signature, result layout and rate tables taken from the [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4) reimplementation of the PS4 library, not confirmed on a PS5 title: the layer field is not checked and MPEG-2.5 stops at 64 kbps; the original file length (`parse_ofl`) layout (Xing/Info + LAME tag, VBRI, Fraunhofer `0xB4` block with its CRC, encoder delay = samples per frame + LAME delay + 529) also comes from shadPS4 only, and the Fraunhofer block has no other public description
- [NGS2 port matrix](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Render.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native) - a port without a matrix, or with a selected matrix whose levels were never set, mixes through a default channel map: mono reaches both stereo channels at -3 dB and the 5.1 and 7.1 centre, stereo folds to mono at -3 dB per side, and wider sources fold down with 0.5 and -3 dB gains. The map and the equivalence of an unset matrix with no matrix were measured from the PS4 firmware 11.00 `libSceNgs2.sprx`, not yet from the PS5 library. That library only accepts submixer and mastering voices with 1, 2, 6 or 8 channels, so the map into 3, 4, 5 or 7 channels throws. A matrix set with fewer levels than the destination needs still mixes only the rows it has
- [sceAjmBatchJobGetCodecInfo, sceAjmBatchJobGetGaplessDecode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp) (libSceAjm.native) - the `RUN_GET_CODEC_INFO` flag (0x800), the ATRAC9 codec info layout and its next frame size are taken from the [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4) and [RPCSX](https://github.com/RPCSX/rpcsx) reimplementations, not confirmed on a PS5 title; a run job without buffers returns result 0, as in shadPS4. The gapless sideband reports the total and skip counts the title set and does not count them down: RPCSX does the same, shadPS4 counts them down and BryKytyPS5 returns its current counts
- [AJM Opus decoder](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp) (libSceAjm.native, codec 24) - the initialize parameter layout and little-endian `u16` packet byte count come from The Smurfs Dreams (`2, 48000, 0`) and Lost Soul Aside (`4, 48000, 1`). The third word is interpreted as the mapping family, as in [KytyPS5's initialize structure](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/984cfcebd141b211aa9e0a8cde63b0ebe652d07a/src/libs/ajm/opus_decoder.h#L11-L15). Family 1 uses the conventional stream counts, coupled counts and channel map from [libopus 1.5.2's surround layout](https://github.com/xiph/opus/blob/v1.5.2/src/opus_multistream_encoder.c#L47-L55), passed to FFmpeg through an [RFC 7845 section 5.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc7845#section-5.1) header with zero pre-skip and gain; gapless skipping remains controlled by AJM sideband parameters. FFmpeg returns PCM in its native speaker order. The implicit stream layout, PCM channel order and invalid family-0 channel count result are not verified on a console. Only 48000 Hz and mapping families 0 and 1 are implemented; other rates and families throw
- [sceAjmBatchJobSetResampleParametersEx](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp) (libSceAjm.native) - the `(info, instance, float ratio_start, float ratio_change_per_sample, flags, result)` signature is inferred from the name and from `sceAjmBatchJobSetResampleParameters`; with a zero change per sample it sets the ratio as `sceAjmBatchJobSetResampleParameters` does (Watch Dogs: Legion, PPSA01500, calls it so). How a non-zero change ramps the ratio is unknown and throws
- [sceAjmBatchJobControl](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp) (libSceAjm.native) - RESET (bit 13), INITIALIZE (bit 14) and the sideband input order (gapless decode, then initialize parameters) come from [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4) (PS4); other flags throw
- [sceAjmBatchJobControl](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp) (libSceAjm.native) - RESET is assumed to clear the context as sceAjmBatchJobClearContext does
- [sceAjmBatchJobControl](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp) (libSceAjm.native) - initialize parameters are 8 bytes for ATRAC9, 12 for Opus and none for MP3, as one title passes them; other codecs and sizes, and an output other than the 8-byte result, throw
- [sceAjmBatchJobControl](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp) (libSceAjm.native) - SIDEBAND_GAPLESS_DECODE with RESET overwrites both counts as in [RPCSX](https://github.com/RPCSX/rpcsx); shadPS4 ignores a zero count when the other is nonzero. Without RESET they differ, so it throws
- [NGS2 custom submixer rack](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Custom.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native, rack 0x4002) - structure layouts from [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4)'s `ngs2_custom.h`; module id 0x1f for UserFx2, the voice parameter id `0x40001f00 | module index`, the process flags (1 on the first process call after setup, 2 after a parameter change, 0 otherwise) and the call order (setup per voice at rack creation, process after the inputs are mixed, cleanup at destruction) come only from [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5). Only UserFx2 modules on a single buffer are accepted: other module ids, more buffers, a module control handler, different input and output channel counts, setup flags and default rack options throw. Each module's state is a separate buffer, not the `state_offset` slice of the voice state, and `sceNgs2VoiceGetState` still throws for this rack. As in KytyPS5, process runs only on grains where an input produced samples, so an effect tail stops with the input and a pending flag 2 waits for the next audible grain
- [NGS2 reverb rack](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Reverb.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native, rack 0x2001) - follows the PS4 firmware 11.00 `libSceNgs2.sprx`, read and checked against its output at 11.025 to 48 kHz, including parameter changes during playback (differences at float rounding level); not yet compared with the PS5 library. Structure layouts come from [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4)'s `ngs2_reverb.h` and the param ids 0x20010000 (setup) and 0x20010001 (I3DL2) from PPSA22520. Only the default `reverb_size` of 1 is handled, with its 14400-sample reflection buffer; other sizes and the library's half-rate mode throw. System sample rates below about 7.5 kHz, where an allpass delay would have no samples, throw. Setup only accepts equal input and output channel counts
- [sceHttpParseResponseHeader](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp/Export.cpp) (libSceHttp) - signature follows [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Net/Http.cs); parsing and error codes follow [shadPS4's reverse-engineered PS4 library](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/main/src/core/libraries/network/http.cpp), not confirmed on a PS5 title or hardware. Folded values retain their line breaks and whitespace; an empty first line starts the value scan after its line feed. A consumed byte count above `INT_MAX` throws instead of wrapping.
- [sceHttpParseStatusLine](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp/src/Parse.cpp) (libSceHttp) - behaviour and error codes follow the [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4) reimplementation of the PS4 library, not confirmed on a PS5 title; unlike it, no byte past `lineLen` is read
- [sceHttpUriMerge](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp/src/Uri.cpp) (libSceHttp) - signature follows [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Net/Http.cs); validation, size formula, absolute/relative split and path join follow the disassembly-like body in [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/main/src/core/libraries/network/http.cpp), not confirmed on a PS5 title or hardware. The base URL goes through our `sceHttpUriParse`, which removes dot segments from the base path, while the relative part is appended unswept; base query and fragment are dropped. The relative reference is only classified by its scheme and a leading `//`, and only references with `//` are validated; as in shadPS4, `//host/x` is returned unchanged instead of taking the base scheme, a reference with a scheme but no `//` (`mailto:x`) is joined onto the base directory, and `?q` and `#f` are appended to the base directory instead of the base path.
- [sceHttpUriUnescape](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp/src/Uri.cpp) (libSceHttp) - signature follows [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Net/Http.cs); percent decoding, invalid sequences and error codes follow [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/main/src/core/libraries/network/http.cpp), not confirmed on a PS5 title or hardware. Size includes the final NUL; malformed escapes and `+` remain unchanged, and decoding runs once.
- [sceHttpUriSweepPath](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp/src/Uri.cpp) (libSceHttp) - behaviour follows the [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/main/src/core/libraries/network/http.cpp) reimplementation of the PS4 library and its tests (including the PS4 SDK example), not confirmed on a PS5 title or hardware. It is not RFC 3986 5.2.4: a path that doesn't start with `/` is copied unchanged and a trailing `.` or `..` without a slash is kept. Unlike shadPS4, nothing at or past `srcSize - 1` is read, and `srcSize == 1` writes only the terminator
- [sceHttpSetResponseHeaderMaxSize, sceHttpRedirectCacheFlush, sceHttpsUnloadCert](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp/Export.cpp) (libSceHttp) - signatures follow [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/main/src/core/libraries/network/http.cpp) (PS4); no PS5 reference has them
- [sceNpSetTimeout](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNpManager/Export.cpp) (libSceNpManager) - the signature follows [orbital](https://github.com/AlexAltea/orbital/blob/b321fcdbd700cc02da1cb1ad25412781d5ec73c6/tools/ida/db_types.json), [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/0fe263a4760dfbfa973366890061749b4af0de97/src/core/libraries/np/np_manager.cpp) and [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/5dfdb4b0b166e83dc9ae275001afb695f12c7174/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Np/NpAccount.cs); the rejected values (all five 0, negative retries, a resolve timeout under 1 s or another timeout under 10 s, 0 meaning the default) are shadPS4's, not checked on a console. An id that was never created or was deleted returns `REQUEST_NOT_FOUND`, as in shadPS4; there is no network, so the timeouts are not stored
- [sceHttpSetRequestStatusCallback](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp/Export.cpp) - the three-argument ABI follows [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/main/src/core/libraries/network/http.cpp). Registrations and user data are retained and removed on request deletion; the offline backend never dispatches callbacks. Invalid ids return `0x80431100`, an uncited assumption, not a verified console error. Handle hierarchy, inheritance and deleted-handle validation remain incomplete; callback statuses and dispatch timing are unknown.
- [sceHttpSetRedirectCallback, sceHttpSetCookieRecvCallback, sceHttpsSetSslVersion, sceHttpsGetSslError](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp/Export.cpp) (libSceHttp) - signatures follow [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/main/src/core/libraries/network/http.cpp) (PS4), which only logs and returns 0; a null `errNum` or `detail` in `sceHttpsGetSslError` is assumed to return `SCE_HTTP_ERROR_INVALID_VALUE` like `sceHttpGetLastErrno`
- [sceHttpSetAuthInfoCallback](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp/Export.cpp) (libSceHttp) - signature and callback type follow [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/main/src/core/libraries/network/http.h) (PS4); no PS5 reference has them. The `id` is not checked, as in the other libSceHttp setters
- [libSceHttp2 cookie boxes](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp2/Export.cpp) (libSceHttp2) - `sceHttp2CreateCookieBox` is taken to take the library context id first, as in the [prosper](https://github.com/mattias800/prosper) reimplementation; further arguments are unknown and ignored. `sceHttp2SetRequestNoContentLength` is assumed to take only the request id. Cookies are never stored, so `sceHttp2CookieFlush` has nothing to discard
- [sceUserServiceGetUserName](../../core/libs/prx/libSceUserService/Export.cpp) (libSceUserService) - `0x80960105`, which a PS5 (firmware 13.60) returns for a user id it does not know, has no known name; it is `USER_SERVICE_ERROR_UNKNOWN_USER` here. On the console a registered user who is not logged in still gets their name; here only the initial user exists
- [sceNpSessionSignalingGetMemoryInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNpSessionSignaling/Export.cpp) (libSceNpSessionSignaling) - unknown signature
- [sceNpSessionSignalingGetConnectionStatistics](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNpSessionSignaling/Export.cpp) (libSceNpSessionSignaling) - unknown signature
- [sceUserServiceGetAgeLevel](../../core/libs/prx/libSceUserService/Export.cpp) (libSceUserService) - on a PS5 (firmware 13.60) the value is the age set in the user's parental restriction (0, 7, 12 and 15 were measured) and `0xFFFFFFFF` without one; a user who is not logged in returns `0x80960009` with or without an output. Parental restrictions are not modelled, so the initial user is always reported as unrestricted. PPSA12544 (Unity) only stores the value for its user profile
- [sceUserServiceGetAccessibilityChatTranscription, PressAndHoldDelay, TriggerEffect, Vibration, ZoomEnabled, ZoomFollowFocus](../../core/libs/prx/libSceUserService/Export.cpp) (libSceUserService) - report 0, 0, 1, 1, 0 and 1, what a PS5 (firmware 13.60) reports for a user whose accessibility settings are all at their defaults; the console reports the user's own settings (a changed vibration intensity read 3), which are not modelled.
