What each number means is unknown. A user who is not logged in returns `0x80960009` with or without an output
- [sceGameUpdateGetAddcontLatestVersion](../../core/libs/prx/libSceGameUpdate/Export.cpp) (libSceGameUpdate) - a null `entitlement_label` throws: KytyPS5 accepts it, prosper returns `0x80412803`. Bytes after `found` are zeroed as in KytyPS5; prosper leaves them untouched
- [sceSystemServiceParamGetString](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSystemService/Export.cpp) (libSceSystemService) - the system name (parameter 6) is the fixed `PS5`, not the console's name; other parameters and buffers of 1 to 64 bytes throw
- [sceSystemServiceParamGetInt](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSystemService/Export.cpp) (libSceSystemService) - console language numbers 0 to 29 follow the PS4 SDK enum in [OpenOrbis](https://github.com/OpenOrbis/OpenOrbis-PS4-Toolchain/blob/06fcf7fd108fa080035cdd3259ed22eb07e0508c/include/orbis/_types/sys_service.h#L54-L83) (21 Arabic). 30 (Ukrainian) is not in it and follows PPSA99505 ([Jelly5](https://github.com/02dnot/Jelly5/blob/552b866d89b2a58b27f7f7b0484dc743ce235526/app/src/app/i18n.cpp#L29-L60)) and PPSA99002 ([ProsperoLight](https://github.com/blackbearreloaded/ProsperoLight/blob/b708bbde3457bef1073a9ab6959957cb691e8e2f/include/i18n.hpp#L252-L309)), whose numbers agree with the enum. Not confirmed on a console, and neither title starts on `main` yet. Parameter 400 returns the same number as parameter 1: PPSA01325 asks for 400 first, asks for 1 when that fails, and uses either answer as an index into the same 31 languages; in the PS5 10.01 and 12.60 `libSceSystemService` modules parameter 400 reads the same registry value as parameter 1, without parameter 1's mapping of newer language numbers by SDK version, and returns 0x80a10003 when an imported check that is not identified returns 1; the 4.03 module has no parameter 400. Neither the mapping nor that error is modelled, and the parameter's name is not known. Without `ANYPS5_LANGUAGE` the language is English (United States); the date format, time format, time zone, summer time and enter button stay fixed
- [sceNgs2ReportRegisterHandler, sceNgs2ReportUnregisterHandler](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Unimplemented.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native, NIDs `uBIN24Tv2MI`, `nPzb7Ly-VjE`) are throwing stubs. The signatures (report type, handler, user data, out handle) and (report handle) follow [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero) and [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4)'s `ngs2_report.h`. No report is produced yet, so registering a handler cannot be honoured. PPSA04540 imports both and registers a CPU load handler (type 5)
- [sceNgs2SystemSetSampleRate, sceNgs2SystemSetUserData, sceNgs2SystemGetUserData, sceNgs2SystemLock, sceNgs2SystemUnlock](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/System.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native) - shadPS4 only checks the system handle (`INVALID_SYSTEM_HANDLE`) and SharpProspero names what each call does; neither gives the other limits. A sample rate of 0 and a null user data pointer throw, any other rate is accepted, and changing the rate while a sampler filter is enabled throws because the filter coefficients were computed for the old rate. Lock and unlock take the same lock as `sceNgs2RackLock`
- [sceNgs2RackGetInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Rack.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native) - field order from [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4)'s `OrbisNgs2RackInfo` with the 64-byte name of the other info structures (216 bytes); `type` is assumed to be the rack id divided by 0x1000, `min_grain_samples` 64 (the system minimum) and `state_flags` 1; `uid` comes from a per-process rack counter, `render_count` is the owning system's render count and `active_voice_count` counts voices that are not empty (paused and stopped voices included). The last process ratio and tick and `active_channel_work_count` are left zero
- [sceNgs2VoiceQueryInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Voice.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native) - only info id 0x4001 is known: PPSA22520 passes an 8-byte buffer and branches on the first word being 1 or 2, so it is assumed to be the voice channel count (0 before the voice is set up); the second word is zeroed and other ids throw
- [sceNgs2VoiceGetPortInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Voice.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native) - layout from shadPS4; the delay and destination input are zero because ports have neither yet
- [sceNgs2ParseWaveformFile](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Atrac9.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native) - the signature `(path, offset, info)` is taken from the call site of PPSA21402, which sets only three argument registers; the file is read from `offset` to its end, the data and block offsets are taken to be relative to the start of the file, not of `offset`, and an unreadable file throws. The audio unit fields of a 16-bit PCM waveform are assumed to be one sample frame per unit and per frame; `INVALID_WAVEFORM_DATA` for a `data` chunk that runs past the buffer or ends inside a frame is assumed, and looped PCM and other PCM formats throw
- [sceSaveDataDirNameSearch](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSaveData.native/Export.cpp) (libSceSaveData.native) - the sort keys (0 directory name, 1 user parameter, 2 blocks, 3 modification time, 5 free blocks) and orders (0 ascending, 1 descending) follow shadPS4, not a hardware run; saves with equal keys keep directory-name order before a descending reverse. Every native save reports `SAVE_DATA_BLOCKS_MAX` blocks, as `sceSaveDataGetMountInfo` does, so the two block keys keep directory-name order. The search infos (blocks, free blocks and 32 reserved bytes per returned name, following shadPS4's `OrbisSaveDataSearchInfo`) report `SAVE_DATA_BLOCKS_MAX` for both. Keys other than 0, 1, 2, 3 and 5 (including 4) and orders above 1 return `SCE_SAVE_DATA_ERROR_PARAMETER`; shadPS4 and KytyPS5 only reject keys above 5, so key 4 is rejected here because no source names it
- [sceSaveDataInitialize3](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSaveData.native/Export.cpp) (libSceSaveData.native) - a repeated initialize succeeds: PPSA12544's executable and its Unity SaveData plugin both initialize, and the plugin fails on any error. Initializations are counted and `sceSaveDataTerminate` ends the session at the last one; how the console pairs them is unknown
- [sceTextToSpeech2GetSystemStatus](../../core/libs/prx/libSceTextToSpeech2/Export.cpp) (libSceTextToSpeech2) - unknown signature; returns `EOPNOTSUPP` like `sceTextToSpeech2Initialize`
- [sceCoredumpAttachUserMemoryFile](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Coredump.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown signature
- [sceCoredumpAttachMemoryRegion](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Coredump.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown signature
- [sceKernelAddTimerEvent](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Equeue/Equeue.cpp) (libkernel) - `usec` is taken as the period in microseconds; re-adding an existing timer keeps its pending expiration count and restarts the period from the time of the call, where FreeBSD 12+ `filt_timertouch` clears pending expirations on `EV_ADD`; `usec == 0` fires on every wait. None of this is confirmed on a PS5 title
- [sceKernelWaitEqueue](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Equeue/Equeue.cpp) (libkernel) - a level-triggered user event (`sceKernelAddUserEvent`) is reported at most once per call, as in FreeBSD `kqueue_scan` (`sys/kern/kern_event.c`), which re-queues a reported knote without `EV_CLEAR` or `EV_DISPATCH` behind the scan marker, and kevent(2), where several triggers do not produce several kevents. Not measured on a console. Events are scanned in list order and the list is not rotated, so when `num` is smaller than the number of ready level events the later ones can starve, where FreeBSD moves a reported level knote to the tail. Several completions of one AMPR or VideoOut event within one call are returned as separate entries, where FreeBSD would aggregate them; the Orbis behaviour is unconfirmed
- [sceRtcFormatRFC2822, sceRtcFormatRFC2822LocalTime, sceRtcFormatRFC3339LocalTime](../../core/libs/prx/libSceRtc/Export.cpp) (libSceRtc) - only shadPS4 (PS4) has bodies. A null tick returns `INVALID_POINTER` like the existing `sceRtcFormatRFC3339`, where shadPS4 formats the current time instead; offsets outside ±1439 minutes return `INVALID_VALUE` like `sceRtcFormatRFC3339`; the local-time variants use the host's offset at the given instant (shadPS4 uses `sceKernelGettimezone`, the current offset)
- [snwprintf_s](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/FormattingWide.cpp) (libc) - follows C11 K.3.9.1.3 with `RSIZE_MAX` assumed to be `SIZE_MAX >> 1`. A runtime-constraint violation only returns a negative value: `set_constraint_handler_s` is not exported and the console's default handler is unknown. An invalid multibyte `%s` argument is copied byte by byte instead of being reported as an encoding error, as in `vswprintf`
- [vsnprintf_s and vsscanf_s](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Formatting.cpp) (libc) - `vsnprintf_s` forwards to `vsnprintf` semantics: the C11 K.3.7.1.5 runtime constraints (null buffer, `size == 0`, `%n`, null `%s`) are not checked, so `size == 0` returns the would-be length instead of a negative value, and no constraint handler is invoked. `vsscanf_s` returns the count of items converted before an undersized `%s`/`%c`/`%[` destination (0 when it is the first), not EOF. Neither is confirmed on a PS5 title
- [libSceUlt ulthreads](../../core/libs/prx/libSceUlt/Export.cpp) - `_sceUltUlthreadRuntimeOptParamInitialize`, `_sceUltUlthreadRuntimeCreate` and `_sceUltUlthreadCreate` are assumed to take the same arguments as the existing exports without the underscore, including the trailing build version. `sceUltUlthreadTryJoin` is assumed to return `ULT_ERROR_BUSY` while the ulthread runs, and `sceUltUlthreadRuntimeDestroy` to return `ULT_ERROR_BUSY` while an ulthread created on the runtime is not joined. Neither error has been confirmed on PS5 hardware
- [libSceUlt queues](../../core/libs/prx/libSceUlt/Export.cpp) - the added `sceUltQueuePop`, `sceUltQueueTryPush`, `sceUltQueueDestroy` and `sceUltQueueDataResourcePoolDestroy` signatures are inferred from the existing push/pop and object APIs. Queue destruction is assumed to reject blocked callers with `ULT_ERROR_BUSY` and otherwise discard queued data; pool exhaustion returns `ULT_ERROR_AGAIN`. These rules, alignment checks and shared slot limits have not been confirmed on PS5 hardware or a title. Non-null queue and data-pool option parameters throw because their layouts are unknown.
- [localeconv](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/LocaleSupport.cpp) (libc, re-exported by libSceAmpr) - the 0x90-byte Dinkumware struct (empty monetary strings, `CHAR_MAX` for the 14 `char` fields, `decimal_point` ".", `_False`/`_True` "false"/"true") is read from the `libc.prx` that Astro's Playroom ships. It is a fixed C-locale table: whether the console changes it with `setlocale` is not known, and `_Frac_grouping`, `_Frac_sep`, `_No`, `_Yes` are empty strings as in that dump
- [set_constraint_handler_s](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/WideConversion.cpp) (libc, re-exported by libSceAmpr) - the default handler is assumed to abort; each module keeps its own handler state, so a handler set through one library is not seen by the other. `ctime_s` and `asctime_s` follow the Dinkumware 26-byte format with `EINVAL` for a buffer under 26 bytes, a null pointer or an out-of-range weekday/month; `ctime_s` uses the guest time zone of `localtime_s`, and the console's exact checks are not verified
- [__udivti3](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/RuntimeSupport.cpp) (libc, re-exported by libSceAmpr) - the hardware traps on a zero divisor; this throws instead
- [AC1FtjqMCL0](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [0iEKNAvT600](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [2cELEBPdYQ0](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [5xYxiOtOccA](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [6W+shIH315Q](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [7ilObq815O4](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [7iq7Hfs8HBk](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [AMJZhpC+siY](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [E9XkHowt+dY](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [GADjreszOgM](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [Giyg2xGrXDU](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [KC3y21wrqzc](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [Qb8wS4GZUsw](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [aAQZSjb2fho](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [pnz21I6VjDs](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [qk043QPsrZU](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [uDGXwUKqi8s](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown name, signature
- [sceKernelGetCpuTemperature](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Unimplemented.cpp) (libkernel) - imported by PPSA99902, which fails to load without it; added without an implementation because its behavior is unverified.
- [LinuxProtFromSce](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/DirectMemory.cpp) (libkernel) - protection bits 0x100 and 0x200 are accepted and assumed not to affect host CPU access; host protection is derived only from the read, write and execute bits
- [_sceUlobjmgrRegisterObject](../../core/libs/prx/ulobjmgr/Export.cpp) (ulobjmgr) - parameters taken from shadPS4 (PS4): a nonzero 64-bit object, a nonzero 32-bit kind and a 32-bit id output; the meaning of the first two and the PS5 signature are unverified. The id bound of `_sceUlobjmgrUnregisterObject` (below 0x4000) and the raw `EINVAL` (22) return also come from shadPS4
- [sceShareGetCurrentStatus](../../core/libs/prx/libSceShare/Export.cpp) (libSceShare) - validation (feature flag non-zero, status non-null) and the all-zero 16-byte status taken from the [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5) reimplementation, not confirmed on a PS5 title; the meaning of the recording status values is unknown
- [sceShareGetRunningStatus](../../core/libs/prx/libSceShare/Export.cpp) (libSceShare) - only the first 32-bit word of the status is known: PPSA01317 treats bits 0x3c of it as a running share feature and gives the call a 40-byte buffer. The host never records or broadcasts, so it stores 0 in that word and leaves the rest untouched; a null status returns `INVALID_PARAM` by analogy with `sceShareGetCurrentStatus`
- [sceShareSetContentParamForApplicationTitle](../../core/libs/prx/libSceShare/Export.cpp) (libSceShare) - imported by PPSA12544's Unity plugins; the signature follows [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/main/src/libs/libShare.cpp), not confirmed on a PS5 title. The application title is only checked for null, as KytyPS5 does, since nothing is captured
- [sceSharePlayInitialize](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSharePlay/Export.cpp) (libSceSharePlay) - the library keeps no state, so the heap is not used. A null heap is accepted: PPSA11386 passes a null heap and 0x1800 bytes and stops on any non-zero result. The error for a zero size is unknown, so it throws
- [sceAgcDcbSetZPassPredicationEnableGetSize, sceAgcDcbSetPredicationDisableGetSize, sceAgcDcbSetBoolPredicationEnableGetSize](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/DcbState/src/Predication.cpp) (libSceAgc) - the builders are inline in the SDK and not exported; the size is assumed to be that of the SET_PREDICATION packet `sceAgcDcbSetPredication` writes (16 bytes)
- [Resource registration](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Resource/src/Registration.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - registration always fails with `RESOURCE_REGISTRATION_UNAVAILABLE`, so the owner and resource queries, `sceAgcDriverSetResourceUserData`, `sceAgcDriverFindResourcesPublic` and `sceAgcDriverUnregisterAllResourcesForOwner` return the same error without reading their arguments. Their parameter lists follow the libSceGnmDriver counterparts and are unverified. `sceAgcDriverGetResourceRegistrationMaxNameLength` writes 0xfc and returns 0, as [prosper](https://github.com/mattias800/prosper) does from a title's registration configuration; the `uint32_t*` parameter matches the SDK wrapper `sce::Agc::ResourceRegistration::getMaxNameLength(unsigned int*)`. sharpemu writes 256 and KytyPS5 returns `RESOURCE_REGISTRATION_UNAVAILABLE`, so the value is unverified; a null output throws
- [vieBRwlh1Lw](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Shader/src/InterpolantMapping.cpp) (libSceAgc) follows [OpenAGC](https://github.com/OpenAGC/OpenAGC/blob/3c7e8072f0d7684e930454794fd5c02056946d73/src/game_compat.c) only, not confirmed on hardware: the `dbOlWdppb4o` transform without the identity tail, so it writes one register per pixel shader input and none for a pixel shader without inputs, and a null pixel shader throws. For an `is_f16` 1 or 3 input whose `default_value` and `default_value_hi` differ it throws: OpenAGC takes `DEFAULT_VAL` from `default_value_hi`, while KytyPS5, sharpemu and `dbOlWdppb4o` here take it from `default_value`
- [sceAgcDriverRegisterMultipleResources](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Resource/src/Registration.cpp) uses the existing unavailable-registration state and leaves caller memory untouched. Its four arguments (uint32 handle output, uint32 owner, descriptor pointer, uint32 count) follow the original PPSA34502 / 01.010.000 codzm call. The caller builds 40-byte descriptors and explicitly accepts 0x8A6C9018 with an invalid output handle. Enabled registration and console argument-validation precedence are unverified.
- [Shader registration](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Shaders/ShaderRegistry.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a shader header is accepted at any address: PPSA26344 passes headers 4 and 1 bytes past an 8-byte boundary; whether the console refuses an alignment is unknown
- [NGG vertex workgroups](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Shaders/ShaderRegistry.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - type-2 PRIMGEN programs using DS instructions or `s_barrier` are prepared with the draw's routing and primitive registers on the existing mesh path. The workgroup requirement follows [Mesa's NGG lowering](https://github.com/mihawk-99/PS5_Mesa/blob/b3588f78dd7fd46fbadc7ecd487e5f14d4213e4b/src/amd/common/nir/ac_nir_lower_ngg.c#L1135); the mesh path supplies LDS and two guest lanes per host invocation for wave64 on a 32-lane host subgroup. Console routing has not been measured. Passthrough and tessellation keep their existing paths; unsupported mesh assembly and subgroup budgets throw.
- [Shader header copies](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Shaders/ShaderRegistry.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a header bound for a draw or dispatch at another address than the registered one is accepted when its fixed fields match the registered header except `user_data`; the registered header's metadata is used. PPSA21567 binds such a copy of a vertex shader header with `user_data` set to null. Whether the console reads the copy's `user_data` is unknown
- [HtileFillCovers](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/DepthSurface.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a fill is taken to cover a depth surface's whole HTILE when it spans 32 KiB per 1024x512 pixel block from DB_HTILE_DATA_BASE, the pipe-aligned SW_64KB_Z_X metadata extent AMD PAL's gfx10 addrlib gives for GB_ADDR_CONFIG 0x00100044 (1-8 samples, D16/D32); other metadata layouts are not decoded. A partial fill is ignored (the surface keeps its contents); not measured on the console.
- [Depth fast clear through HTILE](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/DepthSurface.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a surface cleared by an HTILE fill is cleared to DB_DEPTH_CLEAR, also when it is next sampled. For a depth-only HTILE the clear word also carries a value (radv writes `minz = maxz = round(depth * 0x3fff)`, `HTILE_Z_CLEAR_REG` in Mesa's `src/amd/common/ac_descriptors.h`, and allows TC-compatible fast clears only to 0.0 or 1.0); which of the two the texture unit returns for a cleared tile was not measured.
- [Depth-only and stencil HTILE fill](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/DepthSurface.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - in a whole-HTILE fill, ZMask (bits 0-3) 0 clears the depth and SMem (bits 8-9) 0 clears the stencil, while SMem 3 leaves the stencil alone (`HtileFillClears`); other SMem values are taken to leave it alone too. The bit meanings follow the gfx10 HTILE layout as the code reads it, not a measurement; `agc_driver_depth_fast_clear_submit` pins them for the paths the Driver takes.
- [HTILE address of a depth array slice](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - `decodeDepth` adds the `DB_DEPTH_VIEW` slice offset to the Z and stencil addresses but not to `htileAddress`, so every slice of a layered depth target shares slice 0's HTILE address and a fill of slice 0's HTILE marks all slices cleared. Astro's Playroom fills the HTILE of each of its three shadow cascades 0x40000 apart (0x24000 bytes of tiles each) back to back before drawing, so the result is still right there; a title that clears one slice between draws to another would lose depth. The per-slice HTILE stride is not derived yet.
- [sceNgs2VoiceControl sampler filter](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Voice.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native) - param `0x1000000a`, its layout and meaning follow KytyPS5 only: location 1 type 1 is an RBJ low-pass biquad at the system sample rate after resampling, scaled by `level`, a set `channel_mask` bit bypasses that channel, type 0 turns the filter off. Other locations and types throw. The voice keeps playing a filter tail after its last block until the history is zero, with outputs below 1e-20 flushed to zero (a KytyPS5 choice); the console's tail length is unknown
- [sceNgs2PanInit / sceNgs2PanGetVolumeMatrix](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Pan.cpp) (libSceNgs2.native) - follow the PS4 firmware 11.00 `libSceNgs2.sprx`, read and checked against its output for 2, 4, 5 and 7 speakers into 1, 2, 6 and 8 channels; not yet confirmed on the PS5 library. Speakers are taken in the order L R, L R Ls Rs, L R C Ls Rs or L R C Ls Rs Lb Rb and kept in a fixed circular order, not sorted; without angles the defaults are 90/270, 40/110/250/320, 0/40/110/250/320 and 0/40/110/150/210/250/320 degrees. As in that library, defaults converted to radians keep the wrap-around entry in degrees, so angles past the last speaker pan almost fully to it. Error codes are not known for the PS5, so invalid arguments throw, including a speaker angle equal to the unit angle, on which that library loops forever. Angles so large that adding or subtracting a turn leaves them unchanged throw for the same reason
- [sceRtcGetDosTime, sceRtcSetDosTime](../../core/libs/prx/libSceRtc/Export.cpp) (libSceRtc) - bit layout from shadPS4 (PS4, the standard FAT date/time). A year outside 1980-2107 makes `GetDosTime` return `INVALID_YEAR` after writing 0 for an earlier one and `0xff9fbf7d` (2107-12-31 23:59:58, the largest encodable time) for a later one, and `SetDosTime` decodes the year as the unsigned 7-bit FAT field (1980-2107) without validating the fields, as the disassembly-derived [fpPS4](https://github.com/red-prig/fpPS4) reimplementation of the PS4 library does; not confirmed on a PS5 title, and shadPS4 instead masks out-of-range years into the packed value and sign-extends the 16-bit date
- [sce::Json::InitParameter2, InitParameterRtti and InitParameterRtti2](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJson2/Export.cpp) (libSceJson2) - layouts inferred, not known. `InitParameter2` is taken as the 40-byte object of [SharpEmu](https://github.com/sharpemu/sharpemu/blob/1811cef94435db7655209fb39f65ddf258b93fd6/src/SharpEmu.Libs/Json/JsonExports.cs) and [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/eda6ba37ece127ab0396378404d99182e3e8a530/src/libs/libJson2.cpp): the allocator, its user data, the file buffer size, the 32-bit special float format type at 0x18 and 12 bytes both zero, and both constructors zero all of it. `InitParameterRtti2::setAllocatorRtti` writes the allocation parameters and user data where `setAllocator` writes the allocator, as both references do; whether the console also marks the object as using RTTI allocation is unknown. `InitParameterRtti`'s constructor writes the allocation parameters, user data and file buffer size to the first 24 bytes, an order inferred from its mangled arguments. The values of `SpecialFloatFormatType` and what they select are unknown, so `setSpecialFloatFormatType` stores any value and nothing reads it: `Initializer::initialize(InitParameter2)` ignores every field as `initialize(InitParameter)` does, serializing a non-finite real throws whatever the type, and whether the type lets the parser accept NaN or infinity is unknown. The setters return nothing; whether the console's return `this` is unknown
- [sceAjmBatchJobSetResampleParameters, sceAjmBatchJobGetResampleInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp) (libSceAjm) - the ratio is taken as decoded samples per output sample and num_samples as the decoded samples the resampler holds, both inferred from FMOD (Hades II); the console's interpolation is unknown, a 4-point Catmull-Rom is used. The flags argument is ignored, as in shadPS4 and Kyty
- [scePlayerReviewDialogOpen, scePlayerReviewDialogGetResult](../../core/libs/prx/libSceCdlgPlayerReview/Export.cpp) (libSceCdlgPlayerReview) - the PS5-only dialog has no public reference: the parameter is not read and the result is assumed to start with the 32-bit common dialog result, as `sceWebBrowserDialogGetResult` assumes
- [sceHttpWaitRequest](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp/Export.cpp) (libSceHttp) - the timeout is assumed to be in microseconds and a negative timeout to wait without limit; the invalid-value error for a null handle or event array or a non-positive event count is assumed, not measured. No request is ever pending, so a wait returns 0 events when its timeout passes
- [sceVideoOutAddPreVblankStartEvent](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVideoOut/src/Event.cpp) (libSceVideoOut) - how long before the vblank the console signals pre-vblank start is unknown. The event is triggered on each emulated vblank tick immediately before the vblank event, with its own count as the event data
- [sched_get_priority_max, sched_get_priority_min](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) - measured on a PS5 (firmware 13.60): `SCHED_FIFO` (1) and `SCHED_RR` (3) report a maximum of 767 and a minimum of 256, and every other policy, `SCHED_OTHER` (2) included, returns -1 with `EINVAL`. `pthread_setschedparam` ignores the policy and does not check the priority, where the console rejects `SCHED_OTHER` and priorities below 256 with `EINVAL`

- [inet_pton, inet_ntop](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/AddressConversion.cpp) (libkernel) - the text forms are assumed to be those of FreeBSD 11.4 and are unverified on a console: `inet_pton` accepts only four decimal parts without leading zeros for `AF_INET` and the RFC 4291 forms for `AF_INET6` (one `::` standing for at least one group, a dotted-quad tail only as the last 32 bits, no zone id), and `inet_ntop` prints lowercase hex with the first longest run of two or more zero groups compressed, and a dotted tail only for `::a.b.c.d` and `::ffff:a.b.c.d`. They no longer depend on the host resolver library
- [gethostbyname](../../core/libs/prx/libScePosixForWebKit/Resolver.cpp) (libScePosixForWebKit) - numeric IPv4 forms, trailing whitespace and 64-bit accumulation follow FreeBSD 11.4 [inet_aton](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/releng/11.4/lib/libc/inet/inet_addr.c); clearing the resolver error on numeric success follows [fakeaddr](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/releng/11.4/lib/libc/net/gethostnamadr.c). Exact PS5 behavior is unverified; hostname lookup still uses the host resolver

- [sceAppContentGetAddcontDownloadProgress](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAppContent/Export.cpp) (libSceAppContent) - imported by PPSA08755, which fails to load without it; added without an implementation because its behavior is unverified. [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero) lists `(uint32_t serviceLabel, entitlementLabel, SceAppContentAddcontDownloadProgress*)`, but no PS5 reference gives the progress layout or the error codes

- [_sceFiberGetThreadFramePointerAddress](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFiber/Export.cpp) (libSceFiber, NID `0dy4JtMUcMQ`) forwards to `sceFiberGetThreadFramePointerAddress`; that the argument list matches is inferred from the export without the underscore, as for the underscore Ult exports, not measured
- [sceAgcAcbWaitUntilSafeForRendering](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Acb/src/Control.cpp) (libSceAgc) - `(buffer, videoOutHandle, displayBufferIndex)`, the declared arguments, which PPSA12544 (01.000.010) passes inside `waitUntilSafeForRendering` and `waitUntilSafeForRendering VR` markers on an asynchronous compute command buffer, ignoring the result. It writes the same rendering-wait packet as `sceAgcDcbWaitUntilSafeForRendering`, which the driver now accepts on compute queues too; the wait blocks only that queue's worker. Whether the console encodes the compute variant differently is not known

### Functional

- [Reused depth storage](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderResources.cpp): a storage view with another extent or a format that cannot hold the cached depth plane uses guest memory. Depth rendering is not written back to those guest bytes; this admission rule does not prove allocation reuse or preserve a depth writer through an incompatible view. Matching depth/stencil storage access still throws.

- [sceAudioInHqOpen, sceAudioInAsyncOpen](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioIn/Export.cpp) (libSceAudioIn) open the same port as `sceAudioInOpen`, so `sceAudioInInput`, `sceAudioInGetSilentState` and `sceAudioInClose` accept their handles. The signatures agree across [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/fc5d2cc219c43c8c8f354153e7b3d0c04a5d506e/src/core/libraries/audio/audioin.cpp), [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/60977b29103452b9681ae10c7a9011d44965bf16/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Audio/AudioIn.cs) and [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/c86c8b4d9333a766cc8c4495f89f893bf35e6199/src/libs/audio.cpp). HqOpen is limited to 128 samples at 48 kHz; AsyncOpen rejects sizes above 384 and throws for currently unverified inputs. Host capture pacing and console behavior remain unverified.

- [sem_timedwait](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/Posix/Sem.cpp) (libkernel) consumes an available count before checking the timeout and waits indefinitely for a null timeout, following FreeBSD 11's `sem_timedwait`. Console behavior for null and invalid timeouts when a count is available is unverified.
- [MaskedSelectEliminator](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/MaskedSelectEliminator.cpp) can discard inactive source lanes through `ReadFirstLane` only when its read mask implies the write mask and its result is unobserved outside the read mask. A loop-local read mask requires result uses in the same block without phi transport. The empty-EXEC lane-zero fallback follows AMD's [Vega ISA, section 12.8, V_READFIRSTLANE_B32, page 127](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/vega-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) and the existing SPIR-V emitter. The optimization is covered by synthetic IR tests but has not been compared with console hardware; proofs stop at the existing work and nesting limits.

- [getc_unlocked, putc_unlocked](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/StandardStreams.cpp) (libc) - byte I/O uses the host unlocked operations; guest EOF/error flags and macro counters follow `FileStream`. Linux status queries use `feof_unlocked`/`ferror_unlocked` because [glibc `feof`](https://github.com/bminor/glibc/blob/d2097651cc57834dbfcaa102ddfacae0d86cfb66/libio/feof.c) and [`ferror`](https://github.com/bminor/glibc/blob/d2097651cc57834dbfcaa102ddfacae0d86cfb66/libio/ferror.c) can acquire the stream lock. Windows uses [the CRT no-lock byte operations](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cpp/c-runtime-library/reference/fgetc-nolock-fgetwc-nolock) and `feof`/`ferror`, verified under a held stream lock on UCRT. Console behavior and other host CRTs remain unverified.

- [ftrylockfile](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/RuntimeSupport.cpp) (libc) - tries the host CRT stream lock that `flockfile`, `funlockfile` and each locking host stdio call on the stream take, so it returns -1 while another thread holds the stream through `flockfile` or is inside such a call, and 0 when it acquires the lock or already owns it (recursively, released by as many `funlockfile` calls), as FreeBSD 11 [`_ftrylockfile`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/lib/libc/stdio/_flock_stub.c#L89-L104) does. A libc export holds the lock only for each host call it makes, not across several host calls or while it updates the guest `FILE` prefix. Linux uses glibc `ftrylockfile`. UCRT has no try variant of `_lock_file`, so Windows calls `TryEnterCriticalSection` on the `CRITICAL_SECTION` that `_lock_file` enters, found through the private UCRT stream layout (`__crt_stdio_stream_data` in `ucrt/inc/corecrt_internal_stdio.h`, unchanged from Windows SDK 10.0.19041 to 10.0.26100); another CRT or a change of that layout breaks it. A null or closed stream throws where FreeBSD dereferences it

- [libSceOpusDec](../../core/libs/prx/libSceOpusDec/Export.cpp) uses FFmpeg for 48000 Hz mono/stereo signed-16 decoding. The six signatures, 640-byte opaque state allocation and byte-count return follow [public PS5 decoder research](https://github.com/blackbearreloaded/ps5-hardware-audio-decoding/blob/26be5a12f92b2cdc67f8a9e082fd69ae0e9a35a2/docs/DECODING.md#direct-opus-abi) and [ProsperoRadio](https://github.com/blackbearreloaded/ProsperoRadio/blob/2c4029c3a8f43e9e1dfef98374d94d99b14d745d/src/opus_decoder.cpp). Console PCM equivalence and error codes are unverified. Other sample rates, packet loss concealment, invalid inputs and termination with live decoders throw. Context identifiers and host decoder state are private registries; the firmware's opaque state layout and error recovery are unknown.
- [mkstemp and isatty](../../core/libs/prx/libScePosixForWebKit/Filesystem.cpp) (libScePosixForWebKit) follow FreeBSD's [temporary-file API](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/stable/9/lib/libc/stdio/mktemp.c) and [terminal check](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/stable/9/lib/libc/gen/isatty.c), without console verification. `mkstemp` replaces all trailing `X` characters, accepts a fixed name, and creates files through the guest path resolver with exclusive read/write access and mode 0600. Windows file permissions follow the existing kernel file implementation. `isatty` tests Windows console handles rather than all character devices; files, pipes and guest sockets return `ENOTTY`, closed descriptors return `EBADF`.
- [arc4random](../../core/libs/prx/libScePosixForWebKit/Export.cpp) (libScePosixForWebKit) returns four bytes of host OS entropy (`getentropy`, `rand_s` on Windows), as `arc4random_buf` does, uniform over the 32-bit range like FreeBSD 11.4's RC4 keystream in [arc4random.c](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/lib/libc/gen/arc4random.c). The console's generator and its reseeding are unknown; `arc4random_uniform`, `arc4random_stir` and `arc4random_addrandom` are not in the module's export set and are absent
- [fnmatch](../../core/libs/prx/libScePosixForWebKit/Export.cpp) (libScePosixForWebKit) follows FreeBSD 11 [fnmatch.c](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/stable/11/lib/libc/gen/fnmatch.c) in the C locale: each byte is one character, `FNM_CASEFOLD` folds ASCII letters only, and bracket ranges compare byte values. `[:class:]` names are not character classes: as in FreeBSD 11 through 14, `[[:digit:]]` is the set of `[`, `:`, `d`, `i`, `g`, `t` followed by a literal `]` (classes were added to `fnmatch.c` after 14). Multibyte characters and locale collation are not modelled. A null pattern or string throws
- [Dinkumware thread exports](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAmpr/Export.cpp) (libSceAmpr) follow the Dinkumware threads library shipped with [Visual C++ 2013](https://github.com/adityaramesh/concurrency_runtime/tree/master/VS2013_SP2/crt/src/thr) (`cthread.c`) and its `atomic.c`, [microsoft/STL `cthread.cpp`](https://github.com/microsoft/STL/blob/main/stl/src/cthread.cpp) and C11 Annex K, without console verification. `_Thrd_t` is taken to be the guest `pthread_t`, as the `std::_Pad::_Launch(pthread**)` export suggests: `_Thrd_join` returns `_Thrd_error` (4) when `pthread_join` fails and stores the thread's return value truncated to `int`. `_Thrd_join` (32-bit store of the result, 0 or 4) and the spin lock (byte exchange loop, no yield) match that disassembly. The shared_ptr spin lock is one process-wide flag that yields while it spins. `wcstombs_s` is `wcsrtombs_s` with a fresh conversion state, so `*retval` excludes the terminating null as in Annex K, not as in the Microsoft CRT, and `_TRUNCATE` has no meaning.
- [_Cnd_signal, _Cnd_init_with_name](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAmpr/Export.cpp) (libSceAmpr) - take a pointer to the handle slot like the libc `_Cnd_*` exports from [#3079](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/pull/3079) and act on the same conditions. `_Cnd_signal` wakes one waiter and returns `_Thrd_success` (0), as `_Cnd_broadcast` does; whether the console maps a failing internal signal to `_Thrd_error` is not known. The signature `_Cnd_init_with_name(_Cnd_t*, const char* name)` is inferred from [fpPS4 `ps4_mtx_internal.pas`](https://github.com/red-prig/fpPS4/blob/trunk/src/libcinternal/ps4_mtx_internal.pas), where the PS4 `_Mtx_init` is `_Mtx_init_with_name` with the name `SceLibcI_Mtx` and the name only reaches the pthread object; here it is `_Cnd_init` and the name, which may be null, is ignored. Neither is confirmed on a PS5 `libc.prx`. `_Thrd_id` still throws: microsoft/STL returns an `unsigned int` thread id, but whether the PS5 returns the `pthread_t` or a kernel thread id, and at what width, is not known
- [libSceZlib](../../core/libs/prx/libSceZlib/Export.cpp) completes RFC1950 decompression during submission and supports one outstanding request, null-timeout waits and one result retrieval. Timed waits, multiple outstanding requests, repeated retrieval and finalization with an unread result throw. The 64-bit initialization length and 64 KiB output limit with 2 KiB destination alignment follow [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/5dfdb4b0b166e83dc9ae275001afb695f12c7174/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Compression/Zlib.cs); the PS4 initialization length is 32 bits. shadPS4 also requires the output length to be a multiple of 2 KiB and returns `0x81120016` for invalid arguments; this implementation accepts any length up to 64 KiB (SharpProspero documents only the pointer alignment) and throws on invalid arguments. Scratch memory is not used, and its firmware size/alignment requirements are unknown. The initialization errors follow SharpProspero; malformed/checksum/truncated streams report `0x811200FF` and insufficient output `0x8112001C` as in [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/0fe263a4760dfbfa973366890061749b4af0de97/src/core/libraries/zlib/zlib.cpp). PS5 error precedence, scheduling, completion selection, result consumption and cancellation are unverified. The payload SDK's FreeBSD kernel `z_stream` and host errno are not this library's ABI.
- [Windows file timestamps](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/include/WindowsFileTime.hpp) use the host filesystem's timestamp range and resolution. `utimes` and `futimes` reject timestamps outside nonnegative signed 64-bit Windows file-time ticks with `EINVAL`, including exactly 1601-01-01 00:00:00 UTC, whose zero value means "leave unchanged" to `SetFileTime`. Guest directory descriptors still resolve through their recorded path. Filesystems with coarser timestamp resolution cannot retain every guest microsecond
- [fchdir](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel_sys/Export.cpp) (libkernel_sys) changes the guest working directory to the descriptor's directory: on Windows through the path recorded when the descriptor was opened, on Linux through the `/proc/self/fd` link target. It returns `EBADF` for a descriptor that is not open and `ENOTDIR` for one that is not a directory. FreeBSD `change_dir`'s search-permission check (`EACCES`) is not modelled, and a directory renamed after it was opened is still reached through the recorded path. Console behaviour is unverified
- [openat](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel_sys/Export.cpp) (libkernel_sys) passes `AT_FDCWD` (-100) and absolute paths to `open`. Any other relative path is joined to the descriptor's host directory, found the same way as for `fchdir`, so guest path aliases and case-insensitive name matching do not apply to it, and a `..` component is not kept inside the guest root. It returns `EBADF` for a descriptor that is not open and `ENOTDIR` for one that is not a directory. As with `open`, `O_NOFOLLOW` and `O_NONBLOCK` are ignored. PPSA99003 opens directory entries this way. Console behaviour is unverified
- [readlink](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel_sys/Export.cpp) (libkernel_sys) on Windows reads a symbolic link's target from its reparse data and returns it with `/` separators, only when the target is relative, as `symlink` creates them. A symbolic link with an absolute target and a junction throw, as a host path has no guest form. Any other reparse point throws too, where FreeBSD reports `EINVAL` for a file that is not a link
- [Windows file identity](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/NativeStat.cpp) (libkernel) - `stat` and `fstat` report the volume serial number as `st_dev` and the 64-bit file index folded into the guest's 32-bit `st_ino` (never 0), so two different files can in principle share an `st_ino`; the fold is not taken from a reference. `st_nlink` is clamped to 65535, the width of the guest field. The call keeps the `_wstat64` values when `GetFileInformationByHandle` fails
- On Linux, [direct memory](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/DirectMemory.cpp) lives in one sparse memfd at file offset = physical address, so the [GPU imports](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestBufferMemory.cpp) can share one udmabuf buffer object per 64 MiB of it. Released direct memory is not punched out of the file, because a live import of its chunk pins the pages and the GPU would keep reading the old ones: its pages stay committed (the process keeps the high-water mark of the direct memory it used) and are zeroed when the range is allocated again.
- [Per-face depth bias](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Draw.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver): Vulkan has one depth bias per draw, so a draw that rasterizes both faces with different front and back bias (PA_SU_SC_MODE_CNTL, PA_SU_POLY_OFFSET_FRONT/BACK_*) is recorded twice, front faces with the back faces culled and then back faces with the front faces culled. The vertex stages run twice, so a vertex shader that writes memory writes twice, and all front faces land before all back faces instead of in primitive order, which differs only for blending or stencil updates that depend on the order.
- [Partial rect-list primitives](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Draw.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver): a rect-list draw whose vertex or index count is not a multiple of 3 draws its complete rectangles and leaves out the 1 or 2 vertices after them, because rect lists are drawn as Vulkan patch lists of 3 control points, which generate [⌊vertexCount/3⌋ patches](https://registry.khronos.org/vulkan/specs/latest/html/vkspec.html#drawing-patch-lists). What the console draws for the incomplete rectangle is not measured; Palworld (PPSA23126) draws 4 vertices × 9 instances as a rect list when its character creation opens.
- [Separate alpha blend into 8_8 SWAP_ALT targets](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Pipeline.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver): the second channel of a two-channel target with `COMP_SWAP` 1 holds the alpha export, but Vulkan blends it with the color equation of the R8G8 attachment, so a draw whose alpha equation differs from its color equation is recorded twice, the first channel with the color equation and the second with the alpha factors and operation moved into the color equation. The vertex stages run twice, so a vertex shader that writes memory writes twice, and occlusion counts include both passes. Draws that write depth or stencil, pixel shaders that write memory and factors of the destination alpha throw. That the hardware blends the second channel with the alpha equation is taken from Mesa's `si_translate_colorswap` and radeonsi's use of it for luminance-alpha formats, not measured on hardware.
- [Unexported pixel inputs](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderInputState.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver): a pixel input whose `SPI_PS_INPUT_CNTL` parameter the previous stage does not export is programmed as a default-value input (`OFFSET` bit 5, `DEFAULT_VAL` 0, FP16 high half too) and reads (0, 0, 0, 0), as radeonsi's `SI_PS_INPUT_CNTL_UNUSED` does and as rect-list draws already do. The console's value for an unwritten parameter cache entry is not measured on hardware.
- [Compute LDS beyond the device's shared-memory limit](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) lives in a coherent storage buffer with one slice of `LDS bytes / 4 + 1` dwords per workgroup (the last dword is the atomic lock), shared by all such dispatches and grown in 1 MiB steps up to `maxStorageBufferRange`; a dispatch whose slices do not fit throws. It is slower than shared memory and its cost was not measured. An indirect dispatch of such a program always reads its group counts on the CPU. Its results are pinned against the in-limit semantics in `agc_driver_lds_beyond_device_limit`, not measured on hardware.
- [AGC buffer allocations](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/BufferPool.cpp) retry host/device out-of-memory once after reclaiming idle pool buffers and fully unused slab blocks. Live buffers and blocks with live slots are retained. Live images and other retained resources have no global memory bound; allocation failure still throws when reclaim cannot satisfy the request.
- [Graphics pipeline libraries](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/PipelineLibrary.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver): a pipeline is fast-linked at the draw and relinked with `VK_PIPELINE_CREATE_LINK_TIME_OPTIMIZATION_BIT_EXT` on a background thread, and binds the optimized one from the next draw on. Draws recorded before that use the fast link, whose vertex stage keeps the outputs the fragment stage does not read: on RADV (Radeon 660M, Mesa 26.2.4) 346 of 483 Astro's Playroom vertex shaders paired with a fragment stage that reads none of them used more VGPRs (median 8 more, up to 2.5 times), and on an RTX 4090 13 of 15 PPSA36082 vertex shaders up to 68 instead of 38 (#2242). The optimized link compiles to the same code as a whole pipeline
- [Shader modules built at registration](../../core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp) (shader recompiler): on devices with graphics pipeline libraries a vertex or pixel module reads buffer V# words, image modes, swizzles and mip ranges, depth-compare state, export mappings and vertex component selectors at run time instead of specializing on them per draw, so its libraries can be built when the shader is registered. A module that would exceed `SpirvTarget::registrationWordLimit` (200000 SPIR-V words) is emitted specialized as before: on RADV (Radeon 660M, Mesa 26.2.4) a sample of 60 Astro's Playroom pixel shaders compiled 1.9 to 2.2 times slower in registration form below 141k words and 3.8 to 5.7 times slower above 280k words (none in between), so the limit sits in that gap; it was not measured on other drivers. Texel-offset folding, the format word of a buffer accessed with the descriptor's format (`buffer_load_format_*`; as runtime values every format's decoder is compiled, which made CI's lavapipe JIT ten times slower) and the vertex attribute numeric class stay specialized, so shaders with the first two, and pixel shaders whose image needs a multisampled descriptor, get no registration result; these, and every draw whose fixed-function state differs from the prebuild's, still build their libraries at the draw. Compute and the other stages stay specialized: in registration form the `agc_driver_dword_pattern_fill` compute shader hung CI's lavapipe (Mesa 25.2.8) when built with `ANYPS5_ENABLE_SPIRV_TOOLS=ON`, not on RADV, and the cause was not found.
- [Prebuilt pipeline libraries](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver): each registered vertex or pixel shader in registration form gets its pre-rasterization or fragment-shader library built on one background thread, with a fixed state the draw may not use: triangle list, counter-clockwise front face, no depth clamp, depth bias or conservative rasterization, first provoking vertex, one sample. Libraries are kept until the device is torn down; on Astro's Playroom 1948 of them added 0.44 GB of peak memory on RADV.
- [sceAvPlayerGetVideoData](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAvPlayer/src/Source.cpp) (libSceAvPlayer) in sync mode `SCE_AVPLAYER_AV_SYNC_MODE_NONE` skips queued frames that the audio clock has already passed while the title pulls audio, so a title that requests video slower than the film's frame rate keeps the picture with the sound. Whether the console hands out every frame in this mode is unknown. PPSA12544 requests 7-19 frames per second for a 30 fps cinematic
- [`APS5_THREAD_PRIORITY=rt`](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/ThreadPriority.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) lowers the process's hard `RLIMIT_RTTIME` when it is above `APS5_THREAD_RTTIME_US` or rtkit's `RTTimeUSecMax`, because [rtkit](https://github.com/heftig/rtkit/blob/c295fa849f52b487be6433e69e08b46251950399/rtkit-daemon.c#L592-L637) (`verify_process_rttime`) reads the hard limit from `/proc/<pid>/limits` and refuses real-time scheduling when it is higher. An unprivileged process cannot raise the hard limit again, so it stays lowered for the process and its threads even when rtkit then refuses the request (burst limit, user or start-time checks) and the thread throws. rtkit's `MaxRealtimePriority` and `RTTimeUSecMax` are read first, so an unreachable rtkit or a priority above its maximum leaves the limit untouched. The lowered limit only matters for threads that later run under a real-time policy
- On a device without VK_EXT_robustness2's `nullDescriptor`, such as MoltenVK, a null image descriptor in a typed heap is a [placeholder image](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/PlaceholderImages.cpp) of that heap's type, 1×1 and cleared to zero, which every null descriptor of the heap shares. Reads give zeros, as `nullDescriptor` allows, but stores and atomics to it are kept, so a later read of a null storage image can return them, and a depth comparison compares against depth 0. A heap whose placeholder the device cannot create, for example 1D or 3D depth, 64-bit atomics without 64-bit image atomics, or multisampled storage without `shaderStorageImageMultisample`, throws when a null descriptor reaches it.
- [libSceHttp2 asynchronous requests](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp2/Export.cpp) (libSceHttp2) never reach the network: `sceHttp2SendRequestAsync` accepts the request, queues a completion whose result is the library's network error and triggers the user event named by the kqueue option (equeue at offset 0, event id at 8, user data at 16, layout inferred from PPSA17221); `sceHttp2WaitAsync` returns that completion. The completion's `event_type` value is unknown and left 0. PPSA17221 completes its request object only after `sceHttp2SendRequestAsync` returns, and its handler for a synchronous failure calls `shared_from_this` before that, so a send must not fail synchronously. A kqueue option or user event that is missing, an extra option, and a wait with no completed operation throw
- [sceHttpAbortWaitRequest, sceHttpWaitRequest](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp/Export.cpp) (libSceHttp) - the abort takes only the epoll handle, as in [Orbital](https://github.com/AlexAltea/orbital/blob/master/tools/ida/db_types.json) and [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Net/Http.cs); behaviour and error codes follow [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/main/src/core/libraries/network/http.cpp) (PS4), not confirmed on a PS5 title or hardware. An abort stays set: every later wait on the handle, a poll included, returns `SCE_HTTP_ERROR_ABORTED` (0x80431080) until the handle is destroyed. A wait woken by `sceHttpDestroyEpoll`, or by the `sceHttpTerm` that ends the last context, returns 0 events. The epoll calls return `BEFORE_INIT` (0x80431001) while no `sceHttpInit` context is alive and `INVALID_ID` (0x80431100) for an unknown context or handle, and `sceHttpTerm` returns the same errors instead of succeeding for any id. Handles are pointers, so a stale handle whose address a newer epoll reuses is taken as that epoll
- [system](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) runs no command. It follows FreeBSD 11's `lib/libc/stdlib/system.c` for a sandbox without `/bin/sh`: a null command returns 1 unconditionally, as that version does, and any command returns the wait status of a child that could not exec the shell and called `_exit(127)` (`0x7f00`). Whether the console's application sandbox has no shell, and whether `vfork` succeeds there (FreeBSD returns -1 when it fails), is unverified
- [sceKernelWriteThrottlingStatus](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Stdio.cpp) (libkernel) reports no write throttling: the host SSD has no write budget, so the first qword of the 32-byte status is set to `UINT32_MAX` and the rest is zeroed. PPSA17221 reads only that qword: it multiplies it by 64 KiB and compares the product with byte thresholds up to 0x4AF00000 to pick one of five tiers. When the call fails the title assumes 0x4B000001 bytes, which selects the same top tier as `UINT32_MAX`. What the value measures on hardware (a remaining budget or a rate) and the meaning of the other three qwords are unknown
- [Windows directory descriptors](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/DirectoryDescriptor.cpp) (libkernel) take one snapshot of the directory, on the first `getdents`, `getdirentries` or non-zero `lseek` (`SEEK_SET` or `SEEK_CUR`), and keep it until it is discarded; `lseek` to offset 0 only discards the snapshot, and the next read lists the directory again, so every rewind costs O(entries) on the next read. `lseek` takes the offset as an entry index and `SEEK_CUR` with offset 0 only reports the index. `SEEK_END` throws, where the NUL descriptor used to return 0, because FreeBSD defines it relative to the directory size, which is not modelled. A negative result returns `EINVAL` and a `SEEK_CUR` offset that overflows returns `EOVERFLOW`, as FreeBSD `vn_seek` reports them. Names whose UTF-8 encoding exceeds the one-byte `d_namlen` limit are not representable in this guest directory-record layout: `getdents` returns `ENAMETOOLONG` without consuming the entry. Repeated calls therefore report the same error and entries after that name cannot be reached through this descriptor; FreeBSD's `getdents` behavior for host filenames that exceed its `MAXNAMLEN` is not verified. `getdirentries` still reports 0 through `basep` instead of the position of the block it read, so only 0 and values returned by `lseek` are valid offsets; FreeBSD offsets are filesystem cookies
- [/dev/random and /dev/urandom](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/RandomDevice.cpp) (libkernel) opened for reading return a descriptor backed by the host null device whose reads are filled with host OS entropy (`getentropy`, `rand_s` on Windows); both paths behave the same, never block, and positioned reads ignore the offset (FreeBSD `random(4)`). Only the exact paths are recognised, a `dup`ed descriptor reads as an empty file, `fstat`, `lseek`, `mmap` and `select` see the null device, and opening them for writing throws
- [Wide-character conversions](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Multibyte.cpp) (libc) - The wide `wcsto*` functions parse the ASCII prefix of the guest string, as the C locale accepts only ASCII white space and digits. [`wcsrtombs`](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Multibyte.cpp) uses the guest's single-byte C locale through `wcrtomb`; other encodings and shift states are not supported
- [realpath](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/RealPath.cpp) (libc) follows FreeBSD 11's [`realpath.c`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/stable/11/lib/libc/stdlib/realpath.c) except for symlinks. The console's guest filesystem has none, so a host symlink under the guest root is followed for existence and type but not expanded, and `..` drops the previous component lexically, as `Resolve` does for every guest path. Through a host link `linkdir -> sub/deep`, `linkdir/../x` gives `x` beside the link, not beside its target.
- [nan and nanf](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Math.cpp) (libc) return fixed quiet NaN values (`0x7fc00000` and `0x7ff8000000000000`), ignoring the tag argument as observed in the console's `libc.prx` (both exports load a global constant via `mov rax,[rip+..]; vmovsd xmm0,[rax]; ret`); VLC (PPSA85300) stops on nanf at start-up after strsep.
- [Draw input snapshots](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderResources.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver): `PrepareDrawBindings` caps read-only input buffer snapshots at 64 MiB (overridable via `APS5_DRAW_SNAPSHOT_MAX_MIB`). Larger inputs (streaming heaps, bindless pools) or draws exceeding the cumulative budget skip the per-draw snapshot and bind in place, avoiding multi-gigabyte CPU copy stalls (such as Ratchet & Clank: Rift Apart PPSA01474 stalling at frame ~380 copying 36 GB every 5 s). If the title rewrites those large ranges on the CPU before GPU completion, the in-place binding observes the modification, as verified by `drawSnapshotCapTests`.
- [pthread_attr_getinheritsched, pthread_attr_setinheritsched, pthread_attr_getscope, pthread_attr_setscope, pthread_attr_getstackaddr](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/Posix/Attr.cpp) (libkernel) follow FreeBSD 11 libthr ([thr_attr.c](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/5ed7eb0d97ba4436218e810f61bd059acba984c2/lib/libthr/thread/thr_attr.c), [thr_create.c](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/5ed7eb0d97ba4436218e810f61bd059acba984c2/lib/libthr/thread/thr_create.c)), not measured on the console: the scope defaults to `PTHREAD_SCOPE_SYSTEM` (2), only it and `PTHREAD_SCOPE_PROCESS` (0) are accepted and other values return `EINVAL`, and an inherit value other than `PTHREAD_INHERIT_SCHED` (4) or `PTHREAD_EXPLICIT_SCHED` (0) returns `ENOTSUP`. `pthread_attr_getstackaddr` returns the configured stack address pointer (null by default). A null output of `pthread_attr_getinheritsched` throws, since libthr writes through it unchecked. A thread keeps the scope it was created with, taken from the creating thread under `PTHREAD_INHERIT_SCHED`, and `pthread_attr_get_np` and `scePthreadAttrGet` report it, but every guest thread is a host thread at system scope, so process scope changes nothing
- [SPIRV-Tools optimization](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvOptimizer.cpp) skips CCP and Simplification for modules with storage-only 8- or 16-bit types because the pinned folder produces illegal narrow constants. This also disables unrelated constant folding; the restricted full pipeline mirrors the pinned performance recipe.
- [SRT signed comparison](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/SrtWalker/SrtEvaluator.cpp) evaluates `SLessThan32` as signed 32-bit less-than, matching the existing SPIR-V backend and [SPIR-V `OpSLessThan`](https://registry.khronos.org/SPIR-V/specs/unified1/SPIRV.html#OpSLessThan). Signed boundary descriptor expressions are tested on the host; native RDNA2/PS5 execution and title behavior remain unverified.
- On macOS the [write watch](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/GuestWriteWatch.cpp) write-protects watched pages and records the first write to each from a SIGBUS/SIGSEGV handler, because macOS has no counterpart to `userfaultfd` write-protect or `MEM_WRITE_WATCH`. The protection is real, so:
  - a system call that writes into a protected page fails with `EFAULT` instead of faulting. `GuestArena::HostWrite` lifts the protection for the calls it brackets (file reads and socket receives); a host call that writes guest memory without it (`clock_gettime` and the like into a watched page) fails;
  - code that changes the protection of watched pages, or maps over them, has to call `GuestWriteWatchProtectionChanged`, and code that asks the kernel for a page's protection has to ask `GuestWriteWatchProtection` first, since a watched page is read-only between a collection and the next write. Nothing calls either yet: the macOS memory paths are not in the tree;
  - the handler is installed when libc loads and passes other faults to the handler installed before it. A handler installed later has to do the same.
- [Pending storage-image results](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Texture.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) are stored when something reads their memory, not when the GPU work that wrote them completes. A title that waits for that work, frees the image and reuses its memory can have the new data overwritten by the late store (#1970). `APS5_STORE_AT_FLIP=1` stores every pending result at each flip; storing them at the title's GPU waits (labels, fences) is unimplemented.
- Windows host-pointer imports of the guest mapping itself are compared instead of write-watched: a driver can mark imported pages dirty after the synchronous import window. Shared memory imported through a separate writable alias retains protection-based tracking on the guest views; driver access to the import alias does not disarm those views. [GuestMemory](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/GuestMemory.cpp) excludes every overlapping 64 KiB tracker block while its backing is retained. A full block replaced by fresh private backing regains tracking and gets a new generation; protection changes, partial replacements and shared remappings do not restore it. This preserves CPU edits through byte comparisons but disables snapshot reuse and unit shadows for excluded blocks; restoring tracking on import retirement without replacing the backing and measuring the comparison cost remain unimplemented.
- [Storage-image units under a Linux import window](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Texture.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) that the window stamped as a possible CPU store (`BlockMaybeWritten`) are compared with the bytes the image was uploaded from or written back as, and are unchanged when those still match. A CPU store of the bytes already there within the window cannot be told apart from an import that resolves the write protection without storing, as the NVIDIA driver (610.57.04) does for every imported page, so such a store does not win over pending results. Images tracked by layer instead of by 64 KiB unit still read the window as a change and upload again.
- [Shared memory](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/SharedMemory.cpp) (libkernel) on Linux implements anonymous and named objects, bounded descriptor writes and seeks, and `shm_unlink` with effective read/write permission checks from FreeBSD 11. Named paths up to 1023 bytes use `/anyps5-shm-` plus SHA-1; collisions are not detected, and the namespace is shared across processes in the host mount namespace. Named creation applies the guest umask without changing the host mask. Linux procfs and `/dev/shm` are required. Operations use an advisory directory lock; external host operations bypass it, unrelated objects serialize, and descriptor operations require two spare host descriptors and compare device/inode with the initial descriptor identity on both the pinned-object and ordinary-file fallback paths. [Guest mappings](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/PosixMemory.cpp) support shared and private mappings of these descriptors through the allocation tracker and pin a duplicate descriptor during validation and mapping; mapping requires one spare host descriptor; file-backed mappings use the write-watch scan fallback because aliases and descriptor writes bypass per-mapping tracking. Named objects and file-backed mappings on Windows remain unsupported. Invalid non-null name pointers, console behaviour and title compatibility remain unverified.
- [sceKernelAddReadEvent and sceKernelAddWriteEvent](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Equeue/Equeue.cpp) (libkernel): a write event reports the socket's send buffer size (`SO_SNDBUF`) in `data`, not the free space left in it, as Windows has no query for that space. Only guest sockets are supported; other descriptors and a low watermark above 1 throw
- [sceNetSetsockopt](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNet/Export.cpp) (libSceNet) sets `SO_RCVTIMEO` and `SO_SNDTIMEO` as the host socket's timeouts. On Windows a receive or send on a stream socket that times out fails with `ETIMEDOUT`, where FreeBSD returns `EAGAIN` or the partial count: Winsock leaves the connection in an indeterminate state after a timeout and reports no partial count ([SOL_SOCKET options](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/winsock/sol-socket-socket-options)), so FreeBSD's behaviour needs the timeout waited for without the host option. A receive on a datagram socket that times out fails with `EAGAIN`
- [sceNetSendmsg and sceNetRecvmsg](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNet/Export.cpp) (libSceNet) copy the scatter/gather list through one host buffer and accept only the flags `sceNetSend`/`sceNetRecv` accept. Sending control data throws; a receive never returns control data (`msg_controllen` 0), since no socket option that queues it is supported
- [close](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Stdio.cpp) and [setsockopt](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Udp.cpp) (libkernel) reach a [libSceNet](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNet/Export.cpp) socket through hooks libSceNet registers, as `select` polls them; the other libkernel socket calls do not. libSceNet numbers its sockets from 32 in its own table, so a CRT file descriptor and a socket can share a number; `close` then closes the socket. On the console both are one kernel descriptor space
- [Additional content](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAppContent/Export.cpp) (libSceAppContent) is not installed or mounted: `sceAppContentAddcontMount` answers `NOT_FOUND` for every entitlement label and `sceAppContentAddcontUnmount` for every mount point, as no add-content mount is ever handed out; `sceAppContentGetAddcontInfo` answers `DRM_NO_ENTITLEMENT` for every label and `sceAppContentGetAddcontInfoList` reports 0 entries
- [Content export sessions](../../core/libs/prx/libSceContentExport/Export.cpp) (libSceContentExport): `sceContentExportStart` hands out positive session ids, at most 10 live, which `sceContentExportFinish` closes. The signatures follow [Orbital](https://github.com/AlexAltea/orbital/blob/cc408367da6a6ebbf5ac1ac559fefeadc73b2831/tools/ida/db_types.json) and the limit `MaxExecution` [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/5dfdb4b0b166e83dc9ae275001afb695f12c7174/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Content/ContentExport.cs); the parameter layouts follow [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/443f022450f6857256e780daa3d5a14029a748f7/src/core/libraries/content_export/content_export.h). The host has no media library, so `sceContentExportFromDataWithThumbnail` throws once the session is checked, before calling either data callback, and no export ever runs. `sceContentExportGetProgress` and `sceContentExportCancel` throw for a live session, as their result with no running export is unknown. An eleventh `sceContentExportStart` and `sceContentExportTerm` with live sessions throw: whether the console answers `EXECUTION_MAX` or `BUSY` there, or ends the sessions, is unverified. Session id values and error precedence are unverified
- [Runtime-loaded modules](../../core/relinker/relinker/src/guest/GuestModuleBuilder.cpp) (`--module-dir`) that another module needs are initialized at startup with no start arguments, before the module that needs them is loaded; the console is assumed to start them when that module is loaded.
- [sceKernelPread, sceKernelPwrite, sceKernelPreadv, sceKernelPwritev](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Stdio.cpp) (libkernel) on Windows transfer at the given offset with an overlapped `ReadFile`/`WriteFile` and restore the descriptor's file pointer afterwards, so a concurrent `read`/`write` on the same descriptor can see the moved pointer in between, unlike on the console. Handles that are not disk files return `ESPIPE`. `readv` on a pipe or console handle stops after the first iovec read, a short read the console is assumed to allow; each iovec is transferred separately, so a failure after the first transfer returns the bytes already done
- [sceKernelAioSubmitReadCommands, sceKernelAioSubmitWriteCommands and their Multiple variants](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Aio/src/Aio.cpp) (libkernel) on Windows transfer through the same positioned path as `sceKernelPread`/`sceKernelPwrite` and restore the descriptor's file pointer afterwards, so a concurrent `read`/`write` on the same descriptor can see the moved pointer in between, unlike on the console
- [sceKernelAioSetParam](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Aio/src/Aio.cpp) (libkernel) writes the five values into the 20-byte scheduling struct the first argument points to, following the declarations in [Orbital](https://github.com/AlexAltea/orbital/blob/cc408367da6a6ebbf5ac1ac559fefeadc73b2831/tools/ida/db_types.json), [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/5dfdb4b0b166e83dc9ae275001afb695f12c7174/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Kernel/KernelAio.cs) and [ampr_emu](https://github.com/drakmor/ampr_emu/blob/cfa85df379f6eeeb165d7badf9b648e266fe77b7/ps5/include/sce/_fs.h). Whether the console checks the values is unknown, so a null struct, a scheduling window or delayed count limit outside 0..128, a split flag other than 0 or 1 and a split size or chunk size above 0x1000000 (SharpProspero's maximums) throw. The scheduling settings are not used: `sceKernelAioInitializeImpl` ignores the struct and requests run synchronously on submit
- [sceKernelGetFsSandboxRandomWord](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Sandbox.cpp) (libkernel) returns a fixed directory name, `gkbHtXc3Wq`. On the console the name is the process's randomized path (syscall 602, `randomized_path`), and the sandbox exposes system files under it, such as `common/lib` and `common/cert`. Nothing is mounted under the name here, so `/gkbHtXc3Wq/common/...` resolves under the guest root like any other path and does not exist
- [sceKernelSync](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/General.cpp) (libkernel) flushes only the host files and directories recorded through `RecordWrittenPath_nid_no_patch` (the libc, libkernel, save data and app content file functions) and their changed parent directories. A host write in a library that does not record its path is not flushed by `sceKernelSync`
- [sceKernelSync](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/General.cpp) (libkernel) on Windows skips a recorded file it cannot open for writing (read-only, or held open by a handle that denies write sharing), since `FlushFileBuffers` needs a writable handle. On Linux, it skips a recorded file it cannot open for reading. Such a file's data may not reach the disk before a host crash; the guest reads the same data either way
- [`fopen` streams](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/FileAndHeap.cpp) (libc) on Windows share delete access, like the descriptors [`sceKernelOpen`](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Open.cpp) opens, so a file the guest holds open can be removed or renamed. A stream reopened with `freopen` still goes through `_wfreopen`, which does not share delete access
- [getcontext and setcontext](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/UserContext.cpp) (libkernel) and [makecontext](../../core/libs/prx/libSceLibcInternal/UserContext.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal) use FreeBSD 11's amd64 `ucontext_t` (`mcontext` at 0x10, `mc_len` 800, `uc_link` at 0x330, `uc_stack` at 0x338, 0x370 bytes), the layout homebrew built against FreeBSD's headers passes: PPSA99039's OpenSSL `ASYNC` (`crypto/async/arch/async_posix.c`) captures into a 0x370-byte stack buffer. `getcontext` writes the 0x330 bytes before `uc_link`, as FreeBSD's `UC_COPY_SIZE`. The console's own layout for these functions is not verified; the exception handler's context is a different one (see `sceKernelRaiseException`)
- [makecontext](../../core/libs/prx/libSceLibcInternal/UserContext.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal) follows FreeBSD 11's `lib/libc/amd64/gen/makecontext.c`: at most six arguments, a stack of at least `MINSIGSTKSZ` (2048) bytes, and `exit(0)` when the function returns with no `uc_link`. The console's version is not verified
- [getcontext and setcontext](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/UserContext.cpp) (libkernel) keep the 512-byte FXSAVE image in `mc_fpstate` (x87, MXCSR, XMM0-15), so the upper halves of YMM0-15 are neither saved nor restored, and `getcontext` leaves `mc_xfpustate` and `mc_xfpustate_len` 0. `getcontext` sets `mc_fpformat` and `mc_ownedfp` to FreeBSD's `_MC_FPFMT_XMM` and `_MC_FPOWNED_FPU`, `mc_cs` and `mc_ss` to the host selectors, and `mc_flags`, `mc_fsbase` and `mc_gsbase` to 0. `setcontext` loads `mc_fpstate` whatever `mc_fpformat` and `mc_ownedfp` say, and restores neither the segment selectors, `mc_fsbase` and `mc_gsbase` nor the extended state, which FreeBSD restores when `mc_flags` asks for them
- [makecontext](../../core/libs/prx/libSceLibcInternal/UserContext.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal) on Windows pins its stack writable until a later `makecontext` stack overlaps it, since nothing tells the library when the guest frees the stack
- [setcontext](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/UserContext.cpp) (libkernel) and [longjmp](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/JumpBuffer.cpp) (libc) on Windows set the thread's stack bounds in the TEB to the stack they switch to: a `makecontext` stack, or else the committed region around the target stack pointer. A switch to any other guest stack, such as a context built by hand, gets the bounds of that region only
- [sceKernelInstallExceptionHandler](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Exception.cpp) records the handler, but a host fault still ends the process: only signals raised with `sceKernelRaiseException` are delivered. Unity's PS5Util module installs a SIGUSR1 handler that reads `uc_mcontext.mc_rsp` (offset 0xf8 of the context) and raises SIGUSR1 on other threads to stop them for garbage collection
- The Windows libc exception unwinder reads the project's `.ehmeta` and `.ehfram` tables, not PE `.pdata`/`.xdata`. WinLibs GCC 15.2's `libstdc++-6.dll` and `libwinpthread-1.dll` have empty `.eh_frame` sections, so guest exceptions cannot currently cross their frames (for example, the `std::call_once` path through `pthread_once`); `RtlVirtualUnwind` stepping and Windows cleanup handlers are not implemented
- [sceKernelRaiseException](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Exception.cpp) on Linux sends the host SIGUSR1, which the library takes over, to the target thread, so the handler runs wherever the thread was interrupted, emulator code included. On the console kernel objects are system calls and libthr defers signals while a thread holds its internal locks; here a handler that needs a host lock the interrupted code holds deadlocks, and a thread that the handler keeps waiting keeps its host locks. Host waits resume after the handler. The host calls Linux does not restart end early: sleeps that do not retry return before their time, and the poll of `sceNetEpollWait` and socket calls with a send or receive timeout return `EINTR` to the guest
- [sceKernelRaiseException](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Exception.cpp) builds the handler's `ucontext` with FreeBSD's amd64 layout (16-byte sigmask, `mcontext` at 0x40, `mc_rsp` at 0xf8); the console's layout is not verified beyond the fields PS5Util reads
- [sceKernelRaiseException](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Exception.cpp) does not consult the target thread's signal mask
- [pthread_sigmask](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Signals.cpp) (libkernel) shares the process-wide mask of `sigprocmask` and `_sigprocmask`, which keeps only the first 32 signals; FreeBSD keeps a mask per thread
- [sceKernelRaiseException](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Exception.cpp) reaches a thread that waits in an alertable `TimedWait` wait through an APC. A thread blocked anywhere else, including all waits under `APS5_COARSE_TIMED_WAITS` and host waits inside the emulator, is redirected and runs the handler only when that wait returns
- [sceKernelRaiseException](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Exception.cpp) on Windows runs the handler wherever the target thread was interrupted. It waits only while the thread is inside winpthreads or restoring a context through `NtContinue`, and throws if that lasts 1 s, so a handler that calls into other host code the thread was interrupted in, such as the C runtime's heap or a lock of the emulator, can deadlock on it or reenter it
- [sceAgcDebugRaiseException](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Misc/src/Debug.cpp) (libSceAgc) - logs the exception id and returns 0, as retail hardware does nothing per [OpenAGC](https://github.com/OpenAGC/OpenAGC/blob/3c7e8072f0d7684e930454794fd5c02056946d73/src/game_compat.c); the trap (`ud2`) OpenAGC describes for development hardware is not reproduced. Neither is verified on a console. The `uint32` exception id is [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/6f0cddd4404ec5203e71fea77e75fc373299ba3f/src/libs/agc.cpp)'s claim; OpenAGC declares no arguments, so the logged id may be leftover register contents
- [sigaltstack](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Signals.cpp) (libkernel) records each thread's alternate signal stack and reports it back, following FreeBSD's `kern_sigaltstack` (`EINVAL` for flags other than `SS_DISABLE`, `ENOMEM` below `MINSIGSTKSZ` 2048), but handlers never run on it, so `SS_ONSTACK` and `EPERM` are never reported
- [libSceUlt queues](../../core/libs/prx/libSceUlt/Export.cpp) use preallocated host storage instead of the supplied guest work area; its existing size formula and the 512-byte object layouts remain unverified. Waiting-queue pool thread limits and waiter priority ordering are not implemented. Finalization wakes blocked queue callers with `ULT_ERROR_STATE`.
