- [scePthreadJoin, scePthreadCreate](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/src/Thread.cpp) (libkernel) - on a PS5 (firmware 13.60) joining a thread a second time returns `ESRCH` (`0x80020003`); here a joined thread is released, so a second join reads freed memory. A null entry crashed the payload and still throws. Joining the calling thread returns `EDEADLK` and a zeroed attribute creates a default thread, as on the console
- [Thread cancellation](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/src/Thread.cpp) (libkernel) follows FreeBSD libthr (states and types 0/1 and 0/2, `PTHREAD_CANCELED` 1, the `_pthread_cleanup_info` node layout), not measured on the console. A request is acted on only in `scePthreadTestcancel`, the condition variable, `sem` and `scePthreadSem` waits, `scePthreadJoin` and the sleeps, and their POSIX names; file and socket calls are not cancellation points. A sleep is not interrupted: the thread exits when it ends. The asynchronous type acts like the deferred one, except at the two set calls.
- [cpuset_setaffinity](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/Posix/Thread.cpp) (libkernel) - only the calling thread or the calling process is handled. Bits outside `sceKernelGetAvailableCpumask` are dropped, and a mask with none of those bits fails with `EDEADLK`, as FreeBSD does when the new set has no CPU in common with the parent set
- [cpuset_getaffinity](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/Posix/Thread.cpp) (libkernel) - only the calling thread or the calling process is handled. For the process the mask is the union of the affinities of the live guest threads, as [FreeBSD 11.4](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/d106002967eff712894ab8b5337bbbff106ce5e0/sys/kern/kern_cpuset.c#L1150-L1156) ORs the mask of every thread in the process; threads the guest did not create through libkernel are not included, and the console result is unverified
- [dup and dup2](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Stdio.cpp) (libkernel) throw for the combinations they do not model: `dup2` between a guest socket and a host descriptor in either direction, and on Windows duplicating a directory descriptor. Guest sockets share one socket object between the duplicates and the descriptor numbers come from the guest socket counter, not the lowest free number as in FreeBSD `kern_dup`. The errno values for descriptor exhaustion are not verified against the console.
- [Cancellation coverage](ARCHITECTURE.md#thread-cancellation) checks deferred request handling and recovery, not full asynchronous support or console ABI compatibility. Cancellation during file/socket I/O, sleep interruption, cancellation versus exit/detach, condition signal conservation under cancellation, and safe rejection of non-null invalid or reclaimed thread handles remain unverified. No console title or homebrew establishes the cleanup NIDs or buffer layout. Accepted asynchronous mode currently has no diagnostic for its deferred behavior.
- [MIMG `tfe` and `lwe`](../../core/shader/recompiler/RdnaDecoder/src/RdnaImageOpDecoder.cpp) never write the status VGPR after the data. On hardware it is written only when a texel fetch hits an unmapped page of a partially resident texture, or, for `lwe` on sample/gather, when the LOD is below the T# `MIN_LOD_WARN`; texture memory is always resident here, the LOD warning (texture-streaming feedback, like the T# `MIN_LOD_WARN` itself) is never reported, and `lwe` on `image_get_lod` throws.
- [Packed horizontal gathers](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) (`image_gather4h_pck`, MIMG opcode 98): the four window texels are read raw and concatenated into a little-endian bitstream, with DMASK bit i writing stream word i to the next destination register; measured on RDNA2 (gfx1031) at every edge: the anchor is floor(x-0.5) with elements at -1, 0, +1 and +2 clamped horizontally into the width, the row is floor(y), negative rows clamp to row 0, rows at or past the height return zeros, sampler addressing modes do not affect the window, and formats wider than 32 bits per texel write zeros in every register. The emitter fetches each window texel at LOD 0 and packs its bits like `image_load_pck`, so the sampler's filter, addressing and LOD state are not used; formats whose bits are not recoverable from the view and D16 data throw. The anchor and row multiply the normalized coordinate by the image size without contraction. The stream rule was measured for R8_UNORM, RG8_UNORM and RGBA8; only RGBA8 has an execution test. `image_gather8h_pck` (opcode 99) never returns texel data on this hardware - across dmasks 0x1 to 0xFF including the upper nibble, RGBA8, RGBA16 and RGBA32 formats, 1D, 2D and 3D descriptors and normalized and unnormalized sampling it only zero-fills popcount(dmask) registers (sentinel-prefilled destinations) - so it decodes but throws at translation; its eight-texel semantics remain unmeasured.
- [waitpid](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) returns -1 with `ECHILD` (`EINVAL` for unknown options): nothing here creates a child process; child processes would need a real `waitpid`.
- [execv](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) throws: replacing the process image is not supported, the same as `execvp`. PPSA97358 imports it and fails to load without it.
- [kern.proc.pathname](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/SystemConfiguration.cpp) (libkernel) returns `/app0/eboot.bin` for pid -1 and the calling process, following FreeBSD's `sysctl_kern_proc_pathname`; the exact pathname on retail consoles remains unverified.
- [machdep.cacheline_size](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/SystemConfiguration.cpp) (libkernel) returns -1 with `ENOENT`, matching FreeBSD amd64 where this sysctl OID is not defined.
- [getrlimit](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) - `RLIMIT_CPU`, `RLIMIT_FSIZE`, `RLIMIT_CORE`, `RLIMIT_SBSIZE`, `RLIMIT_VMEM`/`AS`, `RLIMIT_NPTS`, `RLIMIT_SWAP`, `RLIMIT_KQUEUES` and `RLIMIT_UMTXP` report infinity as in FreeBSD's process-0 defaults, and `RLIMIT_DATA` mirrors the host limit. `RLIMIT_STACK`, `RLIMIT_RSS`, `RLIMIT_MEMLOCK`, `RLIMIT_NPROC` and `RLIMIT_NOFILE` throw because no console values are established.
- [execvp](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) throws: replacing the process image is not supported. PPSA85300 imports it and fails to load without it; its `fork` and `vfork` fail with `ENOSYS` ([libc_missing.c](https://github.com/ZiZc3/VLC/blob/f3253020a64368153233ede29183014c0b6c8979/ps5/compat/libc_missing.c#L290-L292)), so the child process that would call it is never created.
- [fork](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel_sys/Export.cpp) (libkernel_sys) throws: creating a child process is not supported. PPSA19111 imports it and fails to load without it. Its engine calls `fork` in `Posix_Daemonize`, only with `-daemonize`, and in `Platform_ShellExecute` before `execvp("xdg-open")`, which the main menu's Previews button reaches, so pressing Previews ends in this throw. [setsid](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel_sys/Export.cpp) fails with `EPERM`, as FreeBSD's does for a process group leader, since the guest is taken to lead its own process group (see `kill`). [umask](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel_sys/Export.cpp) keeps the guest's mask (initially 022, masked to 07777) and returns the previous one, Linux named shared-memory creation applies it, but ordinary files are still created with the host process's mask
- [MIMG BY2/BY4 loads](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) (`image_load[_mip]_by2/by4`, opcodes 0x42, 0x43, 0x4a and 0x4b) were measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) with the [hardware oracle](HW_ORACLE.md) for 2D images with identity swizzle. They read N texels (BY2: 2, BY4: 4) from `x & ~(N - 1)` on row y, texel by texel and component by component within each texel, with each texel converted like `image_load`. Every register is zero when the mip level is past the last level, `x + N - 1` is outside the level's width (unaligned x, unsigned), or y is outside its height. Data only comes back when the N texels fit in one dword: R8 and R16 (DMASK 0x3) or RG8 (0xf) for BY2, and R8 (0xf) for BY4. With 32-bit texels (R32, RG32, RG16, RGBA8) the hardware returns zeros. Other formats, masks, dimensions, swizzles, D16/A16 and indirect descriptors throw. The stores (0x52, 0x53, 0x5a and 0x5b) decode but throw at translation. Measured on hardware, they write from the unaligned x up to the end of its dword, which is not implemented.
- [2D stores to 1D storage images](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp): an `image_store` with DIM 2D through a 1D T# writes texel x and ignores y, as measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) for y from 0 to 4 and 0xffffffff, single- and four-component formats and a 1x1 8_8_8_8_UNORM T#: the T# type decides the addressing, DIM only how many address VGPRs are read. Storage loads, atomics and `image_store_mip` through such a pair still throw
- [MIMG PCK2/PCK4 loads](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) (`image_load[_mip]_pck2/pck4`, opcodes 0x70, 0x71, 0x73 and 0x74) were measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) with the [hardware oracle](HW_ORACLE.md) for 2D images with identity swizzle. With data mask 0x1, they write one dword: the raw bits of the N texels (PCK2: 2, PCK4: 4) from `x & ~(N - 1)` on row y, concatenated (element k at bit `k * texel bits`) and zero-extended. The dword is zero when the mip level is past the last level, `x + N - 1` is outside the level's width (unaligned x, unsigned), or y is outside its height. Data only comes back when N texels fit in 32 bits: R8, R16 or RG8 for PCK2, R8 for PCK4. Wider texels (R32, RG16, RGBA8) and masks other than 0x1 write zeros on hardware. Those masks throw at decode, and formats outside UNORM/UINT/SINT R8, R16 and RG8 throw. #1314's earlier measurement saw only zeros because it used masks 0x3 and 0xf. The stores (0x76, 0x77, 0x79 and 0x7a) decode but throw at translation. They are not measured, and opcode 0x76 on 32_32_32_32_FLOAT hung the GPU (#1314).
- [3D image accesses bound to a 2D or 2D-array T#](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp) follow CouchRiv's [hardware oracle](HW_ORACLE.md) measurement on RDNA2 (gfx1036, #2023) and adriadam10's (gfx1035, #2140), not the PS5's gfx1013. On a 2D T#, `image_sample_lz` ignores r and reads the (u,v) texel, `image_load` and `image_load_mip` read the (x,y) texel when z is 0 and return zero otherwise, and `image_get_resinfo` returns (width, height, 1, levels). On a 2D-array T#, these accesses behave like dim 2D-array: r or z is the layer, and `image_get_resinfo` returns the layer count as its third component. Every other access of such an image, including samples with an LOD, bias, derivatives, offset or comparison, gathers, `image_get_lod` and stores, keeps throwing because it is not measured.
- [Unnormalized S# coordinates](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestSamplerResource.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - `FORCE_UNNORMALIZED` uses a Vulkan `unnormalizedCoordinates` sampler, so the S# LOD range, LOD bias and mip filter are not applied, which is exact only on a one-level view.
- [Unnormalized S#s](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/DescriptorBindingBuilder.cpp) are accepted only when direct and used only by explicit-LOD `image_sample` without comparison, derivatives or `_a`, on a single-level, single-layer 1D or 2D view of mip 0, even when the image is also sampled through a normalized S#.
- An [unnormalized S#](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestSamplerResource.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) throws with unequal minification and magnification filters, anisotropy, `TRUNC_COORD`, `MC_COORD_TRUNC` or X/Y clamping other than last-texel or border.
- [Unnormalized texel offsets](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) are added to the f32 coordinates: exact on RDNA2 (gfx1035), which applies the offset before the clamp ([measured by adriadam10](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/pull/822#issuecomment-6033192725)).
- [Image atomics](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp) on 32_SINT and 32_FLOAT surfaces act on the raw dword through an R32_UINT storage view; their float results are assumed to match those measured on 32_UINT surfaces. Other formats throw.
- [`s_memtime`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) on RADV with a GFX10.3 to GFX11.7 GPU (RDNA 2, RDNA 3, RDNA 3.5) reads the Vulkan device clock instead of the subgroup clock, which [ACO](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/main/src/amd/compiler/instruction_selection/aco_select_nir_intrinsics.cpp) reads from the 20-bit `SHADER_CYCLES` register there and which wraps every 2^20 cycles. The device clock is a monotonic 64-bit counter, but it counts the 100 MHz reference clock (the same one as `s_memrealtime`), not shader cycles, so `s_memtime` deltas are about 20 times smaller than on hardware. The families are matched by the name RADV reports ([`NarrowSubgroupClock`](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/SubgroupClock.cpp)); a family added to Mesa later reads the subgroup clock until it is listed. AMD's proprietary Windows driver also exposes a 20-bit subgroup clock on device 0x1114 (Radeon 860M, measured in #1615), device 0x744c (Radeon RX 7900 XT, measured in #1632), and device 0x747e (Radeon RX 7800 XT, measured in #1632); other AMD proprietary devices remain unverified.
- [Background link-time-optimized relink of library pipelines](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/include/PipelineLinkOptimization.hpp) (libSceAgcDriver) is skipped on every GPU under NVIDIA's proprietary Vulkan driver (`VK_DRIVER_ID_NVIDIA_PROPRIETARY`), so those pipelines keep their fast-linked form. The optimized link loses the device when bound on an RTX 3080 (Ampere, #2866) and an RTX 4060 (Ada, #3434); an RTX 5060 and an RTX 5080 (Blackwell) on the same driver do not, so the scope is wider than the evidence. No standard Vulkan query tells the architecture apart, and the driver version that fixes it is unknown. `APS5_NO_PIPELINE_LTO` disables the relink on every driver.
- [`s_memtime` and `s_memrealtime`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) on llvmpipe read only the low 32 bits of the host's nanosecond clock at either scope: Mesa's [`emit_clock`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/main/src/gallium/auxiliary/gallivm/lp_bld_nir_soa.c) builds the high dword with a left shift by 32, so it is always 0 and both counters wrap every 2^32 ns (about 4.3 s). The [shader clock test](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/tests/execution/ShaderClock.cpp) compares them modulo 2^32 there
- [`ds_wrxchg2_rtn_b32`, `ds_wrxchg2st64_rtn_b32`, `ds_wrxchg2_rtn_b64` and `ds_wrxchg2st64_rtn_b64`](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/MemoryInstructions/SharedMemoryInstructions.cpp) are translated as two separate 32-bit or 64-bit atomic swaps per invocation. RDNA2 hardware (measured on gfx1035) performs every lane's first exchange before any lane's second one, so when lanes of the same wave or of different waves overlap addresses, the result can differ from hardware. `offset0 == offset1` throws, although on hardware it is two exchanges in order.
- [`ds_condxchg32_rtn_b64`](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/MemoryInstructions/SharedMemoryInstructions.cpp) uses two 32-bit atomic conditional exchanges. RDNA2 (gfx1035) measurements confirmed aligned return values, conditional bit clearing and 16-bit offsets; out-of-range accesses return zero without modifying LDS. Misaligned accesses fault the wave on gfx1035; the recompiler does not model this fault. Radeon 780M (gfx1103, RDNA3) measurements confirmed aligned return values, conditional bit clearing, EXEC masking and operand overlap in wave32 and wave64; contended cases passed per-dword conservation checks. Contended lane ordering and gfx1013 remain unmeasured; pair-wide indivisibility is not assumed, and concurrent 64-bit LDS atomics use a separate lock. GDS throws. VGPR pairs extending beyond v255 throw; allocated-VGPR EXEC nullification is not emulated.
- [64-bit image atomics](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/MemoryInstructions/ImageInstructions.cpp) (`image_atomic_*` with a two-dword data mask) need `VK_EXT_shader_image_atomic_int64` and act on 32_32 UINT, SINT and FLOAT surfaces through an R64_UINT storage view. 64-bit `image_atomic_inc`, `image_atomic_dec` and the float atomics throw, as does any other access in the same shader to an image it updates with 64-bit atomics.
- [8_SRGB and 8_8_SRGB loads](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) on a device that cannot sample R8_SRGB or R8G8_SRGB return the sRGB EOTF rounded to nearest even at bf16 precision, as adriadam10 measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) in #866; the PS5's gfx1013 is not measured.
- [8_SRGB and 8_8_SRGB textures](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/TextureFormat.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) that the device samples as R8_SRGB or R8G8_SRGB use its own decode, which need not match gfx1035: NVIDIA's R8_SRGB is one bf16 step off in 29 of 256 codes.
- [Samples and gathers](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) of 8_SRGB and 8_8_SRGB textures throw on a device that cannot sample R8_SRGB or R8G8_SRGB.
- [Bindless image table](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp) entries of 8_SRGB and 8_8_SRGB textures read zeros, like converted formats, on a device that cannot sample R8_SRGB or R8G8_SRGB.
- [Shader MODE register access](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/ScalarInstructions.cpp) reads the initial rounding fields from `GuestContext::floatMode`; missing metadata retains the legacy round-to-nearest-even assumption. Immediate rounding writes are accepted only when they preserve that state. State-changing or SGPR-sourced MODE writes throw until runtime mode tracking and affected arithmetic are implemented. Non-rounding register reads still throw; `s_setreg_b32` writes to other hardware registers are still ignored. Tests follow the public RDNA2/LLVM contract, not console measurements.
- A [flat SRT read](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/SrtWalker/SrtDescriptorEvaluation.cpp) through a null or unmapped pointer loaded from memory raises a [BDA fault](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/BdaResources.cpp) only if the program executes it, then stops or reads zero like a BDA read.
- Repeated `s_load`s of one [flat SRT](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/SrtWalker/SrtDescriptorEvaluation.cpp) dword through a null or unmapped pointer report one of their pcs, and an `s_load` whose inaccessible dwords are all unused, or only address repeated loads, reports no fault of its own.
- A value read through a null or unmapped pointer still throws at record time if a [branch condition, uniform fill or table base](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/SrtWalker/SrtDescriptorEvaluation.cpp) needs it, or if the pointer comes from user data and is not null, even if the console skips the read.
- A [V# or flat SRT word](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/SrtWalker/SrtEvaluator.cpp) read through a null pointer taken from user data is zero and counts as a [poisoned SRT read](../../core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp), so a dispatch that reads it is not cached. The console faults if the program executes the read; PPSA28416 branches around it with `s_cbranch_execz`. The fault is not modeled.
- A [V#, T# or S#](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/SrtWalker/SrtDescriptorEvaluation.cpp) loaded through a null or unmapped pointer read from memory is zero when the work is recorded, and the capture throws if no flat SRT slot records that read.
- [2D accesses through a 1D T#](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp) read the texel the 1D addressing selects and ignore the second coordinate, as simonbbby measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) in #2237 for `image_sample_lz` and mip-less `image_load` with point filtering and wrap, clamp and border addressing; the PS5's gfx1013 is not measured. A plain `image_sample` (implicit LOD) through such a pair takes its LOD from the derivatives of the first coordinate alone, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) with the [hardware oracle](HW_ORACLE.md) on the quads of a compute kernel (wave32 and wave64, IEEE off, DX10 clamp on, denormals preserved, round to nearest even): on a 16-texel, 5-mip 32_UINT T# with point filters it picks the same mip and texel as DIM 1D for first-coordinate steps of 0, 1, 2, 4 and 8 texels per lane along x or along y, whatever the second coordinate does (normalised steps up to 16 per lane), while the same rows on a 16x16 2D T# move the mip with the second coordinate. Outside the pixel stage a plain `image_sample` is emitted with LOD 0 for every image type, so this pair reads mip 0 there, where the kernel measurement shows the quad's derivatives being used. Other 2D accesses through a 1D T# (storage, depth, packed, BY loads, mips, explicit LOD, bias, offsets, derivatives, comparison) still throw.
- [Emulated comparison of a 2D-array access through a 2D T#](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp) samples the 2D image and ignores the slice, as the native modes of a 2D-array access through a 2D T# already do. This follows the T# type deciding the addressing; it is not measured on hardware for comparison samples
- [Scalar aperture base sources](../../core/shader/recompiler/RdnaDecoder/src/RdnaInstructionDecoder.cpp) (core/shader/recompiler) - `src_shared_base`, `src_shared_limit`, `src_private_base` and `src_private_limit` (scalar source codes 235 to 238) are not modelled: decoding throws `unsupported scalar source operand code` with the code and the program counter, so a shader that reads one does not compile. Operand 235 (`src_shared_base`) was reported on [#1182](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/pull/1182) from PPSA26344; the flat aperture routing does not read the base, so a shader that builds its own flat addresses from it stops at decode.
- [Flat aperture bases](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp) (core/shader/recompiler) - the layout follows the gfx9+ one in the AMD KFD driver's `kfd_flat_memory.c` (`MAKE_LDS_APP_BASE_V9`, `MAKE_LDS_APP_LIMIT`): each aperture is a 4 GB window whose base is the high dword, so a flat address is routed by its whole high dword, as measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035: `src_shared_base` 0x00010000_00000000, `src_shared_limit` 0x00010000_ffffffff). The PS5's base values, high dwords 0x80000000 (shared, `SharedApertureHigh`) and 0x70000000 (private, `PrivateApertureHigh`), are inferred from GTA V's traversal addresses, not measured on a console or read from SH_MEM_BASES
- [Flat accesses outside the aperture routing](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp) (core/shader/recompiler) - only compute stages route flat accesses, so graphics-stage flat accesses to the shared or private aperture still go to BDA. In compute, a 64-bit flat atomic whose address is in the shared aperture takes the workgroup lock (`ldsLock`) for its two-dword update, and every such atomic requests that lock, so a shader with one pays for it even on global addresses (the path is exercised by `agc_driver_global_atomics_float`). Inferred, not measured on LDS or scratch: an aperture access that is not inside one dword is done byte by byte, an unaligned aperture atomic records the BDA unaligned fault, and LDS or scratch accesses past their end are dropped or read zero.
- On a host subgroup narrower than 32 lanes, [`threadBit`](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/TranslationContext/ExecMask.cpp) reads an EXEC written as a value (`s_mov_b32`/`s_mov_b64`) at bit (subgroup invocation id) of EXEC_LO, so lanes at or above the subgroup size read a lower lane's bit.
- [EXEC read as a value](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/TranslationContext/ExecMask.cpp) (`s_mov_b64 sN, exec`, the `s_*_saveexec` copy, ALU sources) holds only lanes with a host invocation; the console's also holds the wave's other lanes, e.g. 32-63 of a wave64 on 32 host lanes.
- [`v_readlane_b32`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAlu/SpirvAluEmitterMath.cpp) and fetch-inactive `v_permlane16_b32`/`v_permlanex16_b32` read undefined data from a lane that the host subgroup does not hold or whose host invocation is inactive; the console reads the lane's VGPR.
- Without fetch-inactive, [`v_permlane16_b32` and `v_permlanex16_b32`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAlu/SpirvAluEmitterMath.cpp) take a source lane that the host subgroup does not hold as inactive and keep the old value (0 with bound_ctrl); the console reads that lane's VGPR.
- On a host subgroup narrower than the wave, [`v_readlane_b32`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAlu/SpirvAluEmitterMath.cpp) of a constant lane outside it builds only as lane 63 of a half-wave scan (DPP row_shr 1/2/4/8, `v_permlanex16_b32`, EXEC -1) or, in a program whose entry EXEC is every lane (vertex, local and tessellation stages), as 0 when the value is 0 in lanes 32-63; other such reads throw. A value is 0 there when it is built from 0 through selects under a lane index (lane id or `v_mbcnt` of -1) below at most 32, `v_cmp_ne_u32` against 0, OR, DPP and permlanes; the counts in `s3` give such a bound on a 32-lane host. On a 32-lane host a half-wave scan may also run under `s_mov_b64 exec, -1`, use bound_ctrl:1 steps for an OR, add or unsigned max, and take keys that are 0 in lanes 32-63; its lane 31 is then the subgroup reduction of the host's invocations (a shuffle of the scan on a device without subgroup arithmetic): idle invocations add the identity, where the console would add the keys of the lanes `s3` claims.
- [NGG mesh entry](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/InstructionTranslator.cpp) uses the guest wave size (32 or 64) for wave indices, per-wave vertex and primitive counts, and the workgroup wave count in `s3`. The four-bit wave fields follow Mesa's [get_wave_id_in_tg, get_tgsize and get_thread_id_in_tg](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromiumos/third_party/mesa/+/refs/heads/stabilize-13982.70.B-chromeos-amd/src/gallium/drivers/radeonsi/gfx10_shader_ngg.c); partial waves and groups exceeding 15 waves throw. Indexed and non-indexed wave32 mesh rendering with 32, 64 and 128 threads was checked by pixel readback on Windows with an NVIDIA RTX 4070 Laptop GPU. The guest register values have not been compared with console hardware.
- [Mesh shader grid size](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) (shader recompiler) - every mesh shader loads `NumWorkgroups.x` and declares no vertices when it is 0, which no dispatch produces. The read is a workaround for RADV (Mesa 26.2.4, seen on an RX 9070 XT, GFX12): binding a mesh pipeline whose shader reads neither `NumWorkgroups` nor `DrawIndex` keeps the user SGPR count and draw-id flag of the vertex pipeline bound before it in the same command buffer (`radv_bind_pre_rast_shader` updates them only when the shader has the `AC_UD_VS_BASE_VERTEX_START_INSTANCE` slot), so `radv_emit_userdata_mesh` opens a 3-register `SET_SH_REG` and writes one value, the CP takes the following `DISPATCH_MESH_DIRECT` as register data and the GPU hangs (gfx ring timeout, MODE1 reset). Kena: Bridge of Spirits (PPSA01802) hits it on its layered volume draws. Reading `NumWorkgroups` gives every mesh shader the grid-size slot, so the count and the values match; drop the read once a fixed Mesa is the minimum
- [Shader recompilation](../../core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp) currently occurs right before it was transferred to Vulkan with caching, but should be moved to the [relinker](../../core/relinker/main.cpp) stage. For this purpose, [shader/recompiler](../../core/shader/recompiler) was written completely independently from [libs/prx](../../core/libs/prx).
- [v_interp_p1ll_f16, v_interp_p1lv_f16 and v_interp_p2_f16](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/AttributeInstructions.cpp) read the 16-bit halves of each vertex's parameter through `VK_KHR_fragment_shader_barycentric` and throw without it, as fixed-function interpolation cannot split a 32-bit attribute. P10 and P20 are the vertex differences rounded to f16 to nearest even, which is not confirmed on hardware. v_interp_p1ll_f16 and v_interp_p1lv_f16 round the exact `P10 * I + P0` once to f32 and v_interp_p2_f16 rounds the exact `P20 * J + S2` once to f16, as measured on RDNA2 (#697); the exact sums are computed in f64, so these instructions need the Float64 capability. They follow the shader's float mode, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) in all eight combinations of `IEEE_MODE` and kept/flushed f32 and f16 denormals over 768 special and 1,024 random rows. With IEEE=1 a NaN operand is returned quieted; with IEEE=0 it is returned unquieted, except that v_interp_p2_f16's f16 result is always quiet. Flushed f32 denormals flush I/J, v_interp_p2_f16's f32 S2 and the f32 result; flushed f16 denormals flush P0, P10/P20, v_interp_p1lv_f16's f16 S2 and the f16 result. Denormal modes 1 and 2 (flush only inputs or only outputs) and rounding other than to nearest even are not measured and throw. An unknown mode follows IEEE=0 with denormals kept. Lavapipe has no barycentrics, so the draw test is skipped in CI; `agc_driver_interpolation_modes` checks the arithmetic in every mode against the hardware rows on any device with Float64.
- Without `VK_KHR_fragment_shader_barycentric`, a pixel shader whose I/J VGPRs [only feed `v_interp_p1_f32`/`v_interp_p2_f32` of their own pair](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/HostInterpolationChecker.cpp) reads the attribute Vulkan interpolated. Any other read of them or of a raw vertex parameter takes the weights and the three vertex values from a [generated geometry stage](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/BarycentricGeometryShader.cpp), which needs `geometryShader` and a vertex shader before it: tessellation, mesh and rect-list pipelines, primitives other than triangles, more than 32 fragment input locations and interfaces beyond the device's geometry limits throw.
- [v_fma_f64 and v_div_fmas_f64](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAlu/SpirvAluEmitterFloat64.cpp) with a subnormal operand or result round once, as IEEE 754 fusedMultiplyAdd does with f64 denormals kept, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1, `v_div_fmas_f64` with VCC clear and set. The f16/f64 denormal field of `FLOAT_MODE` (bits 7:6) is not read: when it flushes, the hardware flushes the operands and the result of `v_fma_f64` (tininess before rounding) and only the result of `v_div_fmas_f64`, while these keep them. Shaders with f64 instructions declare `DenormPreserve` for 64-bit floats only when `shaderDenormPreserveFloat64` is true and Vulkan reports `denormBehaviorIndependence` as `ALL`; other independence modes are not handled by the current backend because they require coupled execution modes. See [VkShaderFloatControlsIndependence](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkShaderFloatControlsIndependence.html). Elsewhere (including lavapipe), the driver may flush f64 subnormals, and `agc_driver_f64_fma_subnormals` is skipped.
- [SINT storage images](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) of 8- and 16-bit components saturate each stored 32-bit value to the component range, as adriadam10 measured for image stores on RDNA2 (gfx1035) in #944 (448 rows match); the PS5's gfx1013 is not measured.
- [`StoreTexel`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) writes channel 1 of a two-channel format from the data slot whose DST_SEL is Y, for SINT and UINT alike. With DST_SEL X,Y,Z,W, gfx1035 writes it from the W slot, as adriadam10 measured in #944.
- [WAIT_REG_MEM](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Synchronization/WaitMemory.cpp) reports a diagnostic after `APS5_GPU_WAIT_TIMEOUT_MS` without treating the timeout as completion. An unsatisfied wait keeps polling and checking driver failures; a missing producer can therefore leave its queue waiting indefinitely. Existing recorded-label shortcuts remain in use.
- [COND_EXEC](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Packets/PacketExecution.cpp) reads its condition after the earlier packets of its queue complete. Without PFP_SYNC_ME the console's graphics PFP reads it ahead of the ME, so a title that races the read sees a later value here.
- A [flip inside a COND_EXEC range](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Packets/CommandValidation.cpp) throws: the flip is reserved with the video output when the buffer is submitted, and how the console withdraws a skipped one is unknown.
- A [COND_EXEC range](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Queues/Submission.cpp) must hold whole packets and end inside its command buffer. A chained INDIRECT_BUFFER or a REWIND inside a range throws, as what the CP does with the skipped dwords then is unknown.
- A [COND_INDIRECT_BUFFER](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Queues/Submission.cpp) (`sceAgcCbBranch`) runs only with the always-pass compare function; a comparison or a predicated packet throws. Modes 1 and 2 run the first buffer, assumed to be the if-then and if-then-else modes of AMD's PM4 packet. [sceAgcCbBranch](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Cb/src/Branch.cpp) does not check the compare address of an always-pass branch or the address of an empty buffer, as PPSA14632 passes an uninitialised compare address and a null else buffer of 0 dwords.
- A [label or store](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp) (`RELEASE_MEM`, `WRITE_DATA`, `DMA_DATA`) into guest memory the device has no host import of waits for all recorded work and is then written by the CPU, so the queue worker stalls once per such label. The console's CP stores it in stream order without that stall.
- [WRITE_DATA with DST_SEL 3](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Pm4.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) writes guest memory like DST_SEL 5. AMD's GFX10 header names 3 the GDS, but Killzone: Liberation (PPSA11142) writes its frame fence with `sceAgcDcbWriteData(dst 6, cache policy 3, write confirm)`, which the SPRX-confirmed encoding ([OpenAGC](https://github.com/OpenAGC/OpenAGC/blob/main/src/cb_builders.c)) turns into DST_SEL 3 with a 40-bit label address in the title's own memory that `RELEASE_MEM` and `WAIT_REG_MEM` then use; the console must write that label or the title would wait on it forever. Whether the console routes it through the GDS or a cache is unknown
- [SET_*_REG_INDIRECT](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Queues/Submission.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) reads its register list when the command buffer is submitted, with the packets. The console's graphics PFP reads it when it reaches the packet, so a list written after the submission (by the CPU, or by GPU work ahead of the packet behind PFP_SYNC_ME) is read here before that write.
- The [shader float mode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Shaders/ShaderRegistry.cpp) (`FLOAT_MODE`, `DX10_CLAMP`, `IEEE_MODE` and `FP16_OVFL` of `COMPUTE_PGM_RSRC1` and `SPI_SHADER_PGM_RSRC1_PS/GS/HS`, bit positions from Mesa's `gfx10.json` and `gfx103.json`) is decoded into `GuestContext::floatMode` and is part of the shader cache keys and the serialized request (#981). `IEEE_MODE` is read by `v_min_f32`, `v_max_f32`, `v_min3_f32`, `v_max3_f32` and `v_med3_f32`, their f16 forms, `v_min_f64` and `v_max_f64`, `IEEE_MODE` and the `FLOAT_MODE` output denormal bits by the output modifier, `DX10_CLAMP` by the clamp modifier, `FP16_OVFL` by every instruction with an f16 result (see its entry), and the whole mode by the 16-bit interpolation instructions (see their entries). With IEEE=1 a signaling NaN operand returns that NaN quieted (the first operand checked first, min3/max3 as two nested pairs, med3 as min3 when any operand is NaN), measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) over every combination of ±0, ±1, ±inf, denormals, quiet and signaling NaNs with f32 denormals kept and flushed. IEEE=0 and unknown modes keep the previous f32 lowering, which matches IEEE=0. The f16 forms choose their NaN results on the bits: a NaN operand returns the other operand unchanged (src0 when both are NaN), and with IEEE=1 a signaling NaN operand returns that NaN quieted, in the same order as f32. `v_min_f64` and `v_max_f64` quiet a signaling NaN operand the same way with IEEE=1; with IEEE=0 they return src0 unchanged when src1 is NaN, otherwise src1 when src0 is NaN. Both were measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1 with f32 denormals kept and flushed and f16/f64 denormals kept; RDNA2 has no f64 min3, max3 or med3. Unknown modes follow IEEE=0. `v_min_f16` and `v_max_f16` flush each denormal input to its signed zero when bit 0 of the f16/f64 denormal field is clear (0 and 2); fields 1 and 3 preserve inputs, even when output flushing is enabled. Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1, including signed zeros, NaNs and neg/abs modifiers. Unknown modes keep f16 denormals. With f16/f64 denormals flushed the hardware also flushes denormal inputs of f64 min/max, which those lowerings still do not. NaN quieting in f16 arithmetic (the unary instructions and the f32 arithmetic follow it, see below) and `FLOAT_MODE` (f32 denormals flushed before the min/max compare in Astro's Playroom's 0xc0) still follow a single mode, as the entries below record. The mode comes from the registered shader header's `sh_registers`, the same source at registration and at draw/dispatch so the prepared artifact keys match. A title that rewrites RSRC1 at run time, or a raw compute program with no header, compiles with that mode or none.
- [v_ldexp_f16](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) follows the `FLOAT_MODE` f16 denormal field: bit 0 preserves the half input and bit 1 the half result. Otherwise a denormal input is flushed to its signed zero, and a result whose exact scaled value is below the minimum normal (2^-14) is flushed to its signed zero before rounding, so `v_ldexp_f16 0x07ff, -1` gives +0 instead of 0x0400. Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) with round to nearest, IEEE 0 and 1 and denorm16 0..3, over every half magnitude 0x0000..0x0fff of both signs scaled by 2^-1 and 2^0 (16384 rows per mode): no mismatch. NaN and infinity handling and destination high-half preservation are unchanged. Shaders without a known float mode keep the previous lowering.
- A [WAIT_REG_MEM](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Synchronization/WaitMemory.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) that a compute queue passes on another queue's recorded label store, before the GPU has executed it, is landed before the queue's next dispatch, draw, COND_EXEC or predicated packet reads guest memory: the worker polls until memory satisfies the wait or no unfinished batch writes the label. The graphics queue (queue 0) is exempt, as landing its waits costs PPSA01341 about a quarter of its frame rate; a title that fills memory a graphics command buffer reads after it submits the buffer behind such a wait reads it early there. `APS5_LAND_WAITS_ALL_QUEUES=1` lands the graphics queue's waits too.
- The [dispatches that follow a compute queue's wait](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Dispatch/DispatchAhead.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver), up to the next wait that memory does not satisfy, a packet that writes guest memory other than a label, or a conditional packet, are resolved (memory captured, shader recompiled) when the wait passes, as the CP reads them right after it; the graphics queue is exempt (`APS5_GROUP_CAPTURE_ALL_QUEUES=1` includes it). The device still reads guest memory when the dispatch is issued; when that memory no longer matches the capture, the dispatch is resolved again from current memory.
- [v_mullit_f32](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) follows the hardware measured with the MODE register at IEEE=1 and denormals kept (a signaling NaN src0 is quieted only with IEEE=1, as measured at IEEE=0 too; a positive denormal src2 counts as positive); with f32 denormals flushed it follows the f32 denormals entry below, and denormal modes 1 and 2 throw.
- [v_mad_legacy_f32 and v_mac_legacy_f32](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) (VOP3 0x140, VOP2 0x06) round the product to f32 before the add, give a +0 product for a zero factor, and flush their f32 denormal sources, their product and their result to a zero of the same sign in every f32 denormal mode, an exception to the `FLOAT_MODE` model in [f32DenormalFlushFor](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/VectorInstructions.cpp). Measured on gfx1010 (RX 5700 XT) with `tools/hw-oracle/template.s`, assembled by the AMD Windows driver's `amd_comgr_2.dll` and launched through its `amdhip64_6.dll` instead of `oracle.c` and ROCr, over the 32 rows of the five `FmaLegacy.cpp` and the four `MacLegacy.cpp` encodings: identical in wave32 and wave64 at IEEE 0 and 1 with f32 denormal modes 0 to 3, except that the two signaling NaN rows are quieted only with IEEE=1 (#1281, #1290). A sum of two zeros takes its sign on the bits (+0 unless both are -0), as lavapipe returned -0 for +0 + -0. The PS5's gfx1013 is not measured; LLVM decodes the encoding as `v_mad_legacy_f32` there, as on gfx1010. RDNA2 differs: on gfx1031 (RX 6700 XT, measured by pandather in #1281) the encoding is `v_fma_legacy_f32` and fused, with the same zero rule and denormal-source flush, so an RDNA2 measurement is not evidence for the console.
- [v_fma_mixlo_f16 and v_fma_mixhi_f16](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) round the exact product-sum once to f16 with the f64 arithmetic of v_interp_p2_f16, so they need the Float64 capability. On RDNA2 (gfx1036) they follow the `FLOAT_MODE` f16 round and denormal fields, not the f32 round field: -1.5 * 2^-24 + 2^-126 from f32 sources (0xbfc00000, 0x33800000, 0x00800000) gives 0x8001 with f16 denormals kept and round to nearest even, 0x8000 with f16 denormals flushed and 0x8002 with f16 round toward -infinity. With f16 denormals kept, every f16 rounding mode is translated: the exact product-sum is rounded once in the mode's direction. All four modes match RDNA2 (gfx1036) on the 2,233 rows of the #2631 sweep, including `FLOAT_MODE` 0xcc (toward zero, used by ps5-opengl) and the exact cancellations toward -infinity; the PS5's gfx1013 is not measured. A result beyond 65504 becomes infinity or 65504 as IEEE 754 gives for the mode. Flushed f16 denormals still throw, and packed f16 still throws in every mode but round to nearest even with f16 denormals kept. `FP16_OVFL` set is not measured.
- [Signaling NaN inputs](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) of the f32 and f16 unary instructions (`v_sqrt/rcp/rsq/log/exp/sin/cos/fract/floor/ceil/trunc/rndne/rcp_iflag/frexp_mant`), `v_ldexp_f32/f16`, `v_frexp_mant_f16`, `v_div_fixup_f32/f16` and `v_mullit_f32` are returned quieted only with IEEE=1 and unchanged with IEEE=0, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) in both modes, and so are those of the f64 forms (`v_sqrt/rcp/rsq/fract/floor/ceil/trunc/rndne/frexp_mant/ldexp/div_fixup_f64`) and `v_cvt_f64_f32`, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) in both modes; shaders without a known float mode follow IEEE=0. `v_pack_b32_f16` quiets each signaling NaN half by the same rule, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) in both modes. A signaling NaN result of `v_cubesc/cubetc/cubema_f32` is quieted by the same rule and keeps the sign the instruction gives it, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) in both modes. `v_add/sub/subrev/mul/fma/fmac/fmamk/fmaak/mul_legacy_f32` and `v_fma_mix_f32` with f32 sources choose their NaN result on the bits, measured over 1000 operand triples in both modes: the first NaN source in operand order after its modifiers (the subtrahend negated, as the hardware adds its negation), quieted only with IEEE=1, or the default NaN 0xffc00000 for an invalid operation; in the fma forms an invalid product (inf * 0) gives the default NaN even when src2 is a NaN, and in the legacy forms a zero factor makes the product 0 even when the other factor is a NaN (a NaN src2 still wins). `v_mad_legacy_f32` and `v_mac_legacy_f32` follow the same rule with their separately rounded product, as measured on gfx1010 (#1281, #1290): a NaN source quieted only with IEEE=1, inf - inf giving 0xffc00000, the zero rule hiding a NaN factor and a NaN src2 winning. The f16 and f64 arithmetic, `v_fma_mix_f32` with f16 sources and the separately rounded mad/mac forms (not measurable on RDNA2) follow the same rule on hardware but their NaN bits are left to the driver. `v_cvt_f16_f32`, `v_cvt_f32_f64`, `v_dot2_f32_f16`, `v_fma_mixlo_f16` and `v_fma_mixhi_f16` always quiet and `v_div_scale_f32` never does: the f32 signaling NaN 0x7f812345 as src0 or src2 gives 0x7e09 from mixlo and mixhi at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1 on RDNA2 (gfx1036), where `v_fma_mix_f32` gives 0x7f812345 at IEEE=0 and 0x7fc12345 at IEEE=1. The payload of an f16 NaN source of mixlo and mixhi is left to the driver's f16 to f32 conversion, which NVIDIA replaces.
- [Scalar f16 add, sub, subrev and mul denormals](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) follow the `FLOAT_MODE` f16 denormal field at nearest-even: bit 0 preserves inputs and bit 1 preserves the result; otherwise a denormal is flushed to its signed zero. Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) with IEEE=0/1 and FP16_OVFL=0/1, over 32 operand pairs in all four denormal modes, including signed zeros, denormals, minimum normals, infinities and NaNs. Multiply detects tininess after rounding to 11 significant bits with an unbounded exponent, before gradual half packing: `0x3bff * 0x0400` flushes, while `0x3c01 * 0x03ff` rounds to `0x0400` and is kept. Directed rounding and ternary arithmetic keep their previous denormal lowering. Shaders without a known float mode keep the previous lowering. Scalar `v_sub_f16`/`v_subrev_f16` share `float16Binary` with add/mul and were measured on gfx1036 over the same operand set as add (1156 non-NaN-input rows per mode); NaN result bits still depend on the driver as recorded above.
- [f16 unary, frexp and conversion denormals](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) follow the `FLOAT_MODE` f16 denormal field: `v_ceil_f16`, `v_floor_f16`, `v_fract_f16`, `v_rcp_f16`, `v_rsq_f16`, `v_sqrt_f16`, `v_log_f16`, `v_exp_f16`, `v_sin_f16`, `v_frexp_mant_f16`, `v_frexp_exp_i16_f16`, `v_cvt_norm_i16_f16`, `v_cvt_norm_u16_f16` and `v_cvt_f32_f16` flush a denormal half input to its signed zero when bit 0 is clear (0 and 2), and the f16-result ones flush a denormal half result to its signed zero when bit 1 is clear (0 and 1), after the host rounds it to half. Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1 with denorm16 0..3 over every non-NaN f16 input (63,490 rows per mode and instruction): the only mismatches are the 1-ULP driver differences of `v_exp_f16` (2 inputs) and `v_sin_f16` (14), which are the same with denormals kept. NaN inputs are not compared. `v_ldexp_f16` shares the input rule. `v_cvt_pknorm_i16_f16` and `v_cvt_pknorm_u16_f16` are not measured and keep their previous lowering. Shaders without a known float mode keep the previous lowering.
- [Packed f16 NaN results](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) (`v_pk_add/mul/fma/min/max_f16`) follow the hardware measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1 with f16 round-to-nearest-even and f16 denormals kept: with IEEE=1 a signaling NaN input is quieted and min/max return the other operand only for a quiet NaN; with IEEE=0 NaN inputs pass through unquieted and min/max ignore signaling. In `v_pk_fma_f16` an invalid product (inf * 0) gives the default NaN even when src2 is a NaN. A shader without a known float mode is translated as IEEE=0, the mode titles use; a mode with another f16 rounding or that flushes f16 denormals throws.
- [f32 denormals](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/TranslationContext/OperandAccess.cpp) follow the shader's `FLOAT_MODE` f32 denormal field (bits 5:4) when it flushes them (0, and shaders without a known float mode, as in the 0xc0 most titles use), measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) over 1444 rows per instruction at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1. `v_add/sub/subrev/mul/mul_legacy/mullit/fma/fmac/fmamk/fmaak/min/max/min3/max3/med3/fract/floor/ceil/frexp_mant/sin/cos_f32` flush their f32 inputs and their result to a zero of the same sign (`v_mullit_f32` measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) over 6907 rows per mode at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1, so a positive denormal src2 counts as zero); the f32 compares, `v_cvt_i32/u32/rpi_i32/flr_i32_f32`, `v_frexp_exp_i32_f32`, `v_cvt_f64_f32`, `v_cvt_f16_f32`, `v_cvt_pkrtz_f16_f32`, `v_cvt_pknorm_i16/u16_f32` and `v_cvt_pk_u8_f32` flush their f32 inputs. `v_div_scale_f32` flushes all three inputs to signed zeros, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) over 2744 operand triples per mode at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1 with f32 denormals flushed and kept; its scale-down behaviour and host denormal preservation remain incomplete. `v_div_fixup_f32` flushes its src1 and src2 and keeps a denormal src0 and result, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) over 6859 rows per mode at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1. Tininess is detected after rounding, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) for `v_mul_f32` and `v_mullit_f32` at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1: a product is flushed when its value rounded to 24 bits with an unbounded exponent is below 2^-126, so `0x3f7ffffe * 0x00800001` (2^-126 * (1 - 2^-46)) gives `0x00800000` and `0x3f7fffff * 0x00800000` (2^-126 * (1 - 2^-24)) gives zero. The check rounds in the f32 round mode of `FLOAT_MODE` (bits 1:0), measured in all four modes, while the products themselves are still rounded to nearest even. This applies to `v_mul_f32`, `v_mul_legacy_f32`, `v_mullit_f32`, and to `v_fma_f32`, `v_fmac_f32`, `v_fmamk_f32`, `v_fmaak_f32` and `v_fma_mix_f32` when the addend is zero; with a nonzero addend the fused result is kept, so an exact result just below 2^-126 that rounds to it is not flushed. In the three directed modes the check is computed exactly from the significands. To nearest even (0, the mode of shaders without a known float mode) one factor is multiplied by 2^64, which is exact below 2^64, and the product with the other factor is compared with 2^-62. That multiply is a plain `OpFMul` like the product, and no f32 rounding mode is requested in the SPIR-V, so the check relies on the driver rounding f32 to nearest even by default, as the products do, and a product that rounds up to 2^-126 is the emitted product. `v_fma_mix_f32` follows this rule with mixed f16/f32 sources too, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) with f16 in either multiplicand slot, low and high half selection, and an f32 or f16 zero addend: f32 `0x00801002` times f16 `0x3bff` gives `0x00800000` to nearest at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1, while the neighbours `0x00801001` and `0x00801003` give zero and `0x00800001`. The tiny-product boundary was also measured for both signs in all four f32 round modes at IEEE=0; f16 inputs retain their previous conversion and denormal handling. `v_fma_mixlo_f16` and `v_fma_mixhi_f16` flush their f32 sources too and keep a product below 2^-126, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036). `v_mad_legacy_f32` and `v_mac_legacy_f32` flush their inputs, their product and their result in every mode, see its entry; their product is still checked for tininess on its exact value, so one below 2^-126 gives zero even when it rounds to 2^-126, as the console's separately rounded encoding is not measured at this boundary. Denormal modes 1 and 2 throw for the other instructions. Not covered, and not measurable on RDNA2 (no such opcodes) or not measured: `v_mac_f32`, `v_mad_f32`, `v_madak_f32` and `v_madmk_f32` (rounded separately), `v_dot2_f32_f16` and `v_div_fmas_f32` (on hardware `v_div_fmas_f32` flushes only its result), f32 interpolation and `ds_add_f32` keep the previous lowering; `ds_min/max/cmpst_f32` flush both operands by the field, see the LDS float atomics entry. With f32 denormals kept (3) the results depend on the driver's default denormal behaviour, as no `DenormPreserve` execution mode is requested; `v_ldexp_f32` and the f32 compares are the exceptions. `v_cmp_X_f32` and `v_cmpx_X_f32` compare the operands as sign-magnitude integers with the NaN cases handled apart, so a denormal is not read as zero, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) over 256 operand pairs per opcode at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1 with f32 denormals kept and flushed. `v_ldexp_f32` preserves subnormal inputs and rounds a subnormal result in the f32 round mode of `FLOAT_MODE`, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) over 2163 rows in each of the four round modes at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1. An overflow rounds like any other result (infinity, or the largest finite value when the mode rounds that sign toward zero). With f32 denormals flushed it flushes a subnormal input and result, measured over the same rows in each round mode at IEEE=0 only. A device whose driver splits `Fma` into a multiply and an add flushes a product below 2^-126 before adding a non-zero addend in `v_fma_f32` and `v_fmac_f32`: `0x1fffffff * 0x1fffffff + 0x00800000` gives `0x00800000` instead of the fused `0x00ffffff` on RADV RENOIR (Mesa 26.2.4), whose compiled shader has `v_mad_f32` and no `v_fma_f32`. [F32DenormalFlush](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/tests/execution/F32DenormalFlush.cpp) accepts that sum on such a device.
- The executable file that [relinker](../../core/relinker/elfpatcher/src/windows/WindowsPeWriter.cpp) generates opens the console when launched, which is inconvenient for playability.
- [Overlay output bus](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVideoOut/src/VideoOutDriver.cpp) (libSceVideoOut) - flips on a port opened with `SCE_VIDEO_OUT_BUS_TYPE_OVERLAY` complete and signal their events like main-bus flips, but their buffers are not shown: the overlay is not composited over the main output. Presenting them as whole frames made the window alternate between the two buses
- [PA_CL_CLIP_CNTL](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - `DX_LINEAR_ATTR_CLIP_ENA` (bit 24) clear is drawn like bit 24 set, with Vulkan's clipping of `NoPerspective` outputs; what a clear bit changes on the console is undocumented.
- [10_11_11 unorm color targets](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) render into R32_UINT attachments: the pixel shader packs each export, rounded to nearest even like format-30 image stores, which is assumed to match the CB's conversion.
- [PA_SU_SC_MODE_CNTL.PROVOKING_VTX_LAST](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Pipeline.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) uses Vulkan's last-vertex mode for native primitives. Bit 19 follows [opengnm's `flatshade_first` mapping](https://github.com/blackbearreloaded/ps5-opengl/blob/db752da4c89380af228497b5b6852163a4e61069/src/gallium/ps5/ps5_screen.c#L2244); vertex selection follows [Vulkan](https://docs.vulkan.org/spec/latest/chapters/drawing.html#drawing-primitive-topologies), without console measurements. Last mode throws without `VK_EXT_provoking_vertex`, for generated rectangle, mesh or tessellation primitives, explicit per-vertex flat inputs, and triangle strips with per-vertex inputs, whose [ordering depends on the device](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkPhysicalDeviceFragmentShaderBarycentricPropertiesKHR.html).
- [v_rcp, v_rsq, v_sqrt, v_log, v_exp, v_sin and v_cos](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) (f32 and f16) use the driver's approximation for finite results, not the console's: the low bits can differ. Only NaN, invalid, zero and infinite results and the exact sin/cos zeros are chosen on the bits. Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) over every f16 input and 32,832 f32 inputs: RADV returns the hardware's bits for every input of rcp, rsq, sqrt and log and all but one of exp (f16 0x11c5, 1 ULP); NVIDIA (615.71.09) stays within 2 ULP except `v_log_f32` near 1.0 (next entry). [v_sin and v_cos](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAlu/SpirvAluEmitterMath.cpp) take the driver's sine or cosine of the radians of the exact cycle, except within 2^-12 of a turn from a zero of the function, where the result is the linear term of the exact distance to the zero: the hardware's constant 6.2831826 (0x40c90fd5) at the integers, which the hardware applies exactly to inputs below 2^-12 of a turn, and the f32 2π at the half and quarter turns, where the hardware is within 7 ULP of it. Outside those bands, a driver result more than 4 ULP from a Taylor series in turns of the exact cycle is replaced by the series, which is within 2 ULP of the true sine and cosine; the hardware is up to 9 ULP from them (gfx1035, 243,092 f32 inputs), so results stay within 13 ULP of the hardware on any driver. RADV returns the hardware's bits for every f16 input and for 32,422 sine and 32,486 cosine results of the 32,832 f32 inputs; the rest are 1 ULP off, except up to 14 ULP on either side of non-zero integers and up to 15 ULP beyond ±256 cycles, where the hardware's own reduction differs from the exact fraction, and up to 6 ULP inside the half- and quarter-turn bands. NVIDIA's own sine is within 1 ULP on 93% of the f32 inputs and up to 1,166 ULP off just outside the bands, its cosine within 163 ULP; the series replaces those results. A denormal f32 input with f32 denormals kept gives the hardware the input times 6.2831826; both hosts flush it.
- [v_log_f32 and v_log_f16](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAlu/SpirvAluEmitterMath.cpp) keep the driver's log2 unless, for inputs within 1/8 of 1.0, it is more than 4 ULP from an eight-term series in x - 1, which then replaces it. `v_log_f16` goes through the same f32 lowering and is rounded to half. The series is evaluated for every `v_log` and chosen with `OpSelect` rather than a branch, so its seven fused multiply-adds and two multiplies are an unconditional cost. Measured on RDNA2 over all 3,145,728 f32 inputs within 1/8 of 1.0 (gfx1035, IEEE 0, f32 denormals flushed), the hardware is within 1 ULP of the correctly rounded log2 and the series within 2 ULP of it and within 3 ULP of the hardware (2 except at 0x3f752e47, 0x3f7baab0 and 0x3f7bc4fb), so a kept driver value is within 7 ULP of the hardware and a driver that returns the hardware's bits never triggers the gate; RADV (Mesa 26.2.4) returns the hardware's bits on the 9,280 such inputs measured on gfx1036; NVIDIA (615.71.09) was up to 23,947,836 ULP off at 1 - 2^-24 and is within 6 ULP inside the band after the change; outside it its own log2 stays up to 9 ULP off (measured up to 1.375). Over every f16 input, the change moves one `v_log_f16` result on NVIDIA (0x3bed, 1 ULP, onto the hardware's bits) and none on RADV.
- [Dual-source blending](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) takes the MRT1 export as the second source of the MRT0 blend, as RADV and radeonsi program it; what the CB does with other export or target layouts is unknown, so they throw.
- `--to-intel` does not lower RDPRU/MCOMMIT; the [matcher](../../core/relinker/codegen/src/x86/Amd64OnlyInstructionMatcher.cpp) fails the relink instead.
- [Ordered not-equal, less-than and greater-than compares](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAlu/SpirvAluEmitterMath.cpp) (shader recompiler) - `FPOrdNotEqual32` is emitted as `OpFUnordNotEqual`, and `FPOrdLessThan32` and `FPOrdGreaterThan32` as their ordered forms, each with both operands checked for NaN on their bits; the results are the same for every input. The AMD Windows driver 32.0.13058.8001 (Radeon 860M, RDNA 3.5) returns 1 for a NaN operand when any float form of not-equal feeds a ballot on a 32-wide subgroup (#825), and so does a less-than or greater-than against an infinity the driver sees as a constant (#3536), presumably rewritten into a not-equal by its compiler
- [sce::Job::JobManager](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJobManager/Export.cpp) (libSceJobManager) is written from the disassembly of the copies PPSA01474 and PPSA36082 ship in `sce_module`, and `guest_job_manager_tests` passes against both of those modules as well: the memory size formula, the error codes, sync point ordering, the per-sequence limits and the priority order are the original's. Only CPU sequences exist: `initialize` and `calculateRequiredMemorySize` with compute sequences, and both compute dispatches, are not implemented, and `createCompute` and `destroyCompute` return the errors the original returns when no compute sequence was configured. The manager's state lives on the host heap, with only the block header written to the title's memory; the original keeps everything in that block, and a block smaller than 0x2d8 bytes throws here. The worker sleep policy, the spin count and the persistent thread count are validated and stored but change nothing, a factory priority above 1 throws, `shutdown` throws while a thread is blocked in a sequence wait, and using a sequence after its `destroy` throws. `initialize` returns 0 where the original returns the result of its last import (`BG26hBGiNlw`, not resolved to a name), the `sceRazorCpuJobManager*` profiler calls are not made, and a worker thread that cannot be created or configured throws where the original does not check. The order of the sequence and factory virtual functions is read from the modules' vtables only, since no title that calls them has been run
- The length-changing path of the [instruction rewriter](../../core/relinker/codegen/src/x86/X64InstructionRewriter.cpp) is not used by the [converter](../../core/relinker/codegen/src/Amd64OnlyConverter.cpp): it does not adjust data-to-code references (relocations, FDEs, jump tables) or segment sizes, so every substitution keeps the instruction length.
- In executables with a `PT_SCE_DYNLIBDATA` segment, the PS4 layout, the executable segment also holds read-only data after the code (CUSA00900: 37 MiB after its last function). The [relinker pipeline](../../core/relinker/relinker/src/pipeline/RelinkerPipeline.cpp) therefore scans for syscalls and import call sites only up to the end of the PLT that follows the last function of the unwind table; code after the PLT without an unwind entry is not scanned. `--to-intel` and the `unused-filter=1` and `unused-filter=2` analyses are not limited this way.
- [DB_SHADER_CONTROL.CONSERVATIVE_Z_EXPORT](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderInputState.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) becomes `DepthLess`/`DepthGreater`. For a pixel shader that breaks the promise, the host driver decides whether its interpolated Z is depth-tested before shading; what the console does then was not measured.
- [DB_SHADER_CONTROL.STENCIL_TEST_VAL_EXPORT_ENABLE](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) becomes `FragStencilRefEXT`, taken from the second MRTZ channel of the 32_GR and 32_ABGR formats as Mesa's AMD drivers write it ([`emit_ps_mrtz_export`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/3e32b0e1f132231b7a972c1b0b5b06c7e16cf8b9/src/amd/common/nir/ac_nir_lower_ps_late.c#L194)); the console was not measured. The host value is the test value of both faces and what `REPLACE_TEST` writes, so a stencil operation that writes all ones or the operation value throws with it, as do a stencil clear draw, the UINT16_ABGR layout, `STENCIL_OP_VAL_EXPORT_ENABLE` and a device without `VK_EXT_shader_stencil_export` (NVIDIA's driver 610.57 offers none).
- [DB_SHADER_CONTROL.PRIMITIVE_ORDERED_PIXEL_SHADER](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) (shader recompiler) - the host pixel interlock spans the whole shader, not only up to `s_sendmsg` ORDERED_PS_DONE.
- [POPS collision SGPRs](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) (shader recompiler) - the collision wave ID and intrawave collision mask read 0, so a title skips its POPS wait and runs its per-collision loop once.
- [Image loads](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceTracker.cpp) (shader recompiler) - an image_load reads a sampled binding, also of an image the shader stores to, so in a primitive-ordered pixel shader it misses an earlier overlapping primitive's image_store in the same draw.
- [PA_SC_CONSERVATIVE_RASTERIZATION_CNTL](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - 0x6001 uses Vulkan overestimation on devices that add at most 1/256 pixel and keep degenerate triangles; the console's uncertainty region and degenerate handling are unmeasured. A value with `OVER_RAST_ENABLE` and `UNDER_RAST_ENABLE` clear is decoded as disabled even when `UNCERTAINTY_REGION_MODE` or the `OUTER_`/`INNER_UNCERTAINTY_EDGERULE_OVERRIDE` bits (19:16) are set, as PPSA01474 writes 0xe6000; that these fields have no effect without over- or underestimation is assumed from their names, not measured.
- The Linux placement of the `--to-intel` stubs in the [ELF patcher](../../core/relinker/elfpatcher/src/linux/LinuxElfPatcher.cpp) is covered only by a synthetic test.
- The libc [SSE4a trap emulation](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/specifics/windows/Sse4aEmulation.hpp) on Windows is superseded by `--to-intel` and remains only until the relinked title has been verified without it.
- [getifaddrs](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Interfaces.cpp) (libc) lists the host's IPv4 and IPv6 addresses in FreeBSD's `ifaddrs` and sockaddr layouts. FreeBSD's per-interface AF_LINK entries (MAC, index, MTU) and their `if_data` are not produced, so interfaces without an address are omitted. Names are the host's (Linux `lo`, `eth0`; Windows NDIS names such as `ethernet_32769`, or `if<index>` when one does not fit 15 bytes), not the console's. A Linux point-to-point address without a peer loses `IFF_POINTOPOINT`, and an IPv4 broadcast address the host omits is computed from the netmask. On Windows the flags are only `IFF_UP`, `IFF_RUNNING`, `IFF_LOOPBACK`, `IFF_BROADCAST` and `IFF_MULTICAST`, derived from the adapter: PPP and tunnel adapters get neither `IFF_BROADCAST` nor `IFF_POINTOPOINT` because their peer address is not reported, and an address whose prefix length the host reports as unspecified gets a host mask.
- [gethostname](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Hostname.cpp) (libc) reports the fixed guest name `AnyPS5` without querying the host machine name. Buffer copying, null queries and `ENAMETOOLONG` follow [FreeBSD gethostname](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/main/lib/libc/gen/gethostname.c); the console's configurable hostname and its integration with `sysctl` and name resolution are unverified and not implemented.
- [Descriptor pool chain](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderResources.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - each pool holds two storage buffer descriptors more than a set can take, as a workaround: RADV (Mesa 26.0 and later) lets a `FREE_DESCRIPTOR_SET` pool place a set up to 32 bytes past the pool's buffer ([`util_vma_heap_init`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/main/src/amd/vulkan/radv_descriptor_pool.c) is given `bo_size + RADV_POOL_HEAP_OFFSET` as a size), and with a page-multiple pool size those bytes are an unmapped page
- [CMASK fast clears](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Draw.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - each fast-cleared CMASK tile (code 0) is stored as `CB_COLOR_CLEAR_WORD` into its 8x8 texels at the first draw into the target or at its fast-clear eliminate or DCC decompress pass, not as the color block writes the tile, and the CMASK is then stored as expanded (0xF): a texture or copy read of the target after that draw and before the title's eliminate sees the clear value in tiles no draw wrote, where the console keeps their previous bytes. Tile addresses follow the pipe-aligned SW_64KB_Z_X CMASK equation for 16 pipes; other CMASK swizzle modes, codes other than 0 and 0xF, a cleared tile of a DCC target and a fast clear of a mipmapped, 3D or array color target throw. When the clear runs as a compute pass (a resident target whose CMASK is host-imported), an invalid code throws at the next fast-clear materialization, not at the one that read it
- [HTILE depth fast clears](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/DepthSurface.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - HTILE is not modeled. Only RADV's `clear_htile_mask` compute kernel (17 GFX10 words, 64x1x1 groups) is recognized, and only when it leaves ZMASK at 0 (`user[4]` and `user[5]` low nibbles both 0); the dispatch still runs, and the host depth image is cleared to `DB_DEPTH_CLEAR` the next time the surface with that `DB_HTILE_DATA_BASE` address is bound. The address match is exact, so a clear of a different HTILE range does not hit. HTILE initialization, the stencil-only update, and fast clears made by the SDK's own paths, CP DMA fills or other kernels are not covered and leave the depth image as it was. The kernel words are the ones the PR author saw RADV submit; they are not compared with Mesa's source.
- [Host imports](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestBufferMemory.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) on Linux skip ranges that are not mapped read-write, which are copied instead. The NVIDIA driver (595.91.07) refuses `VK_EXT_external_memory_host` imports of read-only or executable mappings (and shared mappings of regular files) with `VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_DEVICE_MEMORY`, after which every `vkAllocateMemory` on the device fails the same way. Read-only ranges are never imported, even on drivers that accept them. Shared mappings of regular files are not checked for. On Windows, a run of adjacent registered ranges whose shared views are not one contiguous run of views of a section gets no read-write alias, so its ranges are imported one at a time, as before runs were imported whole, and a buffer that crosses two of them is copied. Adjacent registered ranges are joined into one import only where they fill a whole 8 MiB aligned window; ranges of a partly filled window are imported one at a time, and a buffer that crosses two imports is copied. The 8 MiB window is a choice: unbounded runs were re-imported whole on every growth of a heap mapped in small pieces, and the NVIDIA driver (610.57.04, 615.78.08) then refused imports or lost the device. Not measured on RADV.
- [dma-buf imports](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestBufferMemory.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - the udmabuf of a guest range lists the runs of the direct memory backing file behind it (`SharedBackingRuns`, one per physical allocation the range was mapped from, up to udmabuf's 1024), where it took the first run's offset for the whole range. The start-up probe also stores to the import from the GPU after CPU stores to the same pages and counts pages the CPU still reads stale: i915 maps a dma-buf without snooping the CPU caches (4 of 4 pages stale on Meteor Lake with Mesa 26.2.3 and main), so such a device does not fall back to dma-buf imports and copies instead. The memory type the import reports is host-coherent for the driver's own mappings; the guest's mapping of the same pages is what goes stale.
- [Cube texture descriptors](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestTextureResource.cpp) with equal `BASE_ARRAY` and `DEPTH` expose six faces starting at `BASE_ARRAY`. Measured with `image_load`, `image_sample` and `image_get_resinfo` on RX 6600 (gfx1032), using 64x64 R32_UINT faces and base slices 0, 5 and 6. Other ranges containing fewer than six faces or incomplete cube arrays still throw: hardware load bounds and filtered-sample addressing differ. Out-of-range cube-array sampling remains unimplemented. Samplers with `DISABLE_CUBE_WRAP` are created with `VK_SAMPLER_CREATE_NON_SEAMLESS_CUBE_MAP_BIT_EXT`, the flag RADV maps to that bit (Mesa `src/amd/vulkan/radv_sampler.c`, `src/amd/common/ac_descriptors.c`); without `VK_EXT_non_seamless_cube_map` a cube texture sampled through such a sampler throws. PS5 equivalence has not been measured on console hardware.
- [Multisampled CMASK](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a multisampled color target's CMASK is decoded under CB_COLOR_INFO FAST_CLEAR (bit 13, as for a single-sample target) or COMPRESSION (bit 14). Which bit a multisampled target sets is inferred: its fast clears were seen on GTA V's multisampled targets through their CMASK fills, not in a register dump. A COMPRESSION-only target's zero CMASK is not read as cleared (inferred: zero means cleared only under FAST_CLEAR), so only a fill after its first use clears it. A slice of a multisampled array is its own image at its own address with its own CB_COLOR0_CMASK (inferred from the register layout). Its CMASK is main's whole-metablock size.
- [Multisampled CMASK state](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/MultisampleTarget.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a multisampled target has no guest texels, so its CMASK state comes from fills rather than from a read at each draw as a single-sample target's does. A fill of the whole CMASK with 0 clears the host image at the target's next use, one with 0xffffffff marks it expanded, and any other pattern, or a fill that covers only part of the CMASK, throws at the next use (inferred: main's rule that a mixed CMASK is unmodeled, applied to the one host image). A FAST_CLEAR target starts from its CMASK keys at its first use, and a COMPRESSION-only target starts expanded. Copies by DMA into the CMASK are not seen, and a later use with another CMASK address throws. The clear does not rewrite the CMASK bytes as expanded, so a new target over the same CMASK (a different extent, so a different image) seeds from them again and clears once more.
- [Multisampled targets in CB passes](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a CB eliminate or DCC pass over a multisampled target throws, since its texels are on the host and only a CB resolve is modeled. A CB resolve into a fast-clear destination throws: the resolve writes the destination's texels but not its CMASK, which would still read as cleared.
- [Multisampled target guest access](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/MultisampleTarget.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - the memory a multisampled color target throws on (CPU read, DMA copy, texture or storage image read) is taken as `max(CB bytes * samples, width * height * element bytes * samples)` from its address, an upper bound on the sample planes of a target laid out in 64 KiB blocks (inferred: the single-sample layout size times the sample count; the exact fragment-aware footprint of addrlib is not computed, so a few bytes past the real footprint can throw too). Targets are kept per device for the device's lifetime, one per surface address, format, extent and sample count, and are not evicted.
- [Custom MSAA sample locations](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) support a uniform 1x1 pattern for 2, 4 or 8 samples through VK_EXT_sample_locations. Patterns varying across the guest's 2x2 pixel grid throw. Devices without the required sample count, four subpixel bits or the full guest coordinate range refuse custom patterns. Depth images remain in GENERAL between draws, and render passes cannot continue across pattern changes. Guest offsets retain the existing decoder's signed four-bit, sixteenth-pixel interpretation; PS5 coverage and interpolation equivalence have not been measured on hardware.
- [DCC display buffers](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp) are presented only when all keys of the console's DCC extent agree on uncompressed or a fast-clear code, padding keys included; other keys, and register-clear keys over a pending image, throw.
- [Storage image write-back](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Texture.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) stores the GPU results over a CPU-stamped range whose guest bytes equal the copy taken when the image's layers got their generation (images up to 1 MiB whose guest bytes were settled at that point, with no unit shadow, pending image results or recorded GPU store over them; other images keep the previous rule: the CPU's bytes win). A CPU store that rewrites the baseline value after the GPU changed it is not told apart from no store, and the GPU result wins there. The 1 MiB limit is a choice, not a console value.
- [16-bit float display buffers](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/DisplayBuffer.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver, libSceVideoOut) - pixel formats `0xC001000622000000` and `0xC001000600000000` are taken as R16G16B16A16 and B16G16R16A16 float, eight bytes per texel, as [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/59183dbfa81ed7efdff6069177fed50de0552725/src/graphics/host_gpu/renderer/image/imageInfo.h) maps them and [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/5dfdb4b0b166e83dc9ae275001afb695f12c7174/src/SharpProspero/Interop/VideoOut/VideoOutTypes.cs) names them (`Rgba16Float`, `Bgra16Float`); PPSA11386 registers 3840x2160 tiled buffers of the first. The tiled layout is assumed to be the 8-byte SW_64KB_R_X equation of `TextureSwizzleEquations.hpp` applied to full coordinates, as addrlib applies it, by analogy with the 32-bit scanout; `ColorTargetLayout` applies the equations to in-block coordinates, which for 8-byte texels differs in every odd 64-row block row (address bit 11 takes y bit 6) and for 16-byte texels also differs, so a float display buffer written through `WriteColorTarget` and presented from guest memory would show those rows misplaced. The values are assumed to be linear light and are encoded with the sRGB curve for the 8-bit output. Bits 32-39 of these formats are `0x06`, as in the 10-bit formats SharpProspero lists without sRGB transfer, while its sRGB formats have `0x00`, its 10-bit PQ formats `0x04` (with `0x07` in bits 40-47) and its float BT.2100 PQ formats `0x07`: they show only that these formats are not the sRGB ones. NaN and negative values give 0, values of 1 and above give 255, alpha is linear. Neither the layout nor the transfer is verified against console or title frames, and HDR output is not modelled. DCC metadata on a float buffer throws.
- [DCC key stores](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/DccMetadata.cpp) cover the console's DCC extent only for pipe-aligned DCC of single-level, single-layer 4-byte SW_64KB_R_X surfaces; other surfaces, and every texture and color target key read, cover one byte per 256 bytes.
- [DCC key fills over other images](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Texture.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a uniform fill at a live image's `dccAddress`, at least one key per 256 bytes long, is a key fill of that image even when other live images overlap it, but only while it is no longer than the image's key extent (`DccKeyCount`, the pipe-aligned count); a longer one is classified by its overlaps as before. The bound is inferred, not measured on a title: a longer fill also writes the bytes past the keys, which the overlapping image may own. Whether the title's bloom-target key fills stay within the extent was not checked (`APS5_TRACE_DCC_KEYS` prints the length of the fills classified as keys, not of the refused ones).
