- [Comp-to-single DCC keys](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Draw.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - key 0x10 (`GFX9_DCC_CLEAR_SINGLE` in Mesa `ac_descriptors.h`) is expanded only by a CB DCC decompress pass over uniform keys of a non-linear target: every texel takes the element at the start of its 256-byte block, the top-left texel of the block where radv's `build_clear_dcc_comp_to_single_shader` stores the clear color. A fast-clear eliminate over such keys and mixed keys throw; textures and color targets that read them use their stored texels. "Uniform" is judged over the key bytes the driver reads, one per 256 surface bytes (see DCC key stores above), so a pipe-aligned surface whose keys differ past that range is expanded as if all were comp-to-single, as for the other clear codes. Not verified on hardware
- [8_SRGB color targets](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Draw.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) on a device that cannot attach and blend R8_SRGB render into an RGBA8_SRGB copy of the target, so the sRGB encode, decode and blend rounding are the device's; the console's are not measured.
- [8_SRGB color targets](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Texture.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) on such a device throw unless they are single-mip 2D surfaces with a resident image; linear targets have none.
- [1_5_5_5 color targets](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) render into A1R5G5B5. AMD formats keep the first component in the least significant bits (X in bits 0-4, Y in 5-9, Z in 10-14, W in bit 15, as in the [fpPS4](https://github.com/red-prig/fpPS4) `kSurfaceFormat1_5_5_5` doc comment and as measured for textures in the packed 16-bit textures entry), while A1R5G5B5 keeps R in bits 10-14 and B in bits 0-4, so the pixel exports are written with X and Z exchanged, which makes the stored bits follow the guest layout. The decoded format, component mapping and write mask are tested, and an execution test draws 32_ABGR exports into tiled and linear targets and compares every stored texel with the guest layout on the host device; the export direction is not measured on a PS5. Only UNORM with the standard component swap is accepted. Constant blend colors go through the same component-swap handling as other targets with that export mapping (`0xc6`), which is not tested for this format. PPSA06699 renders into this format.
- [COPY_DATA from the GPU clock](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Pm4.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver, `SRC_SEL` 9) writes the host steady clock in 10 ns ticks when the packet runs, the same counter as the `RELEASE_MEM` timestamp, not the console's GPU clock. The `COPY_DATA` cache policy fields are accepted and have no effect.
- [16-bit RGBA color targets](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) support the standard and the alternate (`COMP_SWAP` 1) component order. Alternate swaps red and blue in shader exports, write masks and constant-color blend factors, following `ac_translate_colorswap` in [Mesa 26.2.2 ac_formats.c](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/mesa-26.2.2/src/amd/common/ac_formats.c). The reverse orders (`COMP_SWAP` 2 and 3) keep throwing when blending, and simultaneous targets requiring conflicting blend constants throw. Console equivalence has not been measured on hardware.
- [UINT16_ABGR color exports](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - export format 7 is accepted only into an unsigned integer target, and each 16-bit value reaches the target unchanged; a value beyond a narrower target's range (`R8_UINT`) is undefined in Vulkan, and whether the console clamps or truncates it is not confirmed. SINT16_ABGR (8) throws
- `--to-intel` guest module trampolines are covered only by a synthetic relinker test; no game title has been verified with them on Linux or Windows.
- `--to-rosetta` lowers what Rosetta lacks on top of `--to-intel` ([matcher](../../core/relinker/codegen/src/x86/Amd64OnlyInstructionMatcher.cpp)). Rosetta runs AVX2, FMA, BMI1/2, F16C and MOVBE but reports none of them through CPUID, so code that checks CPUID takes its baseline path. RDSEED becomes RDRAND and CLWB a NOP. RDPID returns processor 0, because Rosetta's TSC_AUX is the constant 0xAAAAAAAB; the same value reaches RDTSCP's ECX, which is not converted. ADCX/ADOX fault under Rosetta and are not converted: compilers do not emit them, and code that checks CPUID sees no ADX. The conversions are covered by the converter tests; that the converted code runs under Rosetta was checked on an M5 Max only, outside this repository's CI.
- `--to-intel` replaces the approximate legacy SSE and VEX.128 register forms of RSQRTPS/RCPPS and VRSQRTPS/VRCPPS with [correctly rounded](../../core/relinker/codegen/src/x86/ReciprocalLowering.cpp) 1/sqrt(x) and 1/x, not with the AMD approximation: its tables are not public. Intel's approximation of 1/sqrt(1.0) is 0x3F7FF000, and two Newton-Raphson steps on it settle on 0.99999994 instead of 1.0; a renormalised identity quaternion then turns into a NaN axis in PPSA21564, which the console does not show. Refined results now reach the exact value; unrefined ones can still differ from the console in the low bits. Each site costs an out-of-line stub (two jumps, a spill below the red zone, SQRTPS/DIVPS). Legacy SSE forms preserve the destination's upper YMM bits; VEX forms clear them. The 256-bit, scalar and memory forms keep the native instruction, as does a site shorter than 5 bytes with no movable instruction after it for the jump (5 sites in PPSA21564, counted before a following RIP-relative instruction could move into the stub; not recounted).
- [sceKeyboardGetKey2Char](../../core/libs/prx/libSceKeyboard/src/keyboard_impl.cpp) (libSceKeyboard) translates the 101-key (US) arrangement and the alphanumeric layer of the 106-key (Japanese) one, which follows the JIS layout of the HID usages: the Yen (0x89) and Ro (0x87) keys return `\` (0x5C) unshifted, as JIS X 0201 places the yen sign there; whether the console returns U+00A5 instead is unverified. With the Kana LED (bit 4, the HID position) set the 106-key arrangement throws, as the kana the console returns are unknown. Ctrl and Alt do not change the character.
- [sceAudioOut2Set3DLatency](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2System.cpp) and [sceAudioOut2MasteringInit, sceAudioOut2MasteringTerm and sceAudioOut2MasteringSetParam](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2Mastering.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) have no effect: there is no 3D rendering or mastering stage.
- [sceImeKeyboardGetInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libSceIme/Export.cpp) (libSceIme) returns `0x80bc0023` (no resource id) for every id while a keyboard is open, since the IME keyboard never reports a connected device. [sceImeKeyboardSetMode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceIme/Export.cpp) validates the mode bits but the mode has no effect, as no IME keyboard events are delivered.
- [AudioOut2 contexts](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2Context.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) with an SDL device read a grain's port buffers at the next push, at least 100 us after the grain was pushed, and play the mix behind a 40 ms cushion that the queue level leaves out. When the hardware reads the buffers is not known. The timing follows a title whose mixer fills a buffer after pushing it, reuses it two pushes later, and may still be writing it when the next push follows within microseconds. The last grain pushed before a title stops pushing is never read or played.
- [sceAudioOutSysGetHdmiMonitorInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) reports an HDMI sink without a monitor name or audio descriptors: it zeroes the 0x180 bytes and returns 0, since audio reaches the host through SDL as PCM and there is no EDID to describe. The arguments `(type, info, size)`, the required size 0x180 and the fields titles read (monitor name at +0x0B, 13 bytes; descriptor count as a 32-bit value at +0x94; 8-byte descriptors `[coding type, channels, rate mask, 0, extra, 0, 0, 0]` from +0x98) come from [PS5-Audio-Passthrough-Research](https://github.com/sainsaji/PS5-Audio-Passthrough-Research/blob/c3be9c2afcb8f7838784ead0fe1c12a2ceea965a/docs/FINDINGS.md) (firmware 12.70, one monitor). What a console without an HDMI audio sink returns, an error or no descriptors, is unknown. The other bytes, among them the header at +0x90 (`01 09 4f 00` on that monitor), have no known meaning and are written as 0. No LPCM descriptor is reported for the host device, as the rate mask and extra byte of one are not known. A null buffer returns `0x80260004` like the other AudioOut calls; the console's code is not measured. Monitor type 0x10, which the console also accepts, other types and sizes other than 0x180 throw: their results and error codes are unknown.
- [libSceAudiodec](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudiodec/Export.cpp) throws for the 24-bit PCM word size (`iBwPcm` 0): its sample layout is unknown. ATRAC9 decoding is covered only by configuration and error tests, as no ATRAC9 encoder is available for a fixture.
- [sceAjmDecMp3ParseFrame](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp) (libSceAjm.native) throws when the original file length is requested (`parse_ofl` non-zero) for a layer other than III, for a CRC-protected frame, or for a VBRI header outside MPEG-1 stereo: shadPS4 looks for the tag after the CRC and after the side information, while LAME and FFmpeg place it without the CRC and VBRI always at byte 36.
- [ATRAC9 gapless segments](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp) (libSceAjm.native) - a run job stops after the gapless total of output samples counted from the start of the segment, leaving the rest of its input unconsumed, and the next run starts the next segment with the decoder as it is and without a second skip. FMOD in PPSA29141 sets a total shorter than a sound's frames and reads the frames after it on later runs; with the output held at the total its stream reader waited for them forever and the last buffer repeated. The console's handling of the frames after the total is not documented; a reset (`SetGaplessDecode` with the reset flag, `ClearContext`, initialization) starts a new segment. An ATRAC9 run job without `RUN_MULTIPLE_FRAMES` decodes its whole input as one with it: FMOD in PPSA29141 puts two run jobs in one batch with the second's input right after the whole of the first's. Whether the console stops after one frame there is not verified; MP3 and Opus run jobs still stop after one frame
- [Tessellation rings](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/TessellationLowering.cpp) of local, hull and domain programs become Vulkan tessellation interfaces and are never written to guest memory; a title that reads the off-chip or factor ring back sees no data.
- [Inner tessellation factors](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) of fractional-odd triangle patches that are NaN tessellate as 1, as in the D3D11 reference tessellator; the console is not measured.
- [VGT_TF_PARAM](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - DISTRIBUTION_MODE is taken as scheduling only; the host tessellator decides the order of a patch's triangles.
- [Hull programs](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/InstructionTranslator.cpp) start from the hull launch registers (user data, ring bases, v0, v1); on the console they start with what the local program leaves, and that it leaves them unchanged is not checked.
- [Local programs](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Shaders/ShaderRegistry.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) that the hull header leaves at address 0 are prepared at their first draw, one artifact per tessellation configuration, instead of at registration.
- The [output modifier](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/TranslationContext/OperandAccess.cpp) (`mul:2`, `mul:4`, `div:2`) follows the shader's float mode as measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035): the hardware applies it only with IEEE mode off and output denormals of the result's precision flushed (bit 1 of the `FLOAT_MODE` denormal field clear), and ignores it otherwise. On f32 results it is applied as the hardware does with f32 denormals flushed; a shader without a known float mode is translated with IEEE mode off and f32 denormals flushed, the mode titles use. On f16 and f64 results it is ignored, and it throws in the modes where the hardware applies it.
- The [clamp modifier](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/TranslationContext/OperandAccess.cpp) follows `DX10_CLAMP`, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) for f32, f16, packed f16 and f64 results at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1: with `DX10_CLAMP`=1 (and in shaders without a known float mode) a NaN result clamps to +0, including the quieted NaN that `v_pk_min_f16` and `v_pk_max_f16` return for a signaling NaN operand with IEEE=1 (measured on RDNA2 gfx1036 at both `IEEE_MODE` and both `DX10_CLAMP` settings); with `DX10_CLAMP`=0 a NaN result is written unchanged and every other value clamps to [0, 1] as before. f64 results follow the same rule on hardware, but a clamped f64 result with `DX10_CLAMP`=0 throws until it has an execution test. With `DX10_CLAMP`=0 the NaN's bits are those the translation already produces for the instruction without clamp, so where those are left to the driver (f32 and scalar f16 arithmetic) the payload is the driver's, not the hardware's input propagation. `v_dot2_f32_f16` and the integer clamps are unchanged.
- [FP16_OVFL](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/TranslationContext/OperandAccess.cpp) follows the shader's float mode as measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1 with f16 denormals kept and flushed: with `FP16_OVFL`=1 an f16 result that rounds to ±infinity is written as ±0x7bff unless a source operand is infinite, so `v_rcp_f16`, `v_rsq_f16` and `v_log_f16` of ±0 and `v_exp_f16` of 16 or more are clamped while an infinite input passes through, and `v_div_fixup_f16` keeps the infinity it writes for a zero denominator or an infinite numerator and clamps any other infinite quotient, a NaN one included. `v_cvt_pkrtz_f16_f32` rounds toward zero and never overflows. The sources are checked as the f32 values the translation computes with; the rule is applied to `v_fma_mix*_f16` with f32 sources and to `v_interp_p2_f16` without a measurement of infinite f32 sources or parameters. Round modes other than nearest even are not modelled, so the overflow threshold is the nearest-even one (65520).
- [Directed f16 rounding](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/TranslationContext/SubwordPacking.cpp) for `v_cvt_f16_f32`, `v_cvt_f16_i16`, `v_cvt_f16_u16` and `v_ldexp_f16` follows `FLOAT_MODE` bits 3:2 only when f16 denormals are preserved (bits 7:6 = 3), measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036). With f16 denormals flushed (bits 7:6 = 0), or flushed on one side (modes 1 and 2, not measured), the hardware also flushes the f16 result and the directed path does not model that, so those shaders keep round-to-nearest-even. The `clamp` modifier with directed rounding is not measured.
- [Comparison sampling of a color texture](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) (`image_sample_c`/`image_sample_c_lz` on a texture that is not R32 float or R16 unorm) is emulated in the shader, since Vulkan compares only depth formats: the red channel is fetched, compared and, for bilinear filtering, blended (compare, then filter). Only 2D, 2D array and cube views, float/unorm/snorm formats, wrap, clamp-to-edge and clamp-to-border addressing (border red 0 for the black borders, 1 for opaque white) and point or bilinear filtering at one level are implemented, with texel offsets added to the texel indices; LOD biases and clamps are accepted, as they cannot select another level of a single-level sample. Half-border addressing, the border color table, gradients, gathers, other dimensions, anisotropy and mip filtering throw. Under bilinear filtering, taps past a clamp-to-border edge compare the border value and blend with the texel taps, so results near the edge depend on the filter weights. AMD documentation does not say how a color format's reference is handled: it is clamped to [0, 1] for unorm and [-1, 1] for snorm, as Vulkan does for unorm depth, and not clamped for float. 8-bit sRGB (`Format8Srgb`, `Format8_8Srgb`, `Format8_8_8_8Srgb`) returns 0 for every compare function, measured with `image_sample_c_lz` on gfx1035 over references -0.5..1.5 and texel codes 0..255; that measurement also returns 0 for 8-bit UNORM, which this path still compares. A bilinear cube of those 8-bit sRGB formats without `DISABLE_CUBE_WRAP` returns 0 for the same reason; float, unorm and snorm bilinear cubes without that bit still throw. Bilinear weights are full float, not the hardware's fixed-point subtexel weights
- [Negative color-texture comparison wrap indices](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) use explicit `OpSRem` normalization to implement modulo; the negative-offset point-wrap regression fails with direct `OpSMod` and passes with normalization on Mesa 22.3.6 llvmpipe (LLVM 15.0.6), but negative-index wrap has not been measured on PS5 hardware.
- [libScePlayerInvitationDialog](../../core/libs/prx/libScePlayerInvitationDialog/libScePlayerInvitationDialog.cpp) simulates dialog completion without displaying UI or sending invitations, so `scePlayerInvitationDialogGetResult` reports `USER_CANCELED`; its parameter ABI remains unverified. The 40-byte result layout (error code, result, 32 reserved bytes) is taken from [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Dialog/PlayerInvitationDialog.cs) and the error codes from the other common dialogs, not confirmed on a title.
- [Converted unorm images](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) (10_11_11_UNORM, read and written through an R32_UINT view): samples, gathers, LOD queries, 16-bit data and a DST_SEL of the missing W channel throw.
- [Packed 16-bit textures](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/TextureFormat.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - `5_6_5`, `1_5_5_5` and `4_4_4_4` are viewed as R5G6B5, A1R5G5B5 and B4G4R4A4 (the 4-bit format every Vulkan device can sample; R4G4B4A4 is optional and lavapipe lacks it) with a swizzle that reads X from the low bits, and a DST_SEL of the missing W of `5_6_5` reads X. Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035), not on the console: through `image_load` and `image_sample_lz`, X, Y, Z (and W of `1_5_5_5` and `4_4_4_4`) come from the low, middle and high fields, and W of `5_6_5` returns X (the low 5 bits), not 1.
- [Converted unorm image loads](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) divide by 2^n - 1 with the buffer format path's `OpFDiv`, which Vulkan allows to be up to 2.5 ULP off, so some drivers are not bit-exact.
- [64-bit LDS atomics](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp) (`ds_*_u64`, `ds_*_b64`, `ds_*_f64`) read and write the two dwords under a workgroup lock stored after the guest LDS, as LDS is a 32-bit array without 64-bit atomics. On RDNA2 (gfx1036) the LDS serialises a 32-bit atomic on a slot's high dword and a 64-bit atomic on that slot: 128 lanes of `ds_add_u32` and 128 lanes of `ds_add_u64` on one slot sum exactly. Only that pair was measured. Every 32-bit LDS atomic of a program that contains a 64-bit LDS atomic therefore takes the same lock, including a 32-bit `flat_atomic_*` whose address lands in the LDS aperture (a 64-bit one takes it already); the flat forms were not measured against a 64-bit atomic either (the other 32-bit LDS atomics, `ds_min/max`, `ds_cmpst`, the float forms, `ds_inc/dec`, `ds_mskor` and `ds_wrxchg`, are extrapolated from `ds_add_u32` and were not measured against a 64-bit atomic); a program without a 64-bit LDS atomic keeps its native 32-bit atomics. `ds_condxchg32_rtn_b64` is two `ds_mskor` halves, each under the lock but not atomic as a pair, so a 64-bit atomic between the two halves is not excluded. Sub-dword writes (`ds_write_b8`, `ds_write_b16` and their `_d16_hi` forms) and plain `ds_read_*`, `ds_write_*`, `ds_append_u32` and `ds_consume_u32` are not taken under the lock, so a plain access racing a 64-bit atomic on the same dwords can see or overwrite one half of it; whether the hardware orders them was not measured. The lock serialises the workgroup's LDS atomics of such a program; the throughput cost was not measured. `VK_KHR_workgroup_memory_explicit_layout` with `shaderSharedInt64Atomics` would make both widths native and is not used. A 64-bit atomic at an address that is 4-byte but not 8-byte aligned faults the wave on gfx1036, as on gfx1035.
- [LDS float atomics](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp) (`ds_min/max/cmpst_f32`, `ds_min/max/cmpst_f64` and their `_rtn` forms) follow the hardware measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) over 14 x 14 operand sets (zeros, ones, infinities, denormals, quiet and signalling NaNs of both signs and with payloads) in IEEE 0 and 1 with every value 0-3 of the type's denormal field: both operands are flushed to a signed zero when bit 0 of the field is clear (0 and 2; 1 and 3 keep them; the f32 field for the f32 forms, the f16/f64 field for the f64 forms; a shader without a known float mode flushes f32 and keeps f64), then a signalling-NaN data operand is stored quieted, else a signalling-NaN old value is stored quieted, else a quiet-NaN old value is replaced by the data, else a quiet-NaN data operand loses, else the ordered compare with -0 below +0 decides, and the flushed value is what is written, also when the old value wins. `cmpst` compares the flushed bits (both zeros equal, NaN never equal) and leaves memory unchanged when they differ. The returned value is always the old value as it was in memory. `IEEE_MODE` has no effect. The same bit rule serves the buffer float atomics (`AtomicFloatBits`). Not measured: a denormal or signalling-NaN `data1` of `cmpst` (stored as given), and `ds_add_f32`, which keeps the host's `OpFAdd`.
- [V# bases off a DWORD boundary](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp) (shader recompiler) - raw accesses use the exact bytes and `s_buffer_load` reads `(base & ~3) + (offset & ~3)`, as measured on gfx1035 (Linux, alignment mode UNALIGNED; the console's mode is unknown). Formatted accesses off their element's alignment and atomics throw.
- [32-bit bitfield masks](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/IntegerInstructions.cpp) (`S_BFM_B32`, `V_BFM_B32`) use the truncating 32-bit shift specified by the [RDNA 1.0 ISA](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf), sections 12.1 (opcode 36) and 12.12 (opcode 867). Width and offset use their low five bits. This matches gfx1035 (Linux) for every width/offset pair, with and without upper operand bits set, and both leave SCC unchanged; not measured on the console.
- [Vector buffer offsets that are not a multiple of 4](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp) (shader recompiler) - dword accesses count whole dwords from the V# base, where the hardware reads the exact bytes at base + offset.
- [Min/max S# reduction](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestSamplerResource.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - `FILTER_MODE` 1 and 2 use Vulkan's min and max reduction (the same `SQ_IMG_FILTER_MODE` values RADV programs). Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035), not on the console: only texels of non-zero weight are reduced, and the weight is taken after rounding to the 8-bit subtexel grid (a sample within 1/512 texel of a centre reduces that texel alone); a driver with other `subTexelPrecisionBits` reduces a different set there. Gathers ignore the mode.
- [Min/max S#](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestSamplerResource.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver, shader recompiler) - throws with anisotropy, a linear mip filter, depth or color-texture compare, or bilinear filtering of an image the recompiler point-samples (sint, converted, depth-bits).
- [FORCE_DEGAMMA](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Sampler.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - an S# that forces sRGB decoding adds nothing to an sRGB texture, as in shadPS4 (PS4). Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035), not on the console: `8`, `8_8` and `8_8_8_8` sRGB textures sample bit for bit the same with and without the bit (point and bilinear; BC formats not measured). Other textures throw; on the same hardware their UNORM `8`, `8_8` and `8_8_8_8` forms sample bit for bit as the sRGB format (decoded before filtering).
- [Tessellation factor ring](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/State/src/Configuration.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - `sceAgcDriverSetTFRing` records the ring and `sceAgcDriverGetTFRing` reports it. Before a title sets one, it reports the console's default, 0xFF0000000 and 128 KiB, as measured in [PS5_Vulkan's hardware findings](https://github.com/mihawk-99/PS5_Vulkan/blob/fde9e3798c2ec1d8fccc90d5067cd6060490607f/docs/HARDWARE_FINDINGS.md). Nothing is written to the recorded ring: tessellated draws run as Vulkan tessellation stages, which keep their factors on the host, so a title that reads tessellation factors from the ring finds none. A null base or a zero size throws; [ps5-opengl's AGC runtime](https://github.com/blackbearreloaded/ps5-opengl/blob/67c873f985eaefe0a72f713529e5632eaa01139c/src/platform/ps5_agc_native_runtime.c) calls `sceAgcDriverSetTFRing(0, 0)` in its cleanup, and what that does on the console is unknown.
- [Raw `buffer_load_dwordx2`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp) is one 64-bit access on the hardware (with or without glc), so it never sees half of a `buffer_atomic_swap_x2`. Only a glc load that is 8-byte aligned and in bounds, in a program that has the 64-bit storage view (it uses 64-bit buffer atomics), is read with one 64-bit atomic load; every other dwordx2 load is two dword loads and can tear against a concurrent 64-bit write: loads without glc, and loads in a program without 64-bit buffer atomics while another shader writes the pair with `swap_x2`.
- [Buffer loads without GLC or DLC](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAnalysis.cpp) (shader recompiler) are not coherent in a program that has GLC or DLC buffer accesses and no `buffer_gl0_inv`, `buffer_gl1_inv`, `s_gl1_inv` or `s_dcache_inv`: only its GLC and DLC loads, stores and atomics use the `Coherent` buffer view, so a plain load can return a value cached before another work-group's write. This follows the [LLVM AMDGPU memory model for GFX10](https://llvm.org/docs/AMDGPUUsage.html#memory-model-gfx10-gfx11), in which a load-acquire is a GLC DLC load followed by `buffer_gl1_inv` and `buffer_gl0_inv`; it is not measured on the console or on RDNA hardware.
- [DS src2 operations](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/MemoryInstructions/SharedMemoryInstructions.cpp) (`ds_*_src2_*`, GFX10.1 only) read `DS[B]`, with `B = A + 4 * sext(offset1[6:0]:offset0)`, and then update `DS[A]` with the matching 32-bit atomic, so the read of `B` is not atomic with the update of `A`. The form that takes the offset from the address (`offset1[7]` set), the 64-bit forms and GDS throw: whether the LDS address keeps `A[31:17]` in that form, and whether the 64-bit forms scale the offset by 4 or 8, is not documented. `ds_min_src2_f32`, `ds_max_src2_f32` and `ds_add_src2_f32` throw: on RDNA2 (gfx1035, the oracle's hardware, which has no src2 opcodes) `ds_min_f32`, `ds_max_f32` and `ds_add_f32` ignore `IEEE_MODE` but depend on whether f32 denormals are flushed (28, 28 and 12 of 196 special rows differ), and that is not modelled for LDS float atomics yet (#981).
- [wcsrtombs_s](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Multibyte.cpp) (libc) follows the console's `libc.prx`, but a runtime-constraint violation only returns its error: the default handler there writes the message and a newline to `stderr` before returning, which is not done here. Conversion uses the C locale of `wcrtomb` (`MB_CUR_MAX` 1), so the path where a multibyte character does not fit the remaining space is not reached
- [The narrow printf family](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Formatting.cpp) (libc) converts 16-bit guest wide arguments to UTF-8 on both platforms, independently of the host locale. It does not use the guest's single-byte `wcrtomb`; the console's treatment of invalid UTF-16 and locale-specific encoding remains unverified. Linux retains host formatting for formats without wide arguments, so a narrow `%p` there still follows the host (glibc prints a null pointer as `(nil)`, where FreeBSD prints `0x0`); `%m` and argument positions above 99 (FreeBSD's `NL_ARGMAX`) throw when a format requires the guest formatter
- [collate<char>::_Getcat](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/LocaleSupport.cpp) (libc) builds the facet for the C locale only and throws for any other locale name. The console's `libc.prx` stores pointers to its collation state tables at offsets 0x10 and 0x18 of the facet; ours leaves them null, as only the facet's own functions read them. `do_transform` throws for input with embedded null characters, which the console drops depending on the output buffer size, and for results of 4095 characters or more, whose string storage uses the guest's over-aligned large allocation.
- [The scanf family](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Formatting.cpp) (libc) hands `%i` to glibc on Linux, which since 2.38 reads the C23 `0b`/`0B` binary prefix when the library is built as GNU C++: `sscanf("0b101", "%i", &value)` stores 5 where C17 stores 0 and stops at the `b`. The strtol family stops at the prefix as C17 does
- [The scanf family](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Formatting.cpp) (libc), the `_s` forms included, on Linux throws for the 16-bit wide conversions (`%ls`, `%lc`, `%l[`, `%S`, `%C`): glibc would store a 32-bit `wchar_t` per character into the guest's 16-bit buffer. Windows hands `%ls`, `%lc` and `%l[` to the host, whose `wchar_t` is 16 bits
- [The scanf family](../../core/libs/prx/libc/include/WindowsScanning.hpp) (libc) on Windows returns 0, as C17 and FreeBSD do, when input fails after only suppressed conversions have completed (`sscanf("12", "%*d%d", &value)`). Linux hands the format to glibc, which returns `EOF` there. On Windows the host stops a `%x`, `%X`, `%i` or `%p` after the `x` of a `0x` that no hex digit follows (or that the width cuts off), and a float conversion (`%e`, `%f`, `%g`, `%a`) after the `e` of an exponent with no digits (`1e`), where C17 and FreeBSD stop before the `x` or the `e`
- [socketpair](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Udp.cpp) (libkernel) - the pair is the host's `AF_UNIX` pair on Linux and, on Windows, a loopback TCP connection for `SOCK_STREAM` or two connected UDP sockets for `SOCK_DGRAM`; `SOCK_SEQPACKET` throws on Windows. A datagram sent to a full receiver gets `ENOBUFS` on the console, waits or gets `EAGAIN` on Linux and is dropped on Windows, and on Windows a datagram `recv` after the peer closed reports `ECONNRESET` instead of the console's `ENOTCONN`, and a stream `send` after the peer closed reports `ECONNABORTED` instead of the console's `EPIPE`. `read` and `write` on a pair end go to the host like on every guest socket. `getpeername` on a pair end succeeds after the peer closed, where the console returns `ENOTCONN`, and `connect`, `bind` and `sendto` with an address on a pair end report an address-family error instead of the console's `EISCONN`. On Windows `poll` on a stream end reports `POLLHUP` without `POLLIN` after the peer's `shutdown(SHUT_WR)` or `close`, where the console reports `POLLIN`, with `POLLHUP` only after `close`
- [UDP recv/recvfrom](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Udp.cpp) and [sceNetRecv/sceNetRecvfrom](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNet/Export.cpp) translate Windows WSAEMSGSIZE into the copied byte count for datagram sockets, including MSG_PEEK, and retain the sender address. This follows FreeBSD 11.4 recv(2) and Winsock's documented truncated-datagram behavior; console behavior and existing title impact are unverified.
- [send, sendto](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Udp.cpp) (libkernel) - send and sendto without an address on an unconnected datagram socket map Windows `WSAENOTCONN` to FreeBSD's `EDESTADDRREQ` (39).
- [read, write and vector I/O](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Stdio.cpp) (libkernel) on a guest socket use `recv` and `send` without flags; `sceKernelReadv` and `sceKernelWritev` gather or scatter through one socket operation, preserving stream short reads and datagram boundaries. The positioned `pread`, `pwrite`, `preadv` and `pwritev` forms, and their `sceKernel` forms, return `ESPIPE` for an open guest socket and `EBADF` for a closed one. Guest socket descriptors start at `0x10000000` and are allocated upward, so the descriptor range selects the guest socket table or the host descriptor table. `EPIPE` from a write raises the host's `SIGPIPE` on Linux, which the guest can neither ignore nor suppress: `signal(SIGPIPE, SIG_IGN)` and `sigaction` record the disposition, as they do for every number from 1 to 128, but only the six signals with host equivalents (2, 4, 6, 8, 11 and 15) take effect on the host or are delivered, so the host `SIGPIPE` is not stopped; `SO_NOSIGPIPE` is not an option here, and raises nothing on Windows; a `write` longer than `INT_MAX` bytes is `EMSGSIZE` where the console sends it. AIO on a guest socket still goes to the host, which has no such descriptor
- [poll](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Udp.cpp) (libkernel) waits only on guest sockets, through `WSAPoll` on Windows and `poll` on Linux; the `pollfd` layout and flag values are FreeBSD's. A descriptor below the socket range (files, pipes, stdio) throws, as do event bits other than `POLLIN`, `POLLPRI`, `POLLOUT`, `POLLRDNORM`, `POLLRDBAND` and `POLLWRBAND` (the `POLLERR`, `POLLHUP` and `POLLNVAL` bits are ignored in `events`), `POLLPRI` on Windows, which `WSAPoll` rejects, and an infinite timeout with no socket to wait on. A closed socket descriptor reports `POLLNVAL`; the console's limit on the descriptor count is unknown and not checked
- [Socket error numbers](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/include/SocketError.hpp) (libkernel) - a host socket error becomes the FreeBSD 11 `errno.h` number of the same name; the numbers are not checked on the console. Winsock `WSAESHUTDOWN` becomes `EPIPE`, as a send after `shutdown` does on FreeBSD, and a host error with no FreeBSD socket counterpart becomes `EIO`. A nonblocking `connect` in progress reports `EINPROGRESS` on Linux but `EAGAIN` on Windows (`WSAEWOULDBLOCK`), where FreeBSD reports `EINPROGRESS`. On Windows a refused nonblocking `connect` is never reported (`SO_ERROR` is unsupported), so a repeated one keeps reporting `EALREADY` where FreeBSD reports `ECONNREFUSED`. A receive after `shutdown(SHUT_RD)` reports `EPIPE` on Windows where FreeBSD returns 0
- [msync](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/PosixMemory.cpp) (libkernel) follows FreeBSD 11's `kern_msync` and `vm_map_sync`, not measured on the console: the address is rounded down to the page, a range that wraps is `EINVAL`, as is `MS_ASYNC` with `MS_INVALIDATE`, and a range whose start or any page is not mapped (as `sceKernelVirtualQuery` reports it, reservations included) is `ENOMEM`; a page unmapped from inside a larger mapping still counts as mapped there, since `sceKernelVirtualQuery` keeps reporting the whole mapping. The guest has no file mappings, so a successful call writes nothing back. `MS_INVALIDATE` on a mapped range throws: FreeBSD returns `EBUSY` when the range holds `mlock`ed pages, and locked pages are not tracked
- [Thread exit destructors](../../core/libs/prx/libSceLibcInternal/Export.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal) run when a libkernel thread finishes; on the main or a host thread only `_sceLibcInternalThreadDtors` runs them, as `exit` does, else they are dropped
- [sceFontOpenFontSet](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/SystemFont.cpp) (libSceFont) opens only the primary file of a system font set. Characters the console takes from the set's other files (Hangul, Thai and Arabic in Japanese and Chinese sets, Arabic in European sets) are reported as unsupported glyphs. The console file names are those of the PS4 firmware; the names of the PS5-only JG2 sets (`0x1A......`) are unknown, so they use the SSTJpPro files. When a console file is missing, an openly licensed substitute (Noto Sans, Noto Sans Mono, Noto Sans Thai, Noto Sans CJK) is loaded instead. It is not the system font: its metrics differ, so text width, line height and wrapping differ from the console. System font faces are loaded by FreeType in host memory, not in the font memory the title passes to the library: FreeType needs about 1 MiB per CJK face, which the title's font memory is not sized for.
- [sceFontGetCharGlyphMetrics, sceFontGetRenderCharGlyphMetrics](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/Layout.cpp) (libSceFont) report the vertical metrics the way the [FreeType driver](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFontFt/src/Driver.cpp) reports them for a rendered glyph, not confirmed on a title: FreeType's vertical advance, and for a face without vertical metrics a bearing of half the horizontal advance left of the horizontal bearing and of half the advance left over by the glyph height.
- [Font writing lines](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/WritingLine.cpp) (libSceFont) - `sceFontWritingLineWritesOrder`, `sceFontWritingLineRefersRenderStep`, `sceFontWritingLineGetRenderMetrics`, `sceFontWritingLineGetOrderingSpace` and `sceFontWritingLineClear` follow the PS4 11.00 `libSceFont.sprx` (firmware module, read from its disassembly and compared call for call on random sequences): runs are linked in visual order by embedding level, the space at each spaced join is summed for the line, steps walk the visual order from the left (form `0x12`) or from the right (form `0x11`) and the render metrics grow as steps are consumed. The PS5 library was not measured. Not from that module: form `0x10` is accepted and treated as left to right and a detail with id `0x0FD5` is accepted (the PS4 library rejects both); the memory argument of `sceFontCreateWritingLine` and the rest of the detail are not read (the PS4 library validates its library object there); an unknown writing form, another detail id, a null line pointer on creation, unknown and null handles (except in `sceFontWritingLineRefersRenderStep`, which returns null for a null line) and a null output of `sceFontWritingLineGetRenderMetrics` throw instead of returning the PS4 error codes, which are not confirmed for the PS5; `sceFontDestroyWritingLine` was not compared. Lines are allocated on the host, not in the font memory the title passes to the library: FreeType needs about 1 MiB per CJK face, which the title's font memory is not sized for.
- [s_swappc_b64](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/ControlFlowInstructions.cpp) writes the link pair with the same model as `s_getpc_b64`: the guest return address `pc + 4` as a byte offset over `GetShaderBase`. Any other use of the link pair (arithmetic, compare, hash, store) throws, apart from the overwrites and shared links that [static scalar calls](../../core/shader/recompiler/ControlFlow/src/GraphBuilder.cpp) allow; constant-offset folding over the link is not modeled. The special encoding `s_swappc_b64` with SDST 125 still decodes as `s_setpc_b64`, which matches the hardware: measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) in wave32 and wave64, it branches to the source pair and leaves a marker SGPR pair untouched, and with an SGPR destination the link is `PC + 4`.
- The [s_swappc_b64 fetch-shader call at vertex/local/tessellation/mesh entry](../../core/shader/recompiler/ControlFlow/src/GraphBuilder.cpp) assumes the called fetch-shader blob ends in `s_setpc_b64` to the link pair (the GNM fetch-shader shape shadPS4's `EmitFetch` copies); the blob is not part of the shader program, so its return closes the pairing by assumption and any in-program reader of the link pair throws. Attribute fetches are inlined from the request's parsed fetch-shader plan through the same `GetAttribute` path as the embedded fetch analyzer, honoring the buffer resource `dst_sel`; an attribute writing more than four registers, or an entry call without a parsed plan, throws. The vertex/instance offset accumulator inside the fetch blob is not replayed for external blobs.
- The scalar aperture sources [src_shared_base, src_shared_limit, src_private_base, src_private_limit](../../core/shader/recompiler/RdnaDecoder/src/RdnaInstructionDecoder.cpp) (core/shader/recompiler) decode to their own operand kinds (scalar source codes 235 to 238, named as in LLVM's `SIRegisterInfo.td`), but their value is not modelled: reading one refuses with the source it refused named, because the wave's shared and private aperture bases and limits are unknown. Both the scalar-code read and the raw-operand read refuse; a shader that only carries the value into an address still prepares, one that reads it does not
- [VertexBufferReadSize](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/include/VertexInput.hpp) and the [vertex staging copy](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Draw.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a vertex fetch whose index is at or past the descriptor's `num_records` no longer throws for `OOB_SELECT` 0 and 1. The copy still spans the whole fetch, but the bytes from `num_records * stride` onward read as zeros, so the out-of-range vertex is `(0, 0, 0, 0)`. Measured with `buffer_load_format_xyzw` (`idxen`) through the [hardware oracle](HW_ORACLE.md) on RDNA2 gfx1036 for PPSA01325's word 3 `0x0004dfac` (`OOB_SELECT` 0) in #2241: indices 3, 4 and 5 over 3 records each return `00000000 00000000 00000000 00000000`, W included, unlike Vulkan `robustBufferAccess`, which returns `(0, 0, 0, 1)`. The zeros come from the record check, not from memory: stored values placed past the records are ignored, and a `num_records` 6 control returns them. `RESOURCE_LEVEL` 1, `OOB_SELECT` 1 and FORMAT 74 `32_32_32_FLOAT` give the same zeros, in wave32 and wave64; gfx1013 (the console) is not measured. `OOB_SELECT` 2 returns the stored values past the records and `OOB_SELECT` 3 compares `num_records` against the byte offset, both measured on the same setup in #2241; the two keep throwing past `num_records` here, since each needs its own rule. Zero-stride descriptors (`stride` 0) keep their separate byte-count check and are not covered here. Each out-of-range fetch is copied alone and outside the draw-snapshot cache, which is neither read nor written for it: a reused snapshot would carry another draw's bytes into the zero region, and a stored zero-padded copy would hand zeros to a later in-range draw at the same address. The valid prefix is still flushed for GPU writes before it is read.
- [EmitSetAttribute](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) (shader recompiler) - a primitive with a vertex whose clip position is (0,0,0,0) is clipped away through an extra `gl_ClipDistance` element on vertex and tessellation evaluation shaders, matching what a Radeon 610M (RDNA2, RADV) and llvmpipe rasterise; NVIDIA 615.78.08 without it draws the vertex at the viewport centre. Measured for triangles (strip and list) and for a point at (0,0,0,0), which the Radeon 610M does not draw, NVIDIA draws at the viewport centre and llvmpipe at the top-left corner; lines with such a vertex are clipped by the same element and were not measured. Mesh, geometry and the generated rect-list and barycentric stages do not write it, a device without `shaderClipDistance` cannot compile vertex shaders (the compile throws), and guest clip distances are still refused by the pipeline validator. The PS5 rasteriser was not measured.
- [sceImeKeyboardGetResourceId](../../core/libs/prx/libSceIme/Export.cpp) (libSceIme) reports that no USB keyboard is connected (`0x80bc0004`, as [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4) does) because IME keyboard events are not delivered to the handler, although [libSceKeyboard](../../core/libs/prx/libSceKeyboard/src/keyboard_impl.cpp) exposes the host keyboard
- [Player review dialog](../../core/libs/prx/libSceCdlgPlayerReview/Export.cpp) (libSceCdlgPlayerReview) shows nothing: `scePlayerReviewDialogOpen` finishes at once and the result reports that the user canceled, as `sceWebBrowserDialogOpen` does
- [NP reachability callbacks](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNpManager/Export.cpp) retain one callback and its user data; duplicate registration and unregistering an absent callback return the errors used by [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/945dbc3cc3eee80ac3e053b438502ed936fa6bb2/src/core/libraries/np/np_manager.cpp). No callback is delivered because the existing NP model remains signed out with reachability unavailable.
- [Running application ID](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSystemService/Export.cpp) (libSceSystemService) - `sceSystemServiceGetAppIdOfRunningBigApp` reports 1 as the running title's app ID. The value is AnyPS5's own: the console's shell assigns app IDs and their values are not known here, so other calls that take an app ID must use the same one. `sceSystemServiceKillApp` with that ID and the arguments -1, 0 and 0, which [SwanStationPS5](https://github.com/darkxex/SwanStationPS5/blob/4cdc981d1a64ffb7f2af73f7a27a9e91a3cb5eb4/src/main.c#L1050-L1058) passes to close itself and [ps5-payload-dev's hbldr](https://github.com/ps5-payload-dev/websrv/blob/46374edee6627885abac717de24da7a1d73af6f1/src/ps5/hbldr.c#L425-L435) passes to close the running game, ends the title as its own `exit(0)` would: the guest's shutdown and `atexit` handlers run and the process exits with status 0. On the console the call returns and the shell then ends the title, and whether those handlers run there is unknown; here there is no shell, so the call does not return. Another app ID or other arguments throw.
- [sceNpCheckNpAvailability](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNpManager/Export.cpp) (libSceNpManager) - the `(req_id, online_id)` signature and the errors follow [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/0fe263a4760dfbfa973366890061749b4af0de97/src/core/libraries/np/np_manager.cpp) with no user signed in to PSN: a null online id is `SCE_NP_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, and any other online id matches no user, `SCE_NP_ERROR_USER_NOT_FOUND`; the request id is not checked, as in the other NpManager requests
- [Temporary app-content storage](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAppContent/Export.cpp) tracks one `/temp0` mount, preserves its files on unmount, and rejects new path resolutions until remount. Mount, format, space-query and unmount errors follow [fpPS4](https://github.com/red-prig/fpPS4/blob/04cefd43e6fddd1ab033e7980cd356d14c964905/sys/sys_path.pas); the pointer ABI is also declared by [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/AppContent/AppContent.cs). Existing open host descriptors remain usable after unmount; console behaviour with open files is unverified. Host filesystem failures still throw.
- [Guest path resolution](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/General.cpp) falls back to ASCII case-insensitive component lookup, following [shadPS4's host filesystem](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/945dbc3cc3eee80ac3e053b438502ed936fa6bb2/src/core/file_sys/backends/host_fs.cpp). PPSA03747 requests `Skyrim.INI`, `Data/Video` and lowercase archive/plugin names that differ from the dump's filenames. Exact host names take precedence; multiple fallback matches throw instead of selecting an arbitrary file. Existing parent names are resolved when creating a new file, and guest mount aliases use the same ASCII comparison. Results are not cached, so renames and newly created files remain visible. Unicode case folding and PS5 behaviour for case-colliding host entries are unverified; host roots are used as supplied. Explicit native Windows drive and UNC paths bypass guest parent-component validation.
- [localtime, mktime](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Time.cpp) (libc) on Windows convert through the CRT's `localtime_s` and `mktime`, which accept about 1970 to January 3001 UTC and apply the CRT's daylight-saving rule; outside that range `localtime` returns null and `mktime` returns -1, where the console converts. `gmtime` converts every time whose year fits an `int` on both hosts.
- [strftime](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Time.cpp) (libc) expands FreeBSD's `%k`, `%l`, `%s`, `%v` and `%+` itself, ignoring a leading `E` or `O` as FreeBSD does. Unknown conversions, repeated modifiers and incomplete conversions emit literals as in FreeBSD 11; recognized conversions otherwise go to the host's `strftime`. On Windows the `%-`, `%_` and `%0` padding flags still produce nothing, including on the five expanded conversions (`%-k`, `%_k` and `%0k` go to the host), and a standalone `%z` or `%Z` uses the host's zone instead of the broken-down time's `tm_zone`. `%s` follows FreeBSD 9 and 11 and is the local-time `mktime` of the broken-down time (current FreeBSD uses `tm_gmtoff`), which is -1 on Windows outside the range above. `%+` is the C locale's `%a %b %e %H:%M:%S %Z %Y` with `%Z` taken from `tm_zone`, or from `tzname[tm_isdst != 0]` when it is null.
- [LINE_STIPPLE_TEX](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/InstructionTranslator.cpp) (recompiler) reads as 0: line stipple is not emulated. RADV enables the input only as the required pixel input of a shader that reads no interpolants, because it initializes the fewest VGPRs ([Mesa](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/main/src/amd/vulkan/radv_shader.c) `radv_compute_spi_ps_input`), and never reads it
- [Draw snapshot buffer descriptors](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderResources.cpp) rewrite each affected array from element zero, preserving unchanged descriptors. MoltenVK 1.4.2 writes auxiliary buffer lengths at index `i` instead of `start + i` for partial array updates ([MVKDescriptorSet.mm, BufferAuxSize](https://github.com/KhronosGroup/MoltenVK/blob/v1.4.2/MoltenVK/MoltenVK/GPUObjects/MVKDescriptorSet.mm#L1051)). Per-element writes therefore let one buffer length replace another even when Vulkan buffer handles and ranges are correct.
- [Static scalar calls](../../core/shader/recompiler/ControlFlow/src/GraphBuilder.cpp) (`s_call_b64` and `s_swappc_b64`) require an ordinary even-aligned SGPR link pair (s0:s1 through s104:s105) and a later `s_setpc_b64` return reading that exact pair. A pair serves one call, or several calls whose regions are disjoint, each closed by the first return reading the pair after its target. Outside every region of its calls the pair may only be overwritten whole, by `s_mov_b32`, `s_mov_b64`, an SOP2 result or a scalar load, as the caller of a user-data callee does before each call. Regions must nest or be disjoint; recursion, other shared links, escaping link values (including overlapping tuples and resource descriptors), invalid targets, interleaved regions, cross-region branches or fall-through, relative SGPR reads and dynamic `s_setpc_b64` in a program with calls throw. The analysis checks all decoded instructions, including unreachable code. Arbitrary indirect calls and general call frames are not implemented.
- [Relative vector addressing](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/ControlFlowInstructions.cpp) (`v_movrels_b32`, `v_movreld_b32`, `v_movrelsd_b32`, `v_movrelsd_2_b32`, `v_swaprel_b32`) keeps every vector register from the lowest base register the program addresses relatively up to the last register the program names in a zero-initialised function-scope array per invocation (`LoadVectorWindow`/`StoreVectorWindow`, one element per register), so an indexed access is one load or store instead of a compare-and-select chain over the window; the host compiler decides whether that array stays in registers or spills to scratch, which is not measured. A register in the window the program never wrote reads 0, as an unwritten SSA register does; the console's value is undefined. M0 is unsigned: an index at or past the window reads `v0` and drops its write, and `v_swaprel_b32` writes neither side when either is out of range, as measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035, `agc_driver_relative_indexing`). The window ends at the last register the program names rather than at the shader's VGPR allocation, which the recompiler does not receive, so a relative write past the named registers but inside the allocation is dropped where the hardware keeps it for a later relative read. Killzone: Liberation's PSP vertex program (PPSA11142, 3176 ISA words, 104 relative accesses over 154 registers) went from 649,515 to 392,333 SPIR-V words with this.
- [User-data callees](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Shaders/ShaderRegistry.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a compute shader's `s_swappc_b64` whose source pair is a user-data pair, or a copy of one made with `s_mov_b32` or `s_mov_b64` in the same block, calls the code at the address that user data holds. At dispatch the callee is read from guest memory up to its `s_setpc_b64` on the link pair, one copy of it is appended per call after the caller's last instruction, and the call is rewritten to `s_call_b64` to that copy. The words after the caller's last instruction are dropped; in the shaders seen they are metadata, and a caller that reads its program counter, which could address them, throws. A copy runs at another program counter than the callee, so a callee that reads its program counter, makes a call, ends the program, jumps through another pair, returns before code it branches to, spans more than 64 KiB or is not readable throws. Callees are read again whenever a dispatch misses the dispatch cache; a dispatch that hits it reuses the linked program, so a title that rewrote a callee in place at the same address would keep running the old code.
- [DB_RENDER_CONTROL resummarize draws](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - HTILE is not modeled, so a RESUMMARIZE_ENABLE draw without depth or stencil tests and without color writes changes no modeled state and draws nothing. A pure resummarize with pass-through D16/D32 depth, no depth writes or bounds test, no stencil plane, no color writes and no running pixel stage also draws nothing; other resummarize, copy or decompress draws throw.
- [s_call_b64](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/ScalarInstructions.cpp) saves `PC + 4` over `GetShaderBase` and branches to `PC + 4 + signext(SIMM16) * 4`, following [AMD RDNA2 ISA](https://gpuopen.com/download/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) sections 3.6.3, 12.2 and 12.3. Forward callees after `s_endpgm` are decoded. Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) in wave32 and wave64 with SIMM16 = 3: the link pair receives `PC + 4`, the branch goes to `PC + 4 + signext(SIMM16) * 4`, and `s_setpc_b64` of the link returns to the instruction after the call. Execution on the PS5 (gfx1013) and title compatibility for these bounded scalar calls remain unverified; `s_rfe_b64` still throws in translation.
- [sceVideodec2CreateDecoder](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVideodec2/Export.cpp) (libSceVideodec2) decodes HEVC (codec type 974921) and VP9 (2382845) through FFmpeg, besides H.264. The tags come from SharpProspero's `Videodec2CodecType` and EVO-PLAYER-PS5's `sce_videodec2.h`, which records them from hardware runs. HEVC input is converted from length-prefixed NAL units to Annex B as for H.264, and VP9 frames are passed through. Output is the same 8-bit NV12 for every codec; a decoder for HEVC Main10 or VP9 Profile 2 is created, but a frame deeper than 8 bits throws, since the console's 10-bit output layout is unknown, and `QueryDecoderMemoryInfo` reports the 8-bit frame size for it. `sceVideodec2GetPictureInfo` and `sceVideodec2GetAvcPictureInfo` throw for HEVC and VP9 pictures, which use other layouts (`SceVideodec2HevcPictureInfo`, `SceVideodec2Vp9PictureInfo`), and `sceVideodec2GetHevcPictureInfo` and `sceVideodec2GetVp9PictureInfo` are not exported
- [syslog](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Syslog.cpp) (libc) writes each message to `stderr` as `[syslog:<priority>] <message>`, with a newline added when missing, instead of sending it to the console's system log. Priority and facility handling follow FreeBSD's `vsyslog`: `%m` expands to the guest errno's message, unknown bits are reported (as priority 0x23, since FreeBSD passes `LOG_ERR|LOG_CONS|LOG_PERROR|LOG_PID` as the priority) and masked, a missing facility becomes `LOG_USER`, and errno is preserved. `setlogmask` follows [FreeBSD 11](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8d2708de5d6112a492744d367cfdfb8fd219227a/lib/libc/gen/syslog.c): the process-wide mask defaults to 0xff, zero queries it, and nonzero values replace it; masked messages are skipped before formatting, including the unknown-priority diagnostic. The console's mask behaviour is unverified. `openlog` and `closelog` are not implemented, so their identity and options are not applied
- [daemon](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Process.cpp) (libc) throws: forking into the background is not supported. PPSA85300 imports it and fails to load without it. VLC 3.0 calls it only when its `daemon` option is set ([libvlc.c](https://github.com/videolan/vlc/blob/6de05adcbaf2e8b85fe86aad4169393098628119/src/libvlc.c#L190-L197)), and the title starts libVLC with `--ignore-config` and without `--daemon` ([player.cc](https://github.com/ZiZc3/VLC/blob/f3253020a64368153233ede29183014c0b6c8979/src/player.cc#L960-L981))
- [sigtimedwait](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Signals.cpp) (libkernel) throws: guest signals are delivered when raised and never left pending, so there is nothing to wait for or take. PPSA85300 imports it and fails to load without it. Its only call site is VLC's `vlc_writev` ([filesystem.c](https://github.com/videolan/vlc/blob/6de05adcbaf2e8b85fe86aad4169393098628119/src/posix/filesystem.c#L243-L278)), which polls with a zero timeout to drain a `SIGPIPE` only after `writev` fails with `EPIPE`
- [pause, sigwait, sigwaitinfo](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Signals.cpp) (libkernel) throw, as `sigtimedwait` does: guest signals are never left pending, so `sigwait` and `sigwaitinfo` have nothing to take, and `pause` would wait for a handler that `raise`, `kill` and `pthread_kill` only run on the calling thread. PPSA68091, PPSA99169, PPSA99420 and PPSA97358 import them and fail to load without them. PPSA68091 calls `pause` at one site (0x8F7266), in a loop that waits while a global holds another thread's `errno` address. PPSA99169 only stores the address of `pause` in a table of symbol addresses (0x30DB1). PPSA99420 calls `pause` at 0x3BD80F6 and 0x3C9E220, `sigwait` at 0x3C9E4C7 and `sigwaitinfo` at 0x3C9E588. PPSA97358 calls `sigwait` from QEMU's `sigwait_compat` thread ([compatfd.c](https://github.com/ZiZc3/XPSemu/blob/8eec07b4e31f66dce8e0b05c1ca1281af303e933/util/compatfd.c#L28-L53)), which `qemu_signal_init` ([main-loop.c](https://github.com/ZiZc3/XPSemu/blob/8eec07b4e31f66dce8e0b05c1ca1281af303e933/util/main-loop.c#L110-L147)) starts for `SIGIO`, `SIGALRM` and `SIGBUS` when the main loop is created, so it reaches the throw at start-up, and from the vCPU thread of the dummy accelerator ([dummy-cpus.c](https://github.com/ZiZc3/XPSemu/blob/8eec07b4e31f66dce8e0b05c1ca1281af303e933/accel/dummy-cpus.c#L48-L56))
- [getsid](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) throws: process sessions are not modelled. PPSA74126 imports it and fails to load without it; the title made no call to it in a 60 s run
- [sigaction](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Signals.cpp) (libkernel) records the handler, flags and mask of every signal from 1 to 128 and reports them back, following FreeBSD's `sigaction` (`EINVAL` for other signals, and for a handler on `SIGKILL` or `SIGSTOP`); `signal` records its handler with `SA_RESTART`, as FreeBSD's libc does. Only handlers without `SA_SIGINFO` on the signals `signal` already delivers (`SIGINT`, `SIGILL`, `SIGABRT`, `SIGFPE`, `SIGSEGV`, `SIGTERM`) run, the same way `signal` handlers do; their mask and flags are not applied. `SA_SIGINFO` handlers are recorded but never called, since that needs FreeBSD's `siginfo_t` and `ucontext_t`, and neither are the handlers of other signals (`SIGBUS`, `SIGHUP`, ...), whether they are set by `sigaction` or by `signal`, which records them too (every number from 1 to 128, with `SA_RESTART` and an empty mask replacing what `sigaction` stored; `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP` accept only the default handler in both calls, as in FreeBSD's `kern_sigaction`). `raise` of an `SA_SIGINFO` handler on a signal `signal` delivers throws instead of terminating, and `raise` and `kill` of the other signals still fail with `EINVAL`, recorded handler or not
- [kill](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Signals.cpp) (libkernel) follows FreeBSD's `kern_kill` and `killpg1` for the only guest process: `EINVAL` for signals outside 0 to 128, signal 0 only checks that the target exists, and its own pid, 0 and minus its own pid (the guest is taken to lead its own process group) signal it through `raise`, so they share `raise`'s limits above. Any other pid, and -1, which skips the caller, report `ESRCH`, so `EPERM` is never reported
- [sceKernelFcntl](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Open.cpp) (libkernel) runs the POSIX `fcntl` below and returns its guest errno as `SCE_KERNEL_ERROR_*`, the form the other `sceKernel` file exports use. The return values and error codes are unverified on a console
- [fcntl](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/DescriptorControl.cpp) (libkernel) follows FreeBSD 11.4's [`kern_fcntl`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/kern/kern_descrip.c#L479-L799) and [`kern_dup`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/kern/kern_descrip.c#L812) on file, directory, pipe and character device descriptors; a socket keeps the libSceNet path (`F_GETFL`, `F_SETFL` with `O_NONBLOCK`, `EINVAL` for every other command, `F_GETFD` and `F_SETFD` included). The close-on-exec flag and the status flags live in a guest table, the status flags shared by `dup`, `dup2` and `F_DUPFD` as in an open file description; a descriptor the table has not seen, such as 0 to 2, starts from the host's flags, since the console's are not known. `F_GETFL` takes the access mode from the host (the granted access of the handle on Windows), so pipe ends report `O_RDONLY` and `O_WRONLY` where FreeBSD's bidirectional pipes report `O_RDWR`. `F_DUPFD` and `F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC` return the lowest free host descriptor at or above the start, so the bound is the host table (`EMFILE` on Windows, `EINVAL` past `RLIMIT_NOFILE` on Linux), not the console's unknown `RLIMIT_NOFILE` and `kern.maxfilesperproc`. `O_NONBLOCK` switches a host pipe (`PIPE_NOWAIT` on Windows, where an empty read and a full write then report `EAGAIN`) and is only recorded on files, directories and the random device, as their reads never block; on a Windows console or other character device it throws. Changing `O_APPEND` on a Windows file, changing `O_FSYNC` on a file, `O_ASYNC` on a pipe or character device (`SIGIO` is not delivered), `F_GETOWN`, `F_SETOWN` and `F_SETLK_REMOTE` throw; `O_DIRECT`, `F_RDAHEAD` and `F_READAHEAD` are recorded as the hints they are. Record locks (`F_GETLK`, `F_SETLK`, `F_SETLKW` and their forms without `l_sysid`) use the host's POSIX locks on Linux and throw on Windows, and `F_GETLK` with `F_UNLCK` throws. The return values and error codes are unverified on a console
- [ioctl, _ioctl](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Udp.cpp) (libkernel) handle only `FIONBIO` and `FIONREAD` on a socket, and any other request there fails with `ENOTTY`. On the other descriptors they follow FreeBSD's [`kern_ioctl`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/kern/sys_generic.c#L758), [`vn_ioctl`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/kern/vfs_vnops.c#L1488) and [`pipe_ioctl`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/kern/sys_pipe.c#L1346): `FIONBIO` sets `O_NONBLOCK` as `fcntl` does, and `FIONREAD` reports the size past the offset of a file, the queued bytes of a pipe's read end, 0 on its write end and `ENOTTY` on the random device, while it throws on a directory or another character device. Every termios request (group `t`, `TIOCGWINSZ` included) fails with `ENOTTY`, since those descriptors are taken as not a terminal, as in `tcgetattr`, and any other request throws. Stremio Plus (PPSA74126) imports `_ioctl` for its statically linked `tc*` functions, which send `TIOCGETA`, `TIOCSETA`, `TIOCSPGRP` and the other termios requests
- [sendfile](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Udp.cpp) (libkernel) follows FreeBSD 11's `sendfile` for a connected stream socket: headers, the file from `offset` (to its end when `nbytes` is 0) and trailers, the file offset left unchanged, `EAGAIN` with `*sbytes` set after a partial send on a non-blocking socket. It reads the file through `sceKernelPread` in 64 KiB blocks and sends each with `send`. A socket as the file fails with `EINVAL`, as on FreeBSD, but any other descriptor `sceKernelPread` reads, such as `/dev/urandom`, is sent where FreeBSD requires a regular file or shared memory, and a file descriptor as the socket fails with `EBADF`, as for `send`, where FreeBSD answers `ENOTSOCK`. `flags` are ignored: reads complete before sending, so `SF_NODISKIO` never fails with `EBUSY`, and `SF_SYNC` returns once the data is queued on the socket rather than sent. PS5MC (PPSA99420) imports it
- [pthread_kill](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Signals.cpp) (libkernel) returns the error number like FreeBSD's `pthread_kill`: `EINVAL` for signals outside 0 to 128 and `ESRCH` for a null thread. Signal 0 reports any other thread handle as existing, without checking that the thread is still alive. A signal to the calling thread goes through `raise`, so it shares `raise`'s limits; a signal to another thread throws, since guest signals are not delivered to a chosen thread
- [select](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Socket.cpp) (libkernel) validates `tv_usec` but saturates a valid timeout beyond the remaining `steady_clock` range at `time_point::max()`, so the wait can continue effectively indefinitely. The console's behavior for a valid timeout outside the host clock's range is unknown.
- [AJM](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp), [APR](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Apr/src/Apr.cpp) and [NGS2](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/System.cpp) state is allocated with `new` and never freed on purpose: title threads still call into these libraries while `std::exit` runs static destructors, so ordinary statics would be destroyed under them. Keep them leaked; `guest_ajm_teardown` covers the AJM case on Unix.
- PPSA30601 imports declared without parameters, signatures unknown, added without an implementation because the title fails to load without them: [six sce::Json::Object::Pair, sce::Json::Object and sce::Json::Array::iterator members](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJson2/Export.cpp) (libSceJson2), [sceAmprAmmGetUsageStatsData](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAmpr/Export.cpp) (libSceAmpr), [sceMouseResetAbsolutePosition](../../core/libs/prx/libSceMouse/Export.cpp) (libSceMouse) and [sceSaveDataDirNameSearch2](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSaveData.native/Export.cpp) (libSceSaveData.native)
- PPSA07411 imports declared without parameters, signatures unknown, added without an implementation because the title fails to load without them: [sceSslCreateConnection, sceSslDeleteConnection, sceSslEnableVerifyOption, sceSslConnect, sceSslRead, sceSslWrite](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSsl/Export.cpp) (libSceSsl), [four sce::Json::Value members (setElementAccessFailureHandler, setSpecialFloatHandler, setTypeMismatchHandler, operator bool)](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJson2/Export.cpp) (libSceJson2), [sceConvertKeycodeGetCharacter](../../core/libs/prx/libSceConvertKeycode/Export.cpp) (libSceConvertKeycode) and [sceNpSessionSignalingGetGroupFromSessionId](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNpSessionSignaling/Export.cpp) (libSceNpSessionSignaling)
- [Comparison-sampled R32 float and R16 unorm textures](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderResources.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) whose surface a storage image holds are sampled from a D32_SFLOAT or D16_UNORM image that the GPU refills from the storage image whenever its version moves, because Vulkan compares only depth formats. Only surfaces of at least 1 MiB (`APS5_DEPTH_COPY_MIN_KIB`) that are not 3D take the copy; smaller ones stay CPU snapshots compared against guest memory. With copies of small surfaces, one title hit an intermittent boot-time crash that was not explained.
- [Null vertex buffer V#](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/include/VertexInput.hpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a V# whose four dwords are all zero is bound to a zeroed vertex buffer of the attribute's size with stride 0, so every component of its attribute reads 0. This follows the V# fields in the [AMD RDNA2 ISA](https://gpuopen.com/download/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) (`NUM_RECORDS` 0, `DST_SEL` 0 selects 0), not measured on hardware.
- PPSA07411 PS VR2 imports declared without parameters, signatures unknown, added without an implementation because the title fails to load without them: [sceHmd2GetDeviceInformationByHandle, sceHmd2GetFieldOfView and sceHmd2Terminate](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHmd2/Export.cpp) (libSceHmd2), [scePadVrControllerSetVibration, scePadVrControllerSetVibrationMode and scePadVrControllerReadCombinedState](../../core/libs/prx/libScePad/Export.cpp) (libScePad) and [sceVrTracker2ResetLocalCoordinate](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVrTracker2/Export.cpp) (libSceVrTracker2)
- [Descriptor pool recycling](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderResources.cpp) avoids individually freed sets: [RADV 26.2.2](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/mesa-26.2.2/src/amd/vulkan/radv_descriptor_pool.c) initializes its free-set heap with 32 bytes beyond the backing allocation. An allocation-only RX 6600 test reproduces acceptance of a 4,128-byte set in a 4,096-byte pool; the path without individual frees rejects it. Pools reset after their last set is released. A cached set can retain unused slots in an exhausted pool until eviction; peak gameplay pool memory remains unmeasured.
- [sce::Json::InitParameter2 base-object constructor](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJson2/Export.cpp) (libSceJson2) - forwards to the complete-object constructor and uses its inferred layout and default field values; constructor equivalence and initialization bytes remain unverified on the console
- [_fstatfs](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Stdio.cpp) (libkernel) fills the FreeBSD 11 `struct statfs` of the PS5 payload SDK with the host volume that holds the file: block size, transfer size, total, free and available blocks, maximum name length and the read-only, `nosuid` and `noexec` flags (on Windows only read-only, and `f_files` and `f_ffree` are 0, as NTFS has no fixed number of files). The filesystem type number and name, mount paths, owner, filesystem id and I/O counters are left 0 or empty, since they would describe a host mount, not a console one. Sockets and pipes return `EINVAL`, as FreeBSD's `getvnode` does; a Windows descriptor that is neither a file nor a pipe throws. Stremio Plus (PPSA74126) reads the free space before a download and only logs the other fields
- [fstatvfs](../../core/libs/prx/libSceLibcInternal/FilesystemStatistics.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal) converts `_fstatfs` to the 88-byte `struct statvfs` as FreeBSD 9.2's `sfs2svfs` does (`f_bsize` from the transfer size, `f_frsize` from the block size, `f_favail` equal to `f_ffree`, `f_fsid` 0, `ST_RDONLY` and `ST_NOSUID` only). Differences: `f_namemax` is the `statfs` value, not `fpathconf(_PC_NAME_MAX)`; negative `f_bavail` and `f_ffree` wrap to unsigned without `EOVERFLOW`, as the same-width copy in FreeBSD does; inode counts are 0 on Windows, inherited from `_fstatfs`; a null output returns `EFAULT` after the descriptor is validated, where FreeBSD would fault. Layout and conversion are unverified against the PS5 library (PS5MC imports `fstatvfs` but was not run)
- [sceVoiceQoSEnd](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVoiceQoS/Export.cpp) (libSceVoiceQoS) - `0x804E0901` (not initialized) before any initialization was measured on a PS5 (firmware 13.60, #1407); ending an initialized library was not, because a valid `sceVoiceQoSInit` never returns inside a homebrew payload. PPSA14632 calls it at shutdown and PPSA26344 after a nonzero `sceVoiceQoSInit`. After an initialization it returns 0 and clears the init flag, so the library can be initialized again. Whether the console also ends the libSceVoice instance that its `sceVoiceQoSInit` starts ([prosper](https://github.com/mattias800/prosper)'s reading of the module), and what it answers while endpoints exist, is unknown; no endpoint is implemented
- [_WStoul](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Strings.cpp), [_Iswctype](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/RuneLocale.cpp) and [swscanf_s](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/ScanningWide.cpp) (libc) - `_WStoul` is `wcstoul` and throws for a base other than 0 or 2 to 36. `_Iswctype` takes the class numbers of Dinkumware's [xwcc.h](https://github.com/superna9999/ti-cgt6x/blob/cgt6x_7_3_4/include/xwcc.h) (1 `alnum` to 11 `xdigit`, 12 `blank`) and throws for any other value, including the FreeBSD masks `wctype` returns; the numbering is not confirmed on the console's `libc.prx`. `swscanf_s` follows C11 7.29.2.2 and K.3.9.1.5, but reads the size argument as `unsigned int` like `sscanf_s` where K.3.9.1.5 specifies `rsize_t`; the console's width is unverified. A runtime-constraint violation (null buffer, format or receiving pointer) only returns `EOF`, and a too small array gets a null first element as in `sscanf_s`. A literal mismatch is a matching failure and a field that is only a prefix, such as `0x` or `1e`, does not convert, as C11 requires; FreeBSD's `vfwscanf` reports an input failure for the first and converts the second. Integers out of range are truncated from `strtoll`/`strtoull`. It throws for `%p`, `%S`, `%C`, unknown length modifiers, scanset ranges, `%n` with suppression or width, and narrow `%s`, `%c` or `%[` input that the C-locale `wcrtomb` cannot convert
- [sceSharePlayGetCurrentConnectionInfoA](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSharePlay/Export.cpp) (libSceSharePlay) - reports no Share Play session, as the library keeps none: status 0 (DORMANT), mode 0, empty online IDs, account IDs 0 and both user IDs -1 (`SCE_USER_SERVICE_USER_ID_INVALID`). A null pointer returns `SCE_SHARE_PLAY_ERROR_INVALID_ARGS` (0x810E0001). The 72-byte `SceSharePlayConnectionInfoA` layout and the error code follow [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/main/src/libs/libNet.cpp) and [fpPS4](https://github.com/red-prig/fpPS4/blob/trunk/src/ps4_libsceshareplay.pas), which agree on both; KytyPS5 sets the user IDs to -1 and fpPS4 leaves them 0, and which one the console returns is not verified. KytyPS5 also returns `SCE_SHARE_PLAY_ERROR_NOT_INITIALIZED` before `sceSharePlayInitialize`; this library keeps no initialization state and does not.
- [sceAgcDcbSetWorkloadsActive and sceAgcDcbSetWorkloadComplete](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/DcbState/src/Workload.cpp) (libSceAgc) and [sceAgcDriverRegisterWorkloadStream](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Resource/src/Registration.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - the driver call records only the stream id (1 to 31; it returns 0 for any argument and keeps no stream record), and the DCB calls write the packets [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/main/src/libs/agc.cpp) writes: an 18-dword (active) or 12-dword (complete) `IT_NOP` packet with the stream id, and for active the 64-bit mask of the workload ids, for complete the workload id and the 64-bit clear mask of that id. Both return null for a null command buffer, an unregistered or out-of-range stream, a workload id above 63, and for active a duplicate id or a count of 0 or above 63. The layouts and checks come from KytyPS5 alone; the console oracle in [prosper](https://github.com/mattias800/prosper/blob/main/prosper/tests/data/console_oracle/agcbuild.golden.tsv) measures the null return only for unregistered streams, not the packet contents of a registered one. The error codes the console driver returns for an invalid or repeated registration are unknown, so registration never fails here. PPSA29866 calls sceAgcDcbSetWorkloadsActive while building its command buffers (sceAgcDcbSetWorkloadComplete was not observed); nothing else was observed.
- [sceAgcCreatePrimState](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Shader/src/PrimState.cpp) (libSceAgc) - the `ge_user_vgpr_en` special is passed to the user configuration registers at whatever offset the shader header stores, as the SPRX does according to [OpenAGC](https://github.com/OpenAGC/OpenAGC/blob/3c7e8072f0d7684e930454794fd5c02056946d73/src/game_compat.c#L1181-L1252), which copies all four specials verbatim (KytyPS5 stops on any other offset). PPSA99039, PPSA99505, PPSA99515 and PPSA99997 build their headers by hand with it at 0x25c (`GE_USER_VGPR1`) instead of 0x262 (`GE_USER_VGPR_EN`), value 0, and run on the console; it is not moved to 0x262, and the driver reads neither register. `ge_cntl`, `vgt_shader_stages_en` and a used `vgt_gs_out_prim_type` must still be at their own offsets, since the driver reads those registers there, so a header that stores one elsewhere throws naming the offset, although by OpenAGC the SPRX would copy it as well
- [pthread_spin_init, pthread_spin_destroy, pthread_spin_lock, pthread_spin_trylock, pthread_spin_unlock](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/Posix/Spinlock.cpp) (libkernel) follow FreeBSD 11.4 libthr [`thr_pspinlock.c`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/lib/libthr/thread/thr_pspinlock.c) and the owner checks of its [`thr_umtx.h`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/lib/libthr/thread/thr_umtx.h#L80-L175), unverified on the console: `EINVAL` for a null or destroyed lock and a `pshared` other than 0 or 1, `EBUSY` from `trylock` while any thread holds it, `EPERM` from `unlock` by a thread that does not hold it. `PTHREAD_PROCESS_SHARED` allocates a private lock, as guest code runs in one process. libthr spins forever when the holder locks again; here that call throws
- [pthread_cond_reltimedwait_np](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/Posix/Cond.cpp) (libkernel) takes `(cond, mutex, usec)`, a relative timeout in microseconds, and is `scePthreadCondTimedwait` with POSIX error numbers. FreeBSD 11.4 has no such function; the signature comes only from shadPS4, which registers one function for this NID and for `scePthreadCondTimedwait` ([`condvar.cpp`](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/3a9f57265d28975658f1ca941daa5c879c7a74bd/src/core/libraries/kernel/threads/condvar.cpp#L229-L234)). shadPS4 reads a 64-bit count; here it is the 32-bit `SceKernelUseconds` of the SCE twin, as for `sem_reltimedwait_np`. Unverified on the console
- [sysctl kern.arandom](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/SystemConfiguration.cpp) (libkernel) serves FreeBSD 11's MIB {1, 37} (CTL_KERN, KERN_ARANDOM) and the "kern.arandom" sysctlbyname lookup through std::random_device, returning min(*oldLength, 256) random bytes. Following FreeBSD's sysctl_kern_arnd, a null buffer reports min(*oldLength, 256) without writing, a null length returns 0 without writing, and requests with a new value return EPERM (1) as the node is read-only. Cryptographic quality depends on the host's random device implementation.
